# MDARIX 0.11.07: complete code handover

Prepared 19 Nov 2026 for an AI coding assistant (Google Gemini) and the human owner who will work on this
codebase for the Google Startup Buildathon (Hyderabad, 20-21 Nov 2026) and after.

Release: **0.11.07** (`VERSION` file), branch `feat/app-wide-layout` merged onto the trial line `release/0.10`.
Base production release before it: 0.11.06 (tag `v0.11.06`). Database head: Alembic revision `ux2navigationnew`
(0.11.07 adds no migration).

How this document was made: the endpoint, table and migration lists (Appendices A, B, C) were generated by
code from the running application (`backend.app.main:app` routes and `app.openapi()`, SQLAlchemy metadata, the
Alembic folder), so they are complete by construction. Sections 2-7 were written by reading the code and are
reconciled against those appendices; each ends with a coverage checklist. Where a writer could not verify a
claim it says "not verified". If this document and the code disagree, **the code wins**: fix the document.

## 0. Rules for whoever changes this code (read first)

These come from `AGENTS.md` (the project's single source of conventions; `CLAUDE.md` imports it) and from
decisions the owner made. Breaking any of them is a defect even if tests pass.

### 0.1 Where code goes
- API routes: routers under `backend/app/`, mounted in `backend/app/main.py`. Access, administration and
  platform endpoints live in `backend/app/access/<domain>.py`, aggregated by `access_router.py`;
  authorization helpers in `backend/app/access/authz.py`. New routes go in the module for their domain.
- Domain logic: the matching top-level package (`retrieval/`, `evidence/`, `investigator/`, `failure_chain/`,
  ...). No new top-level packages or root modules without a reason in the commit message.
- Database: SQLAlchemy models in `backend/app/db/models/` **plus** an Alembic migration in
  `infrastructure/database/migrations/versions/` for every schema change. Keep a single head.
- Frontend screens: `frontend/src/features/<area>/`. `main.tsx` only boots; the authenticated shell and view
  routing are in `app/App.tsx`; shared types in `app/types.ts`. New API calls use `app/apiClient.ts`
  (`requestJson`), never raw `fetch`.
- Scripts in `scripts/`. Documentation index `docs/README.md`. `docs/history/` is an archive: never edit it to
  describe the present.

### 0.2 Naming
Never name new modules, routers, classes, settings or feature flags after a day, stage, part or package number
(`day14_`, `stage3_`...). Name them after what they do. Existing persisted identifiers (orchestration versions,
audit action codes, old script names such as `provision_day35_fixture.py`) stay because stored data or
runbooks use them.

### 0.3 Security and tenancy (non-negotiable)
- The tenant and the acting user come **only** from the authenticated request context
  (`get_request_context` -> `ctx.tenant_id`, `ctx.user_id`). Never take a tenant id, actor, reviewer or signer
  from the request body or query string.
- Tenant business data uses `get_data_plane_db`; identity, tenancy, plans, configuration and platform audit use
  the control plane (`get_db`).
- PostgreSQL uses **forced row-level security**. The API's runtime role sees only the current tenant's rows.
  Any system job, script or **data migration** that must see all tenants has to open the system scope
  (`SELECT set_config('app.rls_bypass', <system token>, true)` via `backend/app/db/tenant_scope.py`).
  Lesson from 0.11.06: migration `ux2navigationnew` ran an `UPDATE tenants ...` without the system scope and
  moved 0 rows; it had to be repeated by hand. Every data migration needs a test that asserts the row count.
- Authorization is enforced on the server. Hiding a menu item is not a control.
- Segregation of duties: the person who prepares a record must not be the one who approves or signs it.
  Signatures require a fresh authenticator code (TOTP) and are recorded in the audit trail.
- Never commit secrets or personal data. `.env*` files stay local and are never read, printed or edited by an
  assistant; only the `*.example` templates are in the repository. Tests and e2e specs read credentials from
  environment variables.

### 0.4 Tests and gates
- Every behaviour change ships with a test that fails without the change. Prefer behavioural tests to tests
  that grep source files.
- Before any commit: `python -m pytest` (uses the test database `mdarix_r1_test`, never the demo database
  `mdarix_r1`), `python -m ruff check .`, `python -m ruff format --check .`, and in `frontend/`:
  `npm run lint`, `npm run format:check`, `npm test`, `npm run build`. Before a release also the Playwright
  specs listed in section 7.
- Tests never call real AI providers or send real e-mail with developer credentials; `tests/conftest.py`
  enforces this. Run `gitleaks` before every push.
- Known: four backend tests compare the local IST date with the UTC date and fail only between 00:00 and
  05:30 IST; run with `TZ=UTC` (owner question on IST dates is open).
- Formatting: Python `ruff format` (120 columns), frontend `prettier` (120 columns). Pure formatting goes in its
  own commit and into `.git-blame-ignore-revs`.

### 0.5 Honesty rules for the product, the website and the demo
- Sample data is always labelled "Sample". No invented customer numbers, logos, testimonials or quotes.
- **AI is shown only as proposals a person reviews; AI output is never a fact.** In the demo and in the product,
  **do not name the AI model or provider.** AI is off by default per organization.
- No certification or compliance claims (no "Part 11 certified", "ISO certified", "FDA approved"). No prices in
  external material. No competitor names in any document, screen or commit.
- MDARIX never submits to an authority directly. It prepares the files; a person submits outside MDARIX and
  records that.
- Regulatory rules are starter content for the customer's regulatory consultant to confirm. A person answers
  and signs every reportability decision.

### 0.6 Working rules the owner set
- Production database writes, deploys, pushes and tags need the owner's explicit OK each time.
- Secrets, passwords and authenticator codes are typed by the owner only.
- Don't work around a refused permission or a security hook; ask the owner.
- Never `git stash`, never `docker system prune`, never force-kill processes, never force-push shared branches.
- A `v*` tag starts `.github/workflows/release.yml`; the owner's practice is to cancel that run (no GitHub
  release) and deploy from the tag on the VM.
- Update `STATUS.md` only from real command output, with the date. No "GO/NO-GO" or "complete" reports.
- Remove dead code instead of commenting it out.

---

## 1. What MDARIX is

### 1.1 Product in one paragraph
MDARIX is a multi-tenant SaaS for medical-device makers' post-market work: complaints and service calls come in,
are checked and triaged, become investigations that reconstruct the **product reality at the event date**
(version, changes, lots, suppliers: the *Product Lifecycle Twin*), weigh **evidence for, against and missing**
and **unknowns**, and end in a **signed human decision**. That decision drives **reportability per market**
(US FDA, EU, Great Britain, Canada, India MvPI and more) with prepared authority files, a **CAPA** and a signed
**effectiveness check** (Effective, Not effective, Inconclusive). Every step is audited in a tamper-evident chain.
It runs as a self-serve **free trial with sample data** and as pilots. Positioning: India-first small and
mid-size makers and exporters; "the right decision and the right report for every market", with
evidence-grounded investigation as the depth.

### 1.2 Operating modes
- **Connect**: an add-on layer over the customer's existing QMS (exports for QMS entry, imports, connectors).
- **Complete**: MDARIX is the post-market system of record. The system of record is chosen per domain.

### 1.3 The story the navigation tells (0.11.07)
Five work areas in order, shown as a connected "story rail":
**Today** (your work today) > **Cases** (the signal) > **Investigations** (why it happened) > **Products** (what
changed) > **Decisions & Reports** (decide, report, prove). Records & trust and Administration are in the avatar
menu. Each person sees the work of their persona(s) and can switch view ("Viewing as").

### 1.4 Architecture in one page
```
Browser (React/Vite SPA + public website, same bundle)
   |  HTTPS, session cookie, CSRF rules, MFA for sensitive steps
Caddy web container (static files, TLS, reverse proxy /api)
   |
FastAPI "api" container  ── backend/app/main.py (830 endpoints, 110 modules)
   |   request context -> tenant + user (never from the body)
   |   control plane DB (identity, tenants, plans, config, platform audit)  ── get_db
   |   data plane DB (tenant business data, forced RLS)                      ── get_data_plane_db
   |
"background" container: outbox worker, e-mail queue, scheduled jobs (section 7)
PostgreSQL ("postgres" container in production compose; local compose has a control plane + tenant planes)
ClamAV (upload scanning) · object storage (local folder / S3-compatible) · SMTP (outbound mail)
AI host bridge / loopback containers (AI calls only when switched on; see section 5)
backup container (database dump + manifest + off-site copy)
```
Python 3.13, FastAPI, SQLAlchemy + Alembic, PostgreSQL with `pg8000`, React + TypeScript + Vite, vitest,
Playwright, Docker Compose. Production: one Google Cloud VM running Docker Compose behind a TLS front; see 7.

### 1.5 Size of the codebase (generated 19 Nov 2026)
- 830 HTTP operations in 110 Python modules (Appendix A).
- 254 database tables (Appendix B); 126 Alembic revisions, single head `ux2navigationnew` (Appendix C).
- Backend 511 tracked files under `backend/`, about 590 under `tests/`; frontend about 780 tracked files.
- Last full test run on the 0.11.06 code (UX3, 18-19 Nov): backend 5,696 passed (4 failed only on the IST/UTC
  clock window), vitest 2,033 passed, ruff clean, Alembic round trip OK, gitleaks clean. 0.11.07 numbers are in
  section 8.

### 1.6 Glossary
| Term | Meaning in MDARIX |
|---|---|
| Tenant / organization | One customer workspace; all business data is tenant-scoped |
| Customer | The commercial account that owns one or more tenants (Platform Admin only) |
| Control plane / data plane | Identity, tenancy, config DB vs tenant business-data DB |
| Persona | A view of the work for a role (e.g. Complaint Handler, Investigator, Decision Maker) |
| Case | First contact (call, e-mail, form) that may or may not be a complaint |
| Complaint | A case confirmed as a complaint; has triage, deadlines and reportability |
| Investigation | Six-step cockpit: Signal, Product reality, Evidence, Possible causes, Brief, Decision & action |
| Product Lifecycle Twin | Versions, changes, lots, suppliers and expected vs observed rates per version |
| Evidence for / against / missing | Every statement has a kind and an author; AI suggestions are never facts |
| Unknown | An open question that must be answered or accepted as a limitation before a decision |
| Decision brief | The prepared case for a decision, signed by the Decision Maker |
| Reportability | Per-market answer (reportable, not reportable, pending information) signed by a person |
| Rule register | Market rules held as data with effective dates and sources |
| Authority report | Prepared file (US 3500A/eMDR XML, EU/GB MIR, Canada, India MvPI form) |
| CAPA effectiveness | Baseline, recurrence check and a signed result: Effective, Not effective, Inconclusive |
| Sample data | Fictional trial data (OrbitCare 300 infusion pump, PulseWatch 410 monitor, ...), always labelled |
| Trial | Self-serve 30-day free trial organization with sample data and a team |
| System scope | Database session flag that bypasses RLS for system jobs; never for user requests |

## 2. Platform: identity, tenancy, roles, administration and the free trial

Source: worktree `feat/app-wide-layout` at `4aec8b32` (0.11.07 candidate), read on 19 Nov 2026. Every statement below
comes from code read for this section; anything not confirmed in code is marked **not verified**. Paths are relative
to the repository root. The endpoint tables in 2.24 are generated from Appendix A (830 operations) and joined with the
guard each handler calls in its body (the Appendix A "Guards" column shows only FastAPI dependencies, and almost every
route here does its authentication inside the handler, so Appendix A shows only `get_db` for them).

Guard legend used throughout: **PUBLIC** no session; **SESSION** a session cookie that may still owe MFA;
**USER** a fully signed-in person (`get_request_context` or `_authenticated_user`); **ADM** customer or platform
administrator of the caller's own tenant (`_admin_tenant` / `_require_administrator`); **CA** Customer Administrator
only; **PA** platform administrator (`_require_platform_admin`, which then enters system scope); **TRIAL** the tenant is
on the Free Trial plan (`_require_trial`); **OWN** the trial owner (`team.require_owner` / `is_trial_owner`);
**+GRANT** customer-approved support access (`require_customer_approved_access`); **+PEOPLE / +ADMCHG / +AUDITEV**
the narrower support-access helpers in `backend/app/support_access.py`; **+MFA** an MFA-complete session.

### 2.1 Map of the code

| Area | Files | What lives there |
|---|---|---|
| Passwords | `auth/service.py` | scrypt hashing, `password_problem` rules, `NO_PASSWORD`, `burn_verification_time` |
| Sessions and links | `auth/durable.py`, `auth/cookies.py` | DB sessions (`auth_sessions`), invitation and reset links, cookie name and flags, lifetimes |
| TOTP | `auth/totp.py`, `security/keyring.py`, `security/mfa_recovery.py` | RFC 6238 codes, AES-GCM sealing of the secret, key rotation, recovery codes |
| MFA policy | `backend/app/mfa.py` | who must use MFA, lockout, enrolment, code check, admin reset |
| Security e-mails | `auth/security_notices.py`, `auth/emailer.py`, `backend/app/team_notices.py`, `outbox/worker.py`, `backend/app/notifications.py` | every e-mail; queued as outbox events, delivered by the worker |
| Auth API | `backend/app/auth_router.py` (15 routes, prefix `/api/v1/auth`) | sign-up, sign-in, sign-out, session, MFA, passwords, activation |
| Request context | `backend/app/request_context.py` | `get_request_context`, idle timeout, renewal, tenant/customer/membership gates, trial-ended gate |
| Database planes | `backend/app/db/session.py`, `backend/app/db/data_plane.py`, `backend/app/db/tenant_scope.py` | `get_db` (control plane), `get_data_plane_db`, RLS binding and system scope |
| Roles | `backend/app/role_codes.py`, `backend/app/access/permissions.py`, `access_control/policy.py` | role codes and labels, business permissions, `require_permission` |
| Personas | `access_control/personas.py`, `backend/app/access/session.py` | workspace personas ("Viewing as"), legacy R1 persona catalog |
| Plans | `backend/app/access/plan_features.py` (`plan_includes`), `access_control/entitlements.py` | feature entitlement checks |
| Access API | `backend/app/access_router.py` aggregating 19 modules in `backend/app/access/` | tenant and platform administration |
| Authorization helpers | `backend/app/access/authz.py`, `backend/app/access/common.py` | `_require_administrator`, `_require_platform_admin`, `admin_or_trial_owner`, `_guard_user_administration`, `_admin_target_tenant` |
| Customer/tenant lifecycle | `backend/app/customer_lifecycle.py`, `backend/app/customer_admin_continuity.py` | transition matrices, "customer gates tenants" |
| Support access | `backend/app/support_access.py`, `support_access_api.py`, `support_workspace.py` | customer-approved, time-boxed staff access; read-only support workspace |
| Data duties | `backend/app/tenant_purge.py`, `tenant_export_api.py`, `legal_hold_api.py` | erasure, export, purge, legal hold |
| Segregation of duties | `backend/app/governance_router.py` (`approval_check`, `ensure_governance_defaults`), `backend/app/workflow/router.py` (`_own_decision`) | who may approve and sign |
| Deployment role | `backend/app/deployment_role.py` | MULTI / PLATFORM / CELL; `serves_account` |
| Free trial | `backend/app/trial/` (52 files) | sign-up approval, sample story, team, personas, expiry, exit, retention, feedback, demo/MVP requests |
| Operator scripts | `scripts/create_platform_admin.py`, `reset_platform_admin_mfa.py`, `reseal_mfa_secrets.py`, `set_rls_system_token.py`, `configure_runtime_role.py`, `grant_runtime_privileges.py`, `refresh_trial_story.py`, `run_trial_retention.py`, `complete_tenant_purge.py` | break-glass and deployment steps |

### 2.2 Request context, tenancy and the two database planes

**Rule (AGENTS.md):** the tenant and the acting user come only from the authenticated request context. Never from a
body, query string or path for the caller's own tenant.

`get_request_context` (`backend/app/request_context.py:200`) is the single source:

1. Reads the session cookie (`auth/cookies.read_session_token`; only this environment's cookie name is accepted).
2. Finds the `auth_sessions` row by SHA-256 of the token, not revoked, not expired. Else 401 `UNAUTHENTICATED`.
3. Idle timeout: `last_seen_at` older than `MDARIX_SESSION_IDLE_MINUTES` (default 30; a non-integer falls back to 30
   and is logged once) revokes the row on the server and answers 401 `SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT`.
4. A session that still owes MFA (`mfa_state` VERIFY or ENROLL) gets 403 `MFA_REQUIRED` / `MFA_ENROLLMENT_REQUIRED`;
   only `/api/v1/auth/mfa/*` accept such a session.
5. Unless the request carries `X-MDARIX-Background: 1` (polls; `frontend/src/app/apiClient.ts`), updates
   `last_seen_at` and applies sliding renewal (`renew_session_if_due`).
6. `bind_tenant(db, tenant_id)` (row-level security, 2.3), then commit.
7. Loads the `auth_users` row (ACTIVE, same tenant). `serves_account` refuses accounts this deployment role does not
   serve (a customer on PLATFORM, staff on a CELL) as 401.
8. `ensure_tenant_access`: trial ended -> 403 `TRIAL_ENDED` (with `ended_on`, `data_deleted_on`); tenant missing or
   `SUSPENDED`/`TERMINATED`/`ARCHIVED` -> 403 `TENANT_ACCESS_SUSPENDED`; the tenant's customer in a blocking state ->
   403 `TENANT_ACCESS_SUSPENDED` ("customer gates tenants"); membership in a blocked state -> 403
   `MEMBERSHIP_SUSPENDED`. A user with no membership row is treated as legacy and allowed.
9. If any role the person holds now requires MFA and they are not enrolled -> 403 `MFA_ENROLLMENT_REQUIRED` (nothing
   stored, so removing the role lets the session back in).
10. `genai.governance.set_request_tenant(tenant_id, user_id)` so every model call in the request applies this
    organization's AI switch and budget.
11. Returns `AuthenticatedRequestContext(user_id, session_id, tenant_id, membership_id, ...)`. `active_role_id`,
    `persona`, `permission_set_ids`, `plan_id`, `entitlements` are always empty/None here: roles are re-read from the
    database by `permissions.effective_roles`, never trusted from the context.

**Two planes.**

- `get_db` (`backend/app/db/session.py:91`): a session on `RuntimeSessionLocal`, the least-privilege role
  `MDARIX_DB_RUNTIME_USER` when configured (else the owner connection). It starts **unscoped**: under the runtime role a
  query run before `get_request_context` / `bind_system` sees no tenant-owned rows. Identity, tenancy, plans,
  configuration and platform audit use it.
- `get_data_plane_db` (`backend/app/db/data_plane.py`): depends on `get_request_context`; looks up the tenant's ACTIVE
  `tenant_data_planes` row (503 `DATA_PLANE_UNAVAILABLE` if none). Free trials and the demo name
  `MDARIX_TRIAL_SHARED_CONTROL_PLANE`: the same control-plane session is yielded (data isolated by RLS). A dedicated
  plane is resolved only from a deployment secret named in the registry (never from the request), must be at the
  code's Alembic head (503 `DATA_PLANE_SCHEMA_OUTDATED`, cached 60 s; an outage is 503 `DATA_PLANE_UNAVAILABLE`, never
  cached) and is bound to the tenant with `bind_tenant`. Tenant business data uses this dependency.
- Trial approval registers every trial on the shared plane (`trial/service.py approve_request`: `TenantDataPlane`
  with `connection_secret_ref=TRIAL_SHARED_DATA_PLANE`, `schema_version="control-plane"`).

### 2.3 PostgreSQL row-level security and system scope

`backend/app/db/tenant_scope.py`:

- Policies (migrations `md05`, `md15`, `md34`, and `infrastructure/database/migrations/helpers.py tenant_policy`) show a
  tenant-owned row only when `app.tenant_id` equals its tenant, or when the transaction is in **system scope**.
  `tenant_policy` always issues `ENABLE` and `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, so even the table owner is filtered unless it
  is superuser or `BYPASSRLS`.
- System scope = `app.rls_bypass` holds the secret `MDARIX_RLS_SYSTEM_TOKEN`; `public.mdarix_system_scope()`
  (SECURITY DEFINER) compares its SHA-256 with the hash stored by `scripts/set_rls_system_token.py` in
  `public.mdarix_rls_secret`, a table the runtime role cannot read (md15). Local development/tests use
  `LOCAL_SYSTEM_TOKEN`; staging/production refuse to start without their own (`backend/app/config_check.py`).
- `bind_tenant(session, tenant_id)` / `bind_system(session, reason)` store the scope in `session.info` and apply it with
  `SELECT set_config('app.tenant_id', ...), set_config('app.rls_bypass', ..., true)` (transaction-local) at the start of
  every transaction (`after_begin` listener), so it survives commits. Every `bind_system` call names its reason; grep
  `bind_system(` to review them. `bind_connection_system(connection)` is for raw connections (scripts, ingestion) and
  must be called in each transaction. `system_token_accepted` checks that API and database agree.
- Who enters system scope: `_require_platform_admin` (after the role check), pre-authentication steps in
  `auth_router.py` (sign-up email check, sign-in account lookup, forgot password, link redeem/status, counting other
  organizations), `staff_accounts` look-ups, the trial invite's "account elsewhere?" check (separate `SessionLocal`),
  public testimonials, the outbox worker, the scheduler and operator scripts.
- `tests/test_rls_coverage.py` fails when an organization-owned table lacks a forced policy (six reviewed exemptions,
  per STATUS.md).

**Lesson: a data migration or script without system scope silently moves 0 rows.** Under forced RLS a statement run
outside a scope sees no tenant-owned rows, so an `UPDATE ... WHERE ...` reports success with `rowcount = 0`.
STATUS.md ("Scripts under row-level security") records that, before the fix, operator scripts running as
`mdarix_runtime` "saw 0 and 0" organizations and users. The code's patterns for data changes are:
- migrations: lift the forcing for the statement inside the migration's transaction and restore it
  (`md38_customer_approved_support._every_row`, `tr22`, `tr24a_product_references.check_rows`, which first asks
  `rolsuper OR rolbypassrls` and lifts only when needed);
- scripts/jobs: `bind_system(db, "<reason>")` or `bind_connection_system(connection)` (`scripts/refresh_trial_story.py`
  lines 89-92 and 486-489).

Observation (not verified at runtime): the newest migration `ux2_navigation_new_for_everyone.py`
(`UPDATE tenants SET navigation = 'new' WHERE navigation = 'classic'`) does neither; `tenants` is forced since md34. It
moved 6 organizations on production (docs/runbooks/RELEASE_0.11.06.md), so the production migrate role sees all rows
(superuser or BYPASSRLS, not verified). On a database whose owner is neither, it would print "0 organization(s)
moved". Its test (`tests/test_navigation_new_for_everyone_migration.py`) runs on a fresh database of its own and does
not exercise that case. Any new data migration must follow the patterns above and print its row count.

### 2.4 Accounts and passwords

- Accounts: `auth_users` (one row per person per organization; the same e-mail may exist in several tenants; no
  unique (tenant, email) because offboarded e-mails may be reused, so `authz.lock_account_email` takes a
  `pg_advisory_xact_lock` to serialize invitations of one address). Columns of note: `username` (the e-mail, lower
  case), `role`, `status`, `email_verified`, `mfa_*`, `view_persona`, `ai_access`, `email_preferences`,
  `password_changed_at`, `password_expires_at` (always set to None).
- Memberships: `tenant_memberships` (status ACTIVE/INVITED/blocked states, `is_default`).
- Hashing (`auth/service.py`): scrypt, random 16-byte salt, stored as `scrypt$<log2N>$<r>$<p>$<salt>$<digest>`.
  Default N=2^15, r=8, p=3 (owner decision P7, 17 Nov 2026). `MDARIX_PASSWORD_SCRYPT_LOG2_N` sets N with p=1 (tests
  lower it; production refuses below 17, `config_check.py`). Legacy `<salt>:<digest>` (N=2^14) still verifies;
  `needs_rehash` triggers an upgrade at the next sign-in. At most `MDARIX_PASSWORD_HASH_CONCURRENCY` (default 3)
  hashes run at once per process (bounded semaphore).
- `NO_PASSWORD = "!no-password"`: stored for accounts that must never sign in (invited trial owner until activation,
  sample authors, automatic-approval accounts); it never matches and still burns one check's time.
- Rules (`password_problem`, NIST SP 800-63B): 12-128 characters; at least 4 distinct characters
  (`PASSWORD_TOO_SIMPLE`); not in `auth/data/breached_passwords.txt.gz` (`PASSWORD_BREACHED`; if the list cannot be
  read only length rules apply and an error is logged); must not contain the e-mail's local part when it is 4+
  characters (`PASSWORD_CONTAINS_EMAIL`); not the current or last five passwords (`password_history`,
  `PASSWORD_REUSE_NOT_ALLOWED`). No composition rules, **no periodic expiry**.

### 2.5 Sessions and cookies

- Cookie (`auth/cookies.py`): `__Host-mdarix_session` outside development (Secure, Path=/, no Domain); `mdarix_session`
  in local HTTP development (`MDARIX_COOKIE_SECURE=true` forces the secure one). Always `HttpOnly`,
  `SameSite=Strict`.
- Lifetimes: window `SESSION_WINDOW_SECONDS` = 1 h; renewed while used when less than 30 min remain; never past
  `SESSION_SECONDS` = 12 h after sign-in; a session still owing MFA lives `PENDING_MFA_SECONDS` = 10 min and is never
  renewed. Idle timeout 30 min (2.2). Note: `auth/durable.py` has its own `SESSION_SECONDS = 3600`, which is the
  default `ttl` of `new_session` (the window), not the 12 h absolute limit.
- Storage: only `sha256(token)` in `auth_sessions`; raw tokens are `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`. A restart signs nobody
  out.
- `GET /api/v1/auth/session` re-issues the cookie on every check (the browser's watcher calls it before its deadline)
  and returns identity, `mfa_state`, `other_organizations` and `expires_at`/`idle_expires_at`.
  `frontend/src/features/shared/session.ts` and `SessionTimeout.tsx` warn 2 minutes before the end and sign out.
- Revocation: sign-out (audited `SIGN_OUT`), password change (other sessions), reset/activation (all), MFA reset,
  suspension/removal, trial-owner promotion on conversion.

### 2.6 Sign-in, organization choice, throttling and rate limits

`POST /api/v1/auth/signin` (`auth_router.py:872`):
1. Rate limit `signin:ip` (30 per 5 min). Lockout row per **sha256(email|client IP)** in `login_throttles`: 5 wrong
   passwords lock that address out of that account for 15 min (429 `ACCOUNT_TEMPORARILY_LOCKED`). A failed attempt is
   counted and audited in its own short transaction, then `signin:account` (10 per 15 min, failures only) is enforced.
2. System scope to find every ACTIVE, verified account with that e-mail whose password matches; an unknown e-mail
   burns one hash's time. Accounts this deployment does not serve are dropped (answer stays `INVALID_CREDENTIALS`).
3. Several organizations: the body's optional `tenant_id` chooses **among the person's own matched accounts only**;
   without it, more than one open organization returns `TENANT_SELECTION_REQUIRED` with names (no session yet). This
   is the only place a tenant id comes from a body, and it cannot widen access.
4. `ensure_tenant_access` after the password is verified (suspended organization answers are audited
   `SIGN_IN_FAILED` with the reason).
5. Rehash if needed; MFA state from `mfa.session_state_for` (VERIFY when enrolled, ENROLL when policy requires it);
   session TTL 10 min when MFA is owed, else 1 h; audited `SIGN_IN_SUCCEEDED`.

Rate limits (`backend/app/rate_limit.py`, Postgres fixed-window counters `rate_limit_counters`, keys salted with
`MDARIX_RATE_LIMIT_SALT`): `signin:ip` 30/300 s, `signin:account` 10/900 s, `signup:ip` configurable/h,
`signup:email` 3/day, `forgot:ip` 10/h, `forgot:email` 3/h, `token:ip` 20/h, `mfa:ip` 30/300 s, `mfa:user` 10/300 s,
`mfa:change` 10/h, `demo:ip` 5/h, `invite:user` 10/day, `invite:org` 10/day, plus `invite-resend:*`,
`password-change:user`, `feedback:user`. If the counter table is unreachable the authentication scopes answer 503 in
staging/production; others fail open. `MDARIX_RATE_LIMITS_ENABLED=false` is for automated tests only; production
refuses it.

### 2.7 Activation, password reset, link status, change password

- Links (`auth/durable.py`): invitation/activation valid 48 h (`INVITATION_SECONDS`), reset 1 h. A new link retires
  the person's earlier unused ones. `peek` checks without spending; `consume` is one conditional `UPDATE ...
  RETURNING`, so of two simultaneous uses only one wins.
- `POST /auth/activate-account` and `/auth/reset-password` (`_redeem`): system scope; refuse if the account is in an
  administrator-imposed state (`SUSPENDED`, `OFFBOARDED`, `LOCKED`, `DISABLED`); check the new password **before**
  spending the link; then set password, status ACTIVE, `email_verified`, promote INVITED memberships to ACTIVE (never a
  suspended one), revoke all sessions, clear the sign-in lockout, audit (`ACCOUNT_ACTIVATED` or
  `PASSWORD_RESET_COMPLETED` + security notice `PASSWORD_RESET`), spend the link last. Activation sends a welcome
  e-mail after the response (says whether MFA is required).
- `POST /auth/forgot-password`: same answer and timing for every e-mail; one link per organization the e-mail has an
  account in; e-mails sent after the response. In development without SMTP the token is returned as
  `development_token`.
- `POST /auth/link-status`: read-only usability check of a link (token in the body, never a URL).
- `POST /auth/change-password`: needs the current password; other sessions revoked, this one kept; security notice
  `PASSWORD_CHANGED`.
- Administrators: `POST /admin/identity/users/password-reset` (ADM) and
  `POST /platform-admin/customer-admins/{id}/password-reset` (PA) e-mail a reset link; the current password keeps
  working until the link is used.

### 2.8 Multi-factor authentication (authenticator app, TOTP)

- Policy (`backend/app/mfa.py`): everyone enrolled must use it at sign-in. Roles in `MDARIX_REQUIRE_MFA_ROLES`
  (default outside local development: `PLATFORM_ADMIN,CUSTOMER_ADMIN,LEADER,APPROVER`; **empty in local development**)
  must enrol before doing anything else. `required_for` checks every role the person holds (account role, assigned
  custom role, additional workflow roles). Production refuses a weaker setting (`config_check.py`). Nobody can switch
  MFA off for themselves.
- Codes (`auth/totp.py`): 6 digits, 30 s step, +-1 step drift, each step used once (`mfa_last_step`). Secret sealed with
  AES-GCM under `MDARIX_MFA_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, bound to the user id (and key id when `MDARIX_MFA_ENCRYPTION_KEY_ID` is
  set; older keys in `MDARIX_MFA_ENCRYPTION_KEYS`; `scripts/reseal_mfa_secrets.py --apply` after rotation). Without a key
  outside local development, enrolment fails closed (503 `MFA_UNAVAILABLE`).
- Enrolment: `POST /auth/mfa/enroll` returns secret + `otpauth://` URI (and `development_code` in development only),
  stored as `mfa_pending_secret_encrypted`. `POST /auth/mfa/verify-enrollment` with the first code activates it and
  returns 8 recovery codes once (`xxxxxxxx-xxxxxxxx`, stored as unsalted SHA-256, compared in constant time, each
  usable once); security notice `MFA_ENROLLED` or `MFA_REPLACED`.
- Sign-in completion: `POST /auth/mfa/verify` with a code or a recovery code; the session gets a full window
  (`_mfa_complete`). Audited `MFA_VERIFIED`, `MFA_RECOVERY_CODE_USED`.
- Replacing the authenticator / new recovery codes (My Account): fully verified session **plus** current password
  **and** a fresh authenticator code (`_reauthenticate`; for replacement a recovery code may stand in). The password is
  checked first, so a mistyped password never spends a code. The old authenticator works until the new one is
  confirmed.
- Lockout: 5 wrong codes lock MFA checks for the account for 15 min (`login_throttles` key `mfa:<user id>`;
  signatures use `sign:<user id>`, wrong passwords and codes count together); 429 `MFA_TEMPORARILY_LOCKED` /
  `SIGNATURE_TEMPORARILY_LOCKED`. Also `mfa:ip`, `mfa:user`, `mfa:change` rate limits.
- Signatures: `MDARIX_REQUIRE_MFA_FOR_SIGNATURES` (default true) - governed signatures take a fresh authenticator
  code (`governance_router.verify_signing_credentials`). `POST /auth/mfa/challenge` only confirms enrolment.
- Reset by someone else (`mfa.reset`): clears secret, pending secret, recovery hashes, last step, enrolled date;
  deletes `mfa:` and `sign:` throttles; revokes all the person's sessions. Who may: a Customer Administrator for their
  own users (`POST /admin/identity/users/{id}/mfa-reset`), a platform administrator
  (`POST /platform-admin/users/{id}/mfa-reset`, under `require_people_access`: needs a PEOPLE write grant except for
  staff accounts, the only active Customer Administrator, and trial people), the trial owner for colleagues
  (`POST /trial/team/{id}/mfa-reset`; never their own), and `scripts/reset_platform_admin_mfa.py` as break-glass for
  the last platform administrator.
- Screens: `frontend/src/features/auth/AuthPage.tsx` (`/mfa`, `/mfa?setup=1`), `features/auth/MfaAlreadyOn.tsx`,
  `features/shared/TwoFactorSettings.tsx`, `features/shared/twoFactor.ts`, `features/workspace/sections/Account.tsx`.

### 2.9 Security notices and identity e-mail (outbox)

- `auth/security_notices.py`: kinds `MFA_ENROLLED`, `MFA_REPLACED`, `MFA_RECOVERY_CODES_REGENERATED`,
  `PASSWORD_CHANGED`, `PASSWORD_RESET`. Queued with `queue_security_notice` as a `SECURITY_NOTICE` row in
  `event_outbox` **in the same transaction as the change**; the notice never contains a code, link or password.
- Team-change e-mails (`backend/app/team_notices.py`, event `TEAM_CHANGE_NOTICE`): removals, suspensions,
  restorations, withdrawn invitations, role changes, MFA resets, in a trial or a paying organization; one event per
  recipient; payloads hold ids and codes only, addresses are looked up at send time; MDARIX support is shown as
  "MDARIX support", never a staff name.
- Worker (`outbox/worker.py`): one event per transaction, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`; backoff from
  `MDARIX_OUTBOX_BASE_DELAY_SECONDS` (30 s) doubling to `MDARIX_OUTBOX_MAX_DELAY_SECONDS` (1 h), up to
  `MDARIX_OUTBOX_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (12); then dead-lettered and alerted once. Platform console: list, retry, discard,
  record a mail-log check (`/platform-admin/console/dead-letters*`).
- Direct (not outboxed) e-mails sent after the response with `BackgroundTasks`: password reset, welcome, trial request
  alert/acknowledgement, trial invitation (a failed one is recorded on the request and retried).
- SMTP (`auth/emailer.py`): one SMTP path; `MDARIX_SMTP_HOST`, `_USERNAME`, `_PASSWORD`, `_FROM`, `_PORT`,
  `MDARIX_SMTP_VERIFIED_DOMAIN`, `MDARIX_APP_URL`. Production refuses to start without SMTP. `GET /admin/smtp-status`
  (PA) returns which settings are present (booleans only, never values).

### 2.10 Single sign-on: not available

There is no SSO. `POST /platform-admin/tenants/{tenant_id}/identity-provider/verify` always answers 409
`SSO_NOT_AVAILABLE` and records nothing (`platform_onboarding.py:488`). `PUT /admin/security/identity-provider` (ADM)
only stores an HTTPS issuer as PENDING (`identity_provider_configurations`); nothing uses it for sign-in. Do not claim
SSO anywhere.

### 2.11 Roles, permissions and segregation of duties

- Role codes (`backend/app/role_codes.py`): `CANONICAL_SYSTEM_ROLES` = VIEWER, SERVICE_DESK, TRIAGE_LEAD,
  INVESTIGATOR, PRODUCT_QUALITY_ENGINEER, RISK_REVIEWER, REGULATORY_REVIEWER, REVIEWER, APPROVER, LEADER,
  PLATFORM_ADMIN, CUSTOMER_ADMIN. Labels: Service Desk, Complaint Handler (TRIAGE_LEAD), Investigator, Product Quality
  Engineer, Risk Reviewer, Regulatory Reviewer, Quality Reviewer (REVIEWER), Decision Maker (APPROVER), Quality Leader
  (LEADER), Executive (view only) (VIEWER), Customer Administrator, Platform Administrator. Codes never change; labels
  may.
- `WORKFLOW_ROLES` (roles a person may hold **in addition** to the account role, table `user_additional_roles`):
  TRIAGE_LEAD, INVESTIGATOR, PRODUCT_QUALITY_ENGINEER, RISK_REVIEWER, REGULATORY_REVIEWER, REVIEWER, APPROVER, LEADER
  (8; STATUS.md's "seven workflow roles" predates PRODUCT_QUALITY_ENGINEER). Administrator roles are never additional
  and additional roles never widen an administrator account (`roles_of_user`).
- Aliases: `PLATFORM_ADMIN_ALIASES` includes `ADMIN`/`ADMINISTRATOR` for canonicalising stored values, but the
  platform route guard accepts only `PLATFORM_ADMIN` or `MDARIX_ADMINISTRATOR` (`is_strict_platform_admin_role`). A
  custom role may not be named like an administrator (`is_administrator_name`, R11); an administrator account keeps
  its administrator role whatever role assignment exists (`role_of_user`).
- Business permissions (`backend/app/access/permissions.py ROLE_PERMISSIONS`): `records.write`, `ai.run`,
  `data.export`, `regulatory.approve`, `signal.triage`, `investigation.create|assign|work`, `evidence.review|request`,
  `risk.review`, `regulatory.review`, `quality.review`, `decision.sign`, `case.log`, `case.classify`,
  `complaint.triage`. VIEWER none; LEADER and PLATFORM_ADMIN all; CUSTOMER_ADMIN only `records.write`, `ai.run`,
  `data.export` (never approval or signature). Unknown/custom roles get nothing (read-only). Effective permission =
  union over all roles. `require_permission(<perm>)` is the FastAPI dependency; 403 `PERMISSION_DENIED`. Reading needs
  only a signed-in tenant session.
- `/api/v1/session/context` returns `business_permissions` computed by the same rule so the UI can hide what the server
  refuses; hiding is never the control.
- Segregation of duties (`governance_router.approval_check`): any administrator role among the person's roles ->
  `ADMINISTRATOR_CANNOT_SIGN`; otherwise some held role must have an ACTIVE `approval_authorities` row for
  Decision/INVESTIGATION_REVIEW; an ACTIVE `segregation_of_duties_policies` row enforces `creator_cannot_approve` and
  `last_material_editor_cannot_approve` (read from the audit stream). `ensure_governance_defaults` gives every tenant
  Approver and Leader authority and a no-self-approval policy (only what is missing). Workflow tasks: `_own_decision`
  in `backend/app/workflow/router.py` stops a person taking the approval of their own decision or the quality review
  of a Decision Brief they wrote. SoD is per person, whatever roles they hold.
- Administration of these: `POST /admin/governance/approval-authorities`, `POST /admin/governance/sod-policies`,
  `PATCH /admin/governance/policies/{type}/{id}/status`, `GET /admin/governance/policies`,
  `GET /admin/governance/entitlements` (all ADM).
- `POST /api/v1/me/active-role` accepts only the account's own role (403 `ROLE_NOT_ASSIGNED` otherwise); there is no
  real role switching.

### 2.12 Personas, "Viewing as" and the navigation setting

Two persona catalogs exist; do not mix them:

1. **Workspace personas** (`access_control/personas.py WORKSPACE_PERSONAS`), what "Viewing as" and the trial team use:

| Code | Label | Role it maps to | Tabs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `admin` | Admin (trial owner, sees everything) | LEADER | home, complaints, investigations, products, decisions |
| `quality` | Quality Leader | LEADER | home, complaints, investigations, products, decisions |
| `regulatory` | Regulatory Reviewer | REGULATORY_REVIEWER | home, complaints, decisions |
| `servicedesk` | Service Desk | SERVICE_DESK | home, complaints |
| `handler` | Complaint Handler | TRIAGE_LEAD | home, complaints |
| `investigator` | Investigator | INVESTIGATOR | home, complaints, investigations, products |
| `pqe` | Product Quality Engineer | PRODUCT_QUALITY_ENGINEER | home, investigations, products |
| `risk` | Risk Reviewer | RISK_REVIEWER | home, investigations, products |
| `decision` | Decision Maker | APPROVER | home, complaints, investigations, decisions |
| `executive` | Executive (view only) | VIEWER | home, investigations, products, decisions |

   `COLLEAGUE_PERSONAS` = the nine without `admin`. `persona_for_role` maps REVIEWER to `quality`, CUSTOMER_ADMIN and
   PLATFORM_ADMIN to `admin`, any custom role to `quality`.
2. **Legacy R1 personas** (`R1_PERSONAS`: INVESTIGATOR, PRODUCT_QUALITY, REVIEWER, APPROVER, LEADER, ADMINISTRATOR),
   used only by `GET /admin/identity/personas` and the `persona_assignments` table (`/admin/identity/persona-assignments`).
   They grant nothing.

Rules (`backend/app/access/session.py`):
- A persona is a **view, never a permission**: every route follows the person's real role(s).
- `persona_state` (part of `/api/v1/session/context`): `persona`, `persona_label`, `persona_locked`, `trial_owner`,
  `persona_tabs`, `persona_options`.
- `POST /api/v1/me/persona`: the trial owner may view as any persona (stored in `auth_users.view_persona`, kept for the
  next sign-in, audited `PERSONA_VIEW_CHANGED` with `role_changed: false`; the owner's role stays LEADER). A colleague
  may switch only between personas the owner gave them, and only when they hold two or more; otherwise 403
  `PERSONA_LOCKED`. A pilot/customer user's persona is their role's (locked). Unknown code 422 `UNKNOWN_PERSONA`.
- Trial owner = the account whose e-mail equals the trial's `onboarding_requests.email` (`team.is_trial_owner`), while
  the tenant is on the Free Trial plan (`owns_trial`).
- Frontend: `frontend/src/features/workspace/UserWorkspace.tsx:756` posts `/api/v1/me/persona`; tabs drive
  `features/workspace/areas.ts`, `HybridShell.tsx`.

Navigation: `tenants.navigation` is `new` or `classic`. `/api/v1/session/context` returns `navigation` (`new` when
null). Since migration `ux2navigationnew` the column default is `new` and existing `classic` rows were moved;
`classic` stays allowed as a fallback a platform administrator sets in Tenant 360 (`PATCH /platform-admin/tenants/{id}`,
editable field `navigation`, with `lock_version` and an audited reason). Trial approval sets `new` explicitly.
Frontend: `frontend/src/app/linkTable.ts` (`NEW_PLACES` vs `PLACES`). `tenants.sample_only` (also editable there) hides
every upload/import and the import routes refuse with 403 `SAMPLE_ONLY_TRIAL`.

### 2.13 Customer administration (a tenant's own administrators)

User steps (Customer Administrator, screen `frontend/src/features/customer-admin/CustomerAdminControlPlane.tsx`,
sections Users, Access, Security, SupportAccess, Subscription, Organization, Notifications, Audit, Dashboard,
Integrations, DataMapping, AiGovernance, AiReleaseGate, Reviews, EmailIntake):

- **Users** (`backend/app/access/identity.py`, all ADM): list/create (`POST /admin/identity/users`, invitation e-mail,
  48 h link), view, effective access, status change (suspend/offboard; sessions revoked), reactivate, password-reset
  link, MFA reset, additional workflow roles (`GET|PUT /admin/identity/users/{id}/roles`, full list semantics),
  erasure of an offboarded person (`POST /admin/identity/users/{id}/erase`, irreversible), personal-data export
  (`GET /admin/people/{id}/export` in `tenant_export_api.py`).
  Guards in `authz._guard_user_administration`: only platform administrators assign platform roles
  (`PLATFORM_ROLE_NOT_ALLOWED`); a customer administrator cannot touch a platform account (`PLATFORM_USER_PROTECTED`,
  and such accounts are hidden: 404 `USER_NOT_FOUND`, `visible_user_filters`); nobody changes their own role or status
  (`SELF_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED`). `_admin_target_tenant` (`access/common.py:229`): a body `target_tenant_id` other than
  the caller's own is refused for customer administrators (403 `FOREIGN_TENANT_ACCESS_DENIED`).
- **Roles and permission sets**: `/admin/identity/roles`, `/permission-sets`, `/role-permission-sets`,
  `/role-assignments`, `/memberships`, `/persona-assignments` (ADM). Custom roles grant no business permission unless
  they are a canonical role name.
- **Governance**: 2.11.
- **Security**: identity provider (PENDING only, 2.10); MFA status of users.
- **Notifications**: `GET|PUT /admin/notifications/preferences`, `POST /admin/notifications/recipients`.
- **Subscription/lifecycle**: `GET /admin/tenant/lifecycle`, `POST /admin/tenant/lifecycle/renewal-request`.
- **Observability** (`backend/app/access/observability.py`): audit history (ADM or trial owner), audit export and
  evidence package, retention status, health/security/operations/AI summaries, dashboard and trends, integration runs,
  credentials (metadata), AI executions. Tenant-scoped; platform-only `GET /admin/smtp-status`.
- **Configuration and catalog** (`configuration.py`, `catalog.py`, `connector_mapping.py`, `connector_sync.py`):
  connectors (API key stored encrypted and never shown again), mappings with release workflow
  (`release-action`, optimistic lock, audited), standard mappings, mapping check/preview, products and versions,
  mapping versions/overrides (ADM); master mappings (PA only). The generic editor `PATCH
  /admin/control-plane/{resource}/{resource_id}` (ADM).
- **Validation evidence** (`validation_evidence.py`): requirement -> test -> last recorded result and a watermarked
  dry-run pack (ADM or trial owner); only PA records a JUnit run.
- **Support access** decisions (2.15) and own **exports** (`/admin/exports`).
- **E-mail preferences** for oneself (`GET|PUT /api/v1/me/email-preferences`, USER): weekly readiness e-mail and daily
  deadline list; the last deadline-list recipient cannot switch off (409 `LAST_DEADLINE_RECIPIENT`).

Plans and entitlements: `plan_includes(db, tenant_id, feature)` (`access/plan_features.py`): no active plan
assignment -> nothing; a listed feature counts only when enabled and ACTIVE; `DECISION_CENTER_SIGNATURES` and
`ASK_MDARIX` are core (included unless the plan lists them disabled). `GET /api/v1/me/entitlements/{feature_code}`
(USER) reads the plan; `/api/v1/session/context` returns `plan`, `entitlements`, `feature_toggles`, `license`,
`feature_set`, and feature switches (`routine_path`, `challenge`, `closed_loop`, `rule_register`, `b9`, `b6`, `rca`,
`email_intake`). Tables: `plan_definitions`, `feature_entitlements`, `tenant_plan_assignments`,
`subscription_lifecycles`, `subscription_versions`.

### 2.14 Platform administration (MDARIX staff)

Every route here calls `_require_platform_admin` first (strict role check, then **system scope**). A platform
administrator's session stays in the platform's own organization ("MDARIX-OPERATOR" customer). Platform staff are
created with `scripts/create_platform_admin.py` (owner credentials; single-use activation link; its docstring says the link is valid 72 hours while `auth/durable.INVITATION_SECONDS` is 48 hours: which applies to that script is not verified). Screens:
`frontend/src/features/admin/` (`SimplifiedPlatformAdminScreen.tsx`, `console/AdminOverview.tsx`,
`customers/*`, `trials/*`, `areas/*`, `TrialRequestsWorkspace.tsx`, `TrialFeedbackWorkspace.tsx`,
`DemoRequestsWorkspace.tsx`, `MvpRequestsWorkspace.tsx`, `PlatformAdministrationAudit.tsx`, `CreateCustomer.tsx`).

**Customers and tenants (customer gates tenants; "Inactive" = SUSPENDED).**
- A Customer (`customers`) is the legal/commercial parent; a Tenant (`tenants`) the data-isolation boundary. One
  customer owns one or more tenants; a tenant belongs to exactly one customer for life (`customer_lifecycle.py`).
- Lifecycle matrix `LIFECYCLE_TRANSITIONS`: DRAFT -> PENDING_APPROVAL -> PROVISIONING -> PROVISIONED ->
  PENDING_CUSTOMER_ACTIVATION -> ACTIVE <-> EXPIRING_SOON/EXPIRED/SUSPENDED -> TERMINATED -> ARCHIVED. Tenants may also
  go PROVISIONED -> ACTIVE directly.
- Customer status route `POST /platform-admin/customer-records/{customer_id}/lifecycle` (PA, re-authentication,
  optimistic lock): only ACTIVE or SUSPENDED targets (`CUSTOMER_STATUS_SOURCES`). Only the customer's own status
  changes; its tenants keep theirs. A customer in SUSPENDED/TERMINATED/ARCHIVED blocks sign-in, the API
  (`request_context.ensure_tenant_access`) and background jobs (`customer_allows_access_clause` in `scheduler.py`,
  `signals/detection.py`, `readiness_home/digest.py`, `reportability/reminders.py`,
  `decision_followups/impact.py`; `tenants_of_blocking_customers` in `access/connector_sync.py`) for every tenant it
  owns. Reactivating restores exactly the tenants that are themselves active. The operator customer can never be
  suspended (409 `OPERATOR_PROTECTED`).
- Tenant status `POST /platform-admin/tenants/{id}/lifecycle`: activation only while the customer is ACTIVE or
  EXPIRING_SOON (409 `CUSTOMER_NOT_ACTIVE`); the operator's tenant is never suspended/terminated. Scheduled transitions:
  `.../lifecycle/schedule` and `POST /platform/lifecycle/run-scheduled`.
- The UI labels SUSPENDED as "Inactive" and the action "Deactivate"/"Reactivate"
  (`frontend/src/features/admin/customers/model.ts statusAction`, `CustomerDirectory.tsx:97`, `TenantPages.tsx:111`).
- Customer 360 / Tenant 360 (`platform_customers.py`, `platform_tenants.py`): metadata, environments (create, update,
  clone), security, health, audit; editable tenant fields `TENANT_EDITABLE_FIELDS` (includes `navigation`,
  `sample_only`, regions) with `lock_version` and a reason (audited `TENANT_UPDATED`). Tabs and integration details
  that show the organization's own data require a customer-approved grant (`+GRANT`).
- Customer CSV import, onboarding wizard (drafts, validate, idempotent `POST /platform/tenants/provision`, provisioning
  jobs with resume/retry), domain verification.
- Customer Administrators (`platform_customer_admins.py`): list, add to a tenant (activation e-mail; `+ADMCHG` once the
  tenant has an active administrator or is a trial), resend invitation, password-reset e-mail, suspend/reactivate (never
  the last active one), replace (`platform_tenants.py`), activation link for an unactivated person, MFA reset and
  erasure under `require_people_access`. `customer_admin_continuity.replacement_action` retires the incumbent only
  after a different active admin exists.
- Accounts (`platform_accounts.py`): platform users, customer users (read-only), role catalog with counts.
- Plans and subscriptions (`platform_subscriptions.py`): plan catalog/create, a tenant's subscription and usage,
  per-tenant limits (`PUT .../limits`, cannot go below current use; codes such as `CUSTOMER_ADMIN_LIMIT_REACHED`),
  subscription change (`POST /platform/customers/{tenant_id}/subscription`, which also converts a trial and promotes its
  owner, 2.17), expiring list, lifecycle run, reminder policies and e-mail templates.
- Console (`platform_console.py`): renewals, reminder settings, integration runs, AI activity, audit (no old/new
  values), security posture, support-access overview, operations, dead letters, dashboard. Metadata only, never
  customer content.
- Reference (`platform_reference.py`, `platform_reference_data.py`): AI models, API contract (`GET
  /platform-admin/api-contract`, OpenAPI of the registry), canonical model, mapping impact, security controls, product
  configuration of a tenant (`+GRANT`), master reference data (read-only; changing it is a controlled release).
- Legal hold (`legal_hold_api.py`): `PUT` requires PA and an MFA-complete session; blocks purge and erasure.
- Demo requests (`demo_requests.py`), MVP requests and trial queues (`trial/router.py`), problem reports
  (`support_api.py`), feedback publication (`trial/feedback.py`).

### 2.15 Support access (customer-approved, time-boxed, read-only workspace)

`backend/app/support_access.py` (ADR-015) is the only way MDARIX staff reach an organization's data:
1. A platform administrator requests access (`POST /platform-admin/support-access`) for a named colleague: areas
   (`CONFIGURATION`, `AUDIT`, `PEOPLE`, `RECORDS`, `DATA_EXPORT`), read or write, reason, window of at most 24 h
   (`validate_support_window`).
2. A **different** platform administrator approves it for MDARIX (neither requester nor recipient).
3. The organization decides: a Customer Administrator, or in a free trial **its owner only**
   (`support_access_api.require_decision_maker`), approves with a current authenticator code, or denies. Either side
   revokes at any time; it expires on its own.
4. Every platform route that touches the organization's data calls `require_customer_approved_access`; use is written
   to the organization's own audit trail as `SUPPORT_ACCESS_USED`, a refusal as `SUPPORT_ACCESS_REFUSED` (403
   `CUSTOMER_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`).
- Grant states: PENDING, AWAITING_CUSTOMER, APPROVED, DENIED, REVOKED, EXPIRED (`support_access_grants`).
- The **support workspace** (`backend/app/support_workspace.py`) is GET-only platform routes, each through `_gate`
  (one area each): complaints, investigations, decisions, imports (RECORDS), team (PEOPLE), audit (AUDIT). Named fields
  only, never passwords, secrets, recovery codes, tokens or sessions. The staff session never enters the customer's
  organization.
- Not behind a grant by design: the platform's own organization; registry/lifecycle/plan/usage records; legal hold and
  final purge; problem reports the customer sent; what staff did; continuity recovery (the only Customer
  Administrator's authenticator, the first activation, trial people's authenticators).
- Screens: `customer-admin/sections/SupportAccess.tsx`, `workspace/sections/TrialSupportAccess.tsx`,
  `admin/customers/SupportWorkspace.tsx`.

### 2.16 Erasure, export, purge, legal hold

- Erasure of one person (`tenant_purge.erase_person`): only OFFBOARDED/REMOVED/SUSPENDED accounts
  (`USER_STILL_ACTIVE` otherwise), refused under legal hold; pseudonymizes the account (name, e-mail, credentials),
  deletes sign-in records, sessions, invitations, unpublished feedback and roles; audit rows are not changed; logged
  in `erasure_log` so a restore can re-apply it. Routes: ADM (`/admin/identity/users/{id}/erase`), PA
  (`/platform-admin/users/{id}/erase`, `+PEOPLE`).
- Organization purge (`POST /platform-admin/tenants/{id}/purge`, PA): every table with `tenant_id` discovered from the
  database; keeps the archived tenant row, subscription/plan history, pseudonymized accounts, the trial request,
  published consented feedback and `erasure_log`. Two stages; `scripts/complete_tenant_purge.py` finishes PURGING.
- Exports: own (`/admin/exports`, ADM), platform (`/platform-admin/tenants/{id}/exports`, PA + DATA_EXPORT grant for
  request and download).

### 2.17 The free trial (`backend/app/trial/`)

**Steps and rules.**

1. **Sign-up** `POST /api/v1/auth/signup` (PUBLIC, `signup:ip` and `signup:email` limits): valid e-mail
   (`team._EMAIL`), name and organization required and must not look like a link (`NAME_HAS_LINK`,
   `ORGANIZATION_HAS_LINK`), profile validated (`trial/request_profile.py`). An e-mail that already has an account
   anywhere -> 409 `EMAIL_ALREADY_REGISTERED`; an existing request -> 409 `TRIAL_ALREADY_REQUESTED` (this reveals
   known e-mails by product decision of 2026-10-11). Stores `onboarding_requests` (PENDING_REVIEW) with
   `legal_acceptance` (document versions and time). Frontend `features/auth/SignupPage.tsx`, `SignupForm.tsx`,
   `trialProfile.ts`.
2. **Approval**: automatic (`trial/auto_approval.py`, `MDARIX_TRIAL_AUTO_APPROVE` = `company-and-personal` default |
   `company-domains` | `off`; any other value reads as `company-domains`; disposable domains, second request of a
   company, non-resolving domains, daily caps `MDARIX_TRIAL_AUTO_APPROVE_DAILY_MAX` (20) and
   `..._PERSONAL_DAILY_MAX` (10), and the platform's own addresses wait for review; approver is one of two
   non-sign-in "Automatic approval" accounts) or manual (`POST /platform-admin/trial-requests/{id}/approve`, PA,
   optional `sample_only`). `service.approve_request` is **one transaction**: customer + tenant (status ACTIVE,
   `navigation='new'`), Free Trial plan assignment, 30-day `subscription_lifecycles` (`TRIAL_DAYS`), sandbox
   environment, shared data-plane row, oversight time zone from the sign-up country, the owner account
   (role `Leader`, status INVITED, `NO_PASSWORD`), membership, the whole sample story (`sample_clock.seed_sample`),
   audit `TRIAL_REQUEST_APPROVED`. After commit: demo signals seeded, invitation sent and recorded (a failure never
   undoes the approval). Decline queues a "request declined" e-mail through the outbox (`backend/app/notifications.py`); resend re-issues the link.
3. **Activation**: the owner sets a password through the link (2.7); LEADER requires MFA outside local development, so
   the owner enrols an authenticator at first sign-in.
4. **Getting started** (`frontend/src/features/workspace/sections/GettingStarted.tsx`, data from
   `GET /api/v1/trial/status`, `/trial/story`, `/trial/feedback`): `days_left` (ceil of seconds left / 86400, never
   negative), `expires_at`, retention dates, limits (`users` = cap, `rows` = 5000, `file_bytes` = 2 MiB), usage, sample
   and own-data counts, CSV templates (`GET /trial/templates/{entity}.csv`), the ten-step story (`/trial/one-story`),
   the short story, chapters (`/trial/story`), cited-for focus, sample register, removal/restart of the sample
   (owner only), book a demo, ask for an MVP, end early (owner only), feedback.
5. **Import your data**: through `/api/v1/data/import` (`backend/app/data_import_router.py`, another section); refused
   for `sample_only` tenants; trial row and file limits above.
6. **Team** (`trial/team.py`, `/api/v1/trial/team*`): only the owner invites, changes personas, resends, removes,
   resets MFA (`TRIAL_OWNER_REQUIRED`). Cap `MDARIX_TRIAL_USER_CAP` (whole number >= 1, default 50; counts ACTIVE and
   INVITED non-platform accounts, the request row is locked so two invites cannot take the last seat; 409
   `TRIAL_USER_LIMIT_REACHED`). Invitation limits `invite:user` and `invite:org` are checked **before** anything about
   the address is looked up. Address rules (`_invitable`): no account anywhere -> new account; an account in **another
   organization** -> one neutral 409 `CANNOT_INVITE` that does not say why; INVITED here -> 409
   `INVITATION_PENDING`; on the team -> 409 `ALREADY_ON_THE_TEAM`; a colleague removed from this trial (OFFBOARDED) ->
   the same account is re-invited (new random password, MFA reset, membership ACTIVE). Colleagues are workspace users,
   never administrators. Removal sets OFFBOARDED (never deleted; seat freed). Audits: `TRIAL_COLLEAGUE_INVITED`,
   `TRIAL_PERSONA_ASSIGNED` (personas and roles before/after). E-mails through `team_notices`.
7. **Personas for colleagues** `POST /api/v1/trial/team/{user_id}/persona` (body `persona` or `personas`): any of the
   nine colleague personas (`admin` refused, 422 `PERSONA_NOT_FOR_COLLEAGUE`); none named means `quality`. The first
   persona's role becomes the account role, each other persona's role an ACTIVE `user_additional_roles` row (removed
   ones REVOKED, rows kept); the colleague's next request reads the new role. Colleagues invited before 0.10.73 keep
   LEADER until changed. Frontend `workspace/sections/TrialTeam.tsx`.
8. **AI is off by default**: while `MDARIX_AI_TRIAL_CHAIN` is empty (default) a Free Trial tenant never reaches a model
   (`genai/governance.py`). With the chain set, a trial uses AI on sample data only, through that chain, when the
   tenant's `ai_processing_allowed` is on and the person's `auth_users.ai_access` is ACTIVE (column default ACTIVE,
   migration `tr32aiready`). Platform admin switches: `PUT /platform-admin/trial-organizations/{id}/ai-access` (all
   users) and `.../users/{user_id}/ai-access`; monthly trial budget `MDARIX_AI_TRIAL_MONTHLY_TOKENS` (default
   200,000). Sample AI answers are pre-recorded (`trial/sample_ai.py`) and not counted as AI runs.
9. **Expiry**: `trial_has_ended` (expires_at passed and no grace) is checked on every authenticated request; then 403
   `TRIAL_ENDED` with the end time and the earliest deletion date. Reminders and ended e-mails in `auth/emailer.py`.
10. **Exit early**: owner asks (`POST /trial/exit-request`, withdraw), MDARIX approves (optionally deleting data at
    once) or declines (`/platform-admin/trial-exit-requests/*`); `POST /platform-admin/trial-organizations/{id}/end`
    ends it as "Ended by MDARIX" (data kept 30 days).
11. **Extend / convert / delete** (`trial/admin_actions.py`, via `platform_trials.py`, PA): extend 1-30 days with a
    reason, never past original end + 30 days; convert to any active non-trial plan (kind PILOT or PAID); the owner is
    promoted to CUSTOMER_ADMIN and their sessions ended (`platform_subscriptions._promote_trial_owner`, audited
    `TRIAL_OWNER_PROMOTED`); delete data now only when retention says it is due, with the organization's name typed.
    Refusals audited `TRIAL_ACTION_REFUSED`.
12. **Retention** (`trial/retention.py`, `scripts/run_trial_retention.py`, `/platform-admin/trial-retention*`): data
    deleted 30 days after the trial closes (purge + pseudonymization, tenant archived); legal hold and an open "move my
    data to the MVP" request hold it (bounded); unconverted requests' personal fields erased after 90 days.
13. **Sample story refresh**: `scripts/refresh_trial_story.py` (no flag = list, `--counts` = counts only without names,
    `--apply` = write) adds missing sample records to trials approved before a given release, in system scope; never
    changes a trial's own records. Release runbooks run `--counts`, then `--apply` after the owner's OK.
14. **Feedback, demo, MVP**: `/trial/feedback` (consent-based publication; `GET /public/testimonials` serves only
    published, consented quotes), `/trial/demo-request`, `/trial/mvp-request` and their platform queues.

### 2.20 Invariants Gemini must keep

1. Tenant and actor only from `get_request_context` / `_authenticated_user` (`ctx.tenant_id`, `ctx.user_id`). Never
   from a body or query. The only body tenant id is the sign-in organization choice among the person's own accounts,
   and the platform administrator's explicit `target_tenant_id` (refused for anyone else).
2. Tenant business data through `get_data_plane_db`; identity, tenancy, plans, configuration, platform audit through
   `get_db`.
3. Authorization on the server: every new route calls the right guard (`require_permission`, `_admin_tenant`,
   `_require_platform_admin`, `require_owner`, `require_customer_approved_access`). Hiding a menu item is not a control.
4. `_require_platform_admin` is the only route-level way into system scope. Every other `bind_system` names a reason
   and must be reviewed. Never accept a scope or token from a request.
5. Data migrations and scripts touching tenant-owned tables must enter system scope or lift FORCE inside their own
   transaction, and print/verify the row count (2.3 lesson).
6. Personas are views; roles grant rights. Administrators never sign or approve; nobody approves their own work.
7. Passwords: scrypt, NIST rules, no expiry, history of five. Never log or return a password, code, link, token,
   secret or e-mail in audit details.
8. Sessions only in Postgres; cookie `__Host-` + Secure + HttpOnly + SameSite=Strict outside development.
9. MFA required for administrators, Leaders and Approvers outside local development; reset revokes sessions; security
   notices queued in the same transaction as the change.
10. Customer gates tenants (SUSPENDED/TERMINATED/ARCHIVED customer blocks API, sign-in, jobs); operator customer and
    tenant are never suspended; a tenant is activated only under an active customer; never leave a tenant without an
    active Customer Administrator.
11. Support access: 24 h maximum, second staff approver, customer (or trial owner) approval with authenticator code,
    every use audited in the customer's trail.
12. Trial: owner-only actions stay owner-only; `CANNOT_INVITE` stays neutral; cap from `MDARIX_TRIAL_USER_CAP`; AI off
    unless the trial chain is configured; approval is all-or-nothing; no SSO claim.
13. Never name modules/flags after days, stages or packages; existing persisted identifiers (audit action codes,
    persona codes, role codes) never change.
14. Every behaviour change ships with a test that fails without it; run the gates listed in AGENTS.md.

### 2.21 Known limits and observations (read before changing)

- `ux2navigationnew` data update has no system scope (2.3). Not verified on a non-BYPASSRLS owner.
- `POST /admin/identity/users` with a foreign `target_tenant_id` by a platform administrator goes through
  `_admin_tenant` (tenant-bound, no `bind_system`), so under the runtime role the target tenant may not be visible and
  the call may answer 404 `TENANT_NOT_FOUND`. **Not verified.** Platform administrators normally use the
  customer-admin routes in `platform_customer_admins.py`.
- A colleague's **second** persona whose role is SERVICE_DESK or VIEWER is stored in `user_additional_roles`, but
  `permissions.additional_roles_of_user` keeps only `WORKFLOW_ROLES`, so it adds no permissions (VIEWER has none
  anyway; SERVICE_DESK's `case.log`, `case.classify`, `records.write` are not added). `colleague_personas` still shows
  the persona. Observed in code; not verified by a test.
- The trial owner is identified by e-mail equality with the trial request (`is_trial_owner`); erasure or a changed
  username loses ownership. Not verified.
- Sign-up deliberately reveals known e-mails (409); the colleague invite deliberately does not.
- `MDARIX_REQUIRE_MFA_ROLES` is empty in local development, so local behaviour differs from production.
- Without `MDARIX_DB_RUNTIME_USER`, requests use the owner connection; RLS then applies only if the owner is subject to
  forced RLS (it is, unless superuser/BYPASSRLS). Production configuration requires the runtime role.
- `/api/v1/me/active-role` does not switch roles. SSO is not implemented.
- The `persona_assignments` / R1 persona catalog is legacy and grants nothing.
- The 12 Nov `docs/handover/*.md` files are out of date; trust code.

### 2.24 Endpoints by module (every endpoint, from Appendix A)

Guard column: see the legend at the top of section 2. "delegates" means the handler calls another handler of the same
module that applies the guard. "(checked in service)" means the owner rule is enforced in `backend/app/trial/team.py`
or `early_exit.py` (403 `TRIAL_OWNER_REQUIRED` / `TRIAL_OWNER_ONLY`).
