## 3. Complaint-to-CAPA functional flow

This section follows one signal from the moment it arrives to the signed check of whether the action worked. It was written from the code of branch `feat/app-wide-layout` (release 0.11.07 candidate) on 19 Nov 2026 and reconciled against Appendix A (endpoints), Appendix B (tables) and Appendix C (migrations).

Paths are relative to the repository root. `file:line` references point to the line where a rule or function starts in that commit. Statements marked **not verified** were not confirmed in the code.

The endpoint tables in this section are generated from Appendix A. Every endpoint of every module this section owns is listed once, in the capability that uses it.

### 3.0 The flow at a glance and the rules that hold everywhere

#### 3.0.1 The flow

```
 Phone / visit / e-mail (mail_in)   QMS or file import (trial/imports, ingestion)   Manual signal
            |                                     |                                       |
            v                                     v                                       |
   Case = intake event (intake)  ---link--->  Complaint (complaints)                      |
     | Case check Q1-Q10, signed classification      |  second look / backlog (complaint_review)
     | triage at two speeds (FAST/ROUTINE), clocks   |  disposition signed (complaint_disposition)
     | escalate -> Complaint                         |  resolve-and-close / reopen
     v                                               v                                    v
                 Signal detection rule / trend check (signals) --> Signal triage --> disposition
                                                           |
                                                           v  open case (create-investigation / open-case)
   Investigation ("case"): workflow state, versioned scope, readiness (case_workflow), My Work tasks (workflow)
   Six-step cockpit: Signal | Product reality | Evidence | Possible causes | Brief | Decision & action
     Product reality: Product 360, Lifecycle Twin, change impact, field scope, lot impact (trial only)
     Evidence: inventory, review, evidence requests; unknowns; contradictions
     Possible causes: hypotheses (AI vs human), observations, failure chains, challenge, RCA (fishbone, 5 whys)
     Brief: risk / regulatory reviews (case_reviews), Decision Brief versions and quality review (decision_brief)
     Decision & action: signed human decision (human_decision) -> follow-ups -> CAPA (decision_followups)
                        -> CAPA effectiveness plan + signed result (capa_effectiveness)
                        -> "Did it work?" read-out and re-open triggers (closed_loop) -> impact assessment
   Around it: case audit / replay (case_audit), readiness home and inspection pack (readiness_home),
              communications log (communications), signed periodic reviews (signed_reviews)
```

#### 3.0.2 Rules that hold across the whole flow

The six-step cockpit is defined in `frontend/src/features/workspace/caseWorkflow.ts:454-536` (`CASE_SECTIONS`, `STEP_QUESTION`, `STEP_PLACE`, `PART_STEP`, `cockpitStep()`).

**1. Tenancy and plane.** Every flow route takes the tenant from `get_request_context` and reads business data through `get_data_plane_db`. The Guards column in Appendix A confirms this for every endpoint listed here.

Every lookup filters on `tenant_id`. A record that belongs to another organization answers 404, never 403. Examples:
- `case_intelligence/router.py:111-118`
- `evidence_workflow/inventory.py:60-67`
- `case_ai/router.py:39-49`

Signatures are written to the control plane (`signed_approval_records`) and to the data plane in one two-database protocol (point 3).

**2. Reads and writes.**
- **Reads need only a signed-in session.** No read permission is checked (`case_intelligence/router.py:17-20`). Writes carry a permission dependency, which is the "dependency" entry in the Guards column.
- **Refusals are audited.** In intake, `require_any_permission_audited` refuses by default and records an `ACTION_REFUSED` audit event (`backend/app/intake/router.py:75, 90-120`).
- **Closed cases refuse writes** with 409 `CASE_CLOSED`. This applies when `workflow_state` is CLOSED or CANCELLED, or when the legacy `status` is "closed" (`evidence_workflow/rules.py:45, 63-65`).

**3. Electronic signatures.** There is one helper: `governance_router.verify_signing_credentials` (`backend/app/governance_router.py:439-524`).

What it checks:
- **Password:** always required. A wrong password gives 403 `INVALID_REAUTHENTICATION` and the audit event `SIGNATURE_REAUTHENTICATION_FAILED`.
- **Authenticator code:** also required when `mfa.signatures_require_mfa()` is true (`backend/app/mfa.py:45`, env `MDARIX_REQUIRE_MFA_FOR_SIGNATURES`, default "true").

The code refusals are:

| Refusal | When |
|---|---|
| `SIGNATURE_MFA_NOT_SET_UP` | The signer has no authenticator enrolled. |
| `SIGNATURE_CODE_ALREADY_USED` | The code was used before. This refusal does not count toward the lock. |
| `SIGNATURE_CODE_INVALID` | The code is wrong. |

- **Recovery codes** are never accepted for signing: `check_code(..., allow_recovery=False)`.
- **Throttle:** wrong passwords and wrong codes share one "sign" throttle. Five failures lock signing for 15 minutes (`mfa.py:27-28`).
- **On refusal** the helper rolls the session back, writes the caller's audit event, commits and raises.
- **Return value:** it returns `mfa_verified`. Callers store it on the signing audit event.

How a signature is written:
- **The record** is a `signed_approval_records` row (`db/models/foundation.py:1160`). It has a unique constraint `uq_signed_approval_version` on `(tenant_id, object_type, object_id, object_version, decision_type)` (`foundation.py:1180-1188`).
- **The hashes:**
  - `fingerprint` is the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of the signed material (`human_decision/rules.py:521-523`).
  - `signature_hash` is `sha256(fingerprint + signer_user_id + version)` (`human_decision/rules.py:526-528`).
- **The two-database commit:** `signing_commit.commit/settle` (`backend/app/signing_commit.py`) moves a signature through PREPARE, then PENDING (control DB), then SAVED (data plane), then SIGNED.
  - A PENDING signature is never valid.
  - The scheduler job `settle_pending` (`signing_commit.py:233`) settles anything left PENDING after 2 minutes. Without a receipt it marks the signature abandoned (`SIGNATURE_ABANDONED`).
- **Order matters:** `verify_signing_credentials` must run before anything is staged in the control session. A refusal commits the control session (`lot_impact/router.py:360`, `capa_effectiveness/router.py:310`, `signed_reviews/signing.py:106`).

**4. Administrators never sign.**
- Platform admins, admins, administrators and customer admins are refused with 403 `ADMINISTRATOR_CANNOT_SIGN` (`intake/router.py:1242` `_signer_role`; `complaint_disposition/router.py:55`; human decision sign step 1; CAPA effectiveness `ADMINISTRATOR_ROLES`, `capa_effectiveness/router.py:37, 292-293`).
- Approvers are never administrators (`workflow/approvals.py:55, 60-68`).

**5. Segregation of duties (SoD).** These are the actual checks in the code. The gaps are listed in 3.31.

| Where | Rule | Code |
|---|---|---|
| Case check QA review | The reviewer is not the signer (outside a trial) | 403 `REVIEWER_IS_SIGNER`, `intake/router.py:1681` |
| QMS "not needed" | Not the Case signer (outside a trial) | 403 `SAME_PERSON`, `intake/router.py:1889` |
| Void a Case | Not the logger (outside a trial) | 403 `SAME_PERSON`, `intake/router.py:2989` |
| Triage downgrade | The requester cannot approve | 403 `SAME_PERSON`, `intake/router.py:2828` |
| Threat exclusion | The person who marked the threat cannot exclude it (outside a trial) | 403 `SAME_PERSON`, `intake/router.py:2752` |
| Mail "not a case" | Whoever opened the message cannot dismiss it (outside a trial) | 403 `SAME_PERSON`, `mail_in/router.py:249` |
| Decision Brief review | Creator, submitter and contributors never start or do the review | `decision_brief/rules.py:132-138` `sod_denied`; `decision_brief/router.py:750-756` |
| Human decision sign or return | Brief authors (created_by, submitted_by, contributors, task `brief_author_ref(s)`) never decide | `human_decision/rules.py:361-366` `is_author`; `human_decision/service.py:98-115`; `human_decision/router.py:707-708` |
| My Work claim | Nobody claims the approval of their own decision, or the QUALITY_REVIEW or DECISION_APPROVAL task of a brief they wrote | `workflow/router.py:147-156` `_own_decision`, enforced on claim `:745-746` |
| Audit-trail review confirmation | The second reviewer is not the first signer | 403 `SECOND_REVIEWER_REQUIRED`, `signed_reviews/router.py:305-306` |
| Access review | Nobody marks their own row | `signed_reviews/access_review.py:167-186` |
| Approvals (`workflow/approvals.py`) | Self-approval only in a trial; "a pilot always enforces it" | `approvals.py:6-8, 75` |

**6. Trial exemption.** In a free trial (`is_trial`), the same person may perform most second-person steps. The audit records this as "one person". Pilots always enforce SoD.

**7. Clocks, IST and UTC.**
- **Storage.** Every stored timestamp is UTC: `intake/rules.py:482` `utc`, `case_workflow/states.py:60-61` `now_utc`, `capa_effectiveness/service.py:25-26` `today_utc`.
- **Organization zone.** A local zone enters only through:
  - the organization's working calendar (`oversight.service.calendar_of(...)`, with `day_end`, `working_day_end` and `local_day`);
  - the time zone the user names when entering a wall-clock time.
- **Readiness "today".** `readiness_home/service.py:112-117` `organization_today`: "2 Nov in India from 00:00 IST, where the UTC date still read 1 Nov until 05:30". This is tested by `tests/test_readiness_org_day.py`.
- **Intake times.** A time without an offset is read in the zone the user chose (`intake/router.py:873-893` `_zone`/`_at`). **The fallback zone when none is given is `"UTC"`, not the organization's zone.** Case import falls back to the organization zone instead (`intake/case_import.py:151-155, 289-292`).
- **Regulatory day counting** lives in `backend/app/reportability/deadlines.py`, which is outside this section. India is in `AUTHORITY_ZONES` as "Asia/Kolkata" (`:42`). `DAY_ONE_MARKETS = {"GB","IN"}` (`:43`). India counts "the awareness day is day 1; India time (IST) or yours, whichever ends first" (`:50`). A date entered without a time is stored at 12:00 UTC (`deadlines.py:56`). A time typed as exactly 12:00 UTC therefore reads as date-only (`:58`).
- **Calendar-based due dates:**
  - routine triage: `working_day_end(opened, 1)`;
  - Case follow-up: `working_day_end(now, 1)`;
  - Case review task: 5 working days;
  - QMS id task: 5 working days, decide-by 10;
  - CAPA reminder: `day_end(planned_on)`;
  - communications "done in MDARIX": 1 working hour.
- **UTC + N days due dates:**
  - signals: `DUE_DAYS` 1/3/5/10;
  - cases: `DUE_DAYS` 15/30/60/90;
  - follow-ups: 30 days;
  - FAST triage: `triage_fast_hours`, 24/7 clock hours.
- **Known mismatch.** The Cases list "overdue" view compares with UTC midnight (`intake/cases_list.py:105`), not the organization day.

**8. AI suggestions are never facts.**
- **Separate fields.** AI output is stored as the AI produced it, in `ai_executions` and in the AI's own `status`. A person's judgement is stored next to it (`human_status`, `reviewer_disposition = HUMAN_JUDGED`) and never overwrites it (`case_intelligence/rules.py:4-6`; `case_intelligence/router.py:573-578`).
- **Labels.** AI items carry labels:
  - "AI proposal, not reviewed" (`frontend/src/features/workspace/shared/AiReview.tsx:7`);
  - "AI interpretation";
  - "AI draft: written by AI, not reviewed; use it only as a starting point" (`decision_brief/rules.py:147`);
  - signal AI summaries come back with `"review_state": "NOT_REVIEWED"` (`signals/insight.py:835-845`).
- **Validators drop forbidden sentences.** Uncited, causal, certainty and decision sentences are dropped in:
  - the brief writer (`briefs/model_writer.py:1-7`);
  - the signal summary (`signals/insight.py:788-799`);
  - the second-look explanation (`complaint_review/explain.py`, at most 3 sentences quoting record fields).
  The hypothesis validator forbids PROVEN, ROOT_CAUSE and ESTABLISHED_ROOT_CAUSE (`hypothesis_engine/validator.py`).
- **No confirmed state before a signature.** There is no CONFIRMED or "root cause" state anywhere before a signed decision. A confirmed cause exists only through `rca/rules.py:194-217` `root_cause_problem`, called by the decision signature.
- **Nothing runs by itself.** Case AI never starts automatically: `trigger=AUTO` returns `ON_REQUEST_ONLY` (`case_ai/router.py:95-97`). It does nothing while AI is off (`:111-120`).
- **"Worked" belongs to people.** "MDARIX never says an action worked: only a signed effectiveness check does" (`human_decision/summary.py:8`; `closed_loop/rules.py:45`).

**9. Reasons.**
- The standard minimum is **10 characters** (`REASON_MIN`): `intake/rules.py:48`, `complaint_review/rules.py:28`, `complaint_disposition/rules.py:116`, `communications/rules.py:49`, `evidence_workflow/rules.py:39`, `case_intelligence/rules.py:17`, `rca/rules.py:50`, `case_challenge/router.py:44`.
- The frontend mirrors it in `frontend/src/features/workspace/intake.ts:9` and `challenge.ts:50`.
- Exceptions:
  - decision rationale: 20;
  - case review rationale: 20;
  - impact assessment rationale: 20;
  - complaint reopen: `REOPEN_MIN=20`;
  - "OTHER" no-investigation reason: 20.

**10. Nothing is deleted.** Every change is versioned, superseded, retracted or set aside, and audited through `make_audit_event` into `audit_events`, which is append-only (migration `o31auditimmutability`). Examples:
- Case scope versions are append-only (`case_workflow/scope.py:1-7`).
- Evidence is never unlinked (`evidence_workflow/rules.py:110-111`).
- Hypothesis links are retracted with `retracted_at`.
- Signed decisions are superseded.
- Signed dispositions are superseded (`complaint_disposition/rules.py:143`).
- Purged e-mails stay as PURGED rows.

The only deletes found in scope are derived retrieval data: embeddings on reindex (`retrieval/service.py:131-134`). **Immutability of brief versions and decisions is enforced in application code only.** Database triggers protect only `business_id` (`md32`, `md33`).

**11. Hooks never block.** Side effects run after the main commit inside a SAVEPOINT. A failure is logged and swallowed (`workflow/tasks.py:368-385` `run_hook`; `signals/hooks.py:1-3`). The main action is saved even if its task or notification is not.

**12. Feature switches in this flow** (`backend/app/feature_set.py`; "on unless off" unless stated):

| Switch | Effect in this flow |
|---|---|
| `MDARIX_FEATURE_SET` | `mvp` hides counterfactuals and scenarios (404) and parks the failure-chain AI step |
| `MDARIX_ROUTINE_PATH` | B7a routine path (resolve-and-close) |
| `MDARIX_CHALLENGE` | Case challenge |
| `MDARIX_CLOSED_LOOP` | Did it work? and the follow-up field-action and product-change fields |
| `MDARIX_RCA_METHODS` | RCA routes |
| `MDARIX_RCA_INTELLIGENCE` | RCA routes |
| `MDARIX_RULE_REGISTER` | Reportability floors at awareness |
| `MDARIX_B6_LATE_ENTRY` | Late-entry reason |
| `MDARIX_B6_SIMILAR_WORDING` | Similar-wording duplicates |
| `MDARIX_B6_REVIEWS` | Signed periodic reviews |
| `MDARIX_EMAIL_INTAKE` | Mail-in. **Off unless `on`** (`mail_in/__init__.py:17`) |

The trial-only gates, which answer 404 `NOT_A_TRIAL_WORKSPACE` elsewhere:
- Lifecycle Twin and field scope (`product360/twin_router.py:37-43`);
- lot impact (`lot_impact/router.py:64-75`);
- supplier lots (`product360/supplier_router.py:16-18`);
- per-lot "did it work" rows (`closed_loop`, `LOTS_TRIAL_ONLY`).

---

### 3.1 Case intake: logging a contact or an urgent event (`backend/app/intake/`)

**What the user sees and does**
1. Opens *Log a case*, either from the Cases area or from *Make it a case* on an e-mail.
2. Enters, in order:
   - the reporter's own words;
   - the received time, first-knowledge time and their time zone;
   - product, lot, serial and other identifiers;
   - the channel;
   - any answers already known.
3. Chooses CONTACT (the normal case) or URGENT.
4. Submits.
   - A CONTACT gets an unsigned Case check (3.2) and a follow-up request.
   - An URGENT case is escalated at once and raises an urgent alert.
5. While typing, the screen offers:
   - "possible duplicates" (`GET /api/v1/intake/possible-duplicates`);
   - "shipped to" sites for the lot (`GET /api/v1/intake/shipped-to`, exact consignee match only, `service.py:78`);
   - a guide (`GET /api/v1/intake/guide`).

**States and business rules**
- **Kind.** `kind` is CONTACT or URGENT (`router.py:928`). CONTACT needs `case.log`; anyone may log URGENT (`:931`).
- **Words.** The reporter's words are required (at least `REASON_MIN`), else 422 `WORDS_NEEDED` (`:935`). **The reporter's words are never edited afterwards.** `CORRECTABLE` excludes `reporter_words` (`router.py:3204-3205`).
- **Idempotency.** `request_key` returns the existing Case, also on an IntegrityError race (`:938, :1127`).
- **Future times.** A received time more than 5 minutes in the future (`_SKEW`, `:122`) gives 422 `TIME_IN_FUTURE`.
- **Awareness.** For CONTACT, `case_rules.case_awareness` (`case_rules.py:288`) is min(received, first knowledge), but only for a basis in `COUNTED_BASES` (ACTS_FOR_MANUFACTURER, PROCEDURE_DEEMS).
- **Late entry** (`MDARIX_B6_LATE_ENTRY`). Logging more than `LATE_ENTRY_HOURS=24` after receipt needs a reason (at least 10), else 422 `LATE_ENTRY_REASON_NEEDED` (`_late_entry_reason`, `:1137`). A Case made from an e-mail is exempt; its received time is the server receipt time (`:966`).
- **Masking.** `harm_text`, troubleshooting text and the late reason are masked for personal data (`_masked_text`, `:3242`).
- **Identifiers.** Each identifier is marked CONFIRMED or UNCONFIRMED with its source (`case_rules.py:330`). A product taken from the lot is UNCONFIRMED, source LOT (`:1034`).
- **CONTACT** creates `FirstContactCheck` v1 with the rule suggestion `case_outcome`, and `ensure_follow_up` (`service.py:287`).
- **URGENT** calls `escalate_case` (`service.py:1888`) and `urgent_alert.queue` (`router.py:1074-1101`).
- **Flags and references.** Flags (`rules.intake_flags`, `rules.py:420`) are audited as `INTAKE_LOGGED`. Reference prefixes are CALL, URG and SVC (`service.py:273`).
- **Database checks** (`db/models/intake.py:218-226`):
  - status is known;
  - `status <> 'ESCALATED' OR complaint_id IS NOT NULL`;
  - void reason present when voided;
  - `version >= 1`;
  - unique `(tenant_id, reference)`.
- "MDARIX never answers a question, never signs" (`intake/rules.py:29`).

**Backend files:**
- `intake/router.py`: `log_intake` `:917`, `intake_list` `:668`, `intake_detail` `:836`, `shipped_to` `:797`, `possible_duplicates` `:808`, `guide` `:287`.
- `intake/service.py`: `make_reference`, `ensure_follow_up`, `escalate_case`.
- `intake/case_rules.py`, `intake/rules.py`.

**Endpoints (`backend.app.intake.router`, all 35; the ones used by later capabilities are discussed there):**

|---|---|---|

### 3.2 The Case check: classification, signature, QA review, follow-up

**What the user sees and does**
1. Answers Q1-Q10 on the Case. Each save is `PUT /intake/{id}/check`. The rule suggestion updates live.
2. Signs the classification with password and authenticator code (`POST .../check/sign`).
3. If the outcome is "service only", "inquiry" or "not a complaint", a second person does a QA review (`POST .../check/review`): AGREED or RECLASSIFIED.
4. Closes the reporter follow-up (`POST .../follow-up`) as ANSWERED or UNRESOLVED.
5. Checks signed before 0.10.93 may be escalated without a new signature (`POST .../escalate`).

**Answers, outcomes and states**
- **Answers.** YES, NO, UNKNOWN, NOT_APPLICABLE, NOT_STATED (`rules.py:68`). NOT_STATED is allowed only on Q4-Q6 (`:339`). An unanswered question counts as UNKNOWN, never NO (`rules.outcome`, `:352`).
- **Outcomes** (`case_rules.py:39`): COMPLAINT, BOTH, SERVICE_ONLY, INQUIRY, PENDING, PENDING_CONFIRM, NOT_A_COMPLAINT.
  - `case_outcome` (`case_rules.py:148`) returns UNANSWERED while any of Q1-Q9 is open.
  - SERVICE_ONLY becomes INQUIRY when the request is INFORMATION or NONE.
- **Overrides** (`classification_problem`, `case_rules.py:168`):
  - The ANSWER_ONLY outcomes (SERVICE_ONLY, INQUIRY, PENDING_CONFIRM, NOT_A_COMPLAINT) are never reachable by override.
  - Any override needs `override_reason` (at least 10).
  - NOT_A_COMPLAINT also needs suggestion PENDING_CONFIRM and a follow-up closed ANSWERED with exactly `["NO_DEFICIENCY"]` (`_no_deficiency`, `router.py:1509`).
- **Case states** (`case_rules.py:66-101`): OPEN, PENDING, WAITING_INTERNAL, IN_REVIEW, CLOSED, ESCALATED, VOIDED.
  - `TRANSITIONS` is the complete whitelist.
  - `state_after` (`:124`): COMPLAINT or BOTH give ESCALATED; reviewed outcomes give IN_REVIEW; everything else gives PENDING.
  - **The only state writer is `service.set_case_status` (`service.py:1762`).** An illegal move audits `CASE_STATE_REFUSED` and raises.
  - CLOSED needs `close_problem`: SERVICE_ONLY needs a resolution summary of at least 10 (`case_rules.py:117`).
  - An escalated Case never moves again (`case_rules.py:110`).
- **Optimistic lock.** `seen_version` must match, else 409 `CHANGED_MEANWHILE` (`router.py:1187, 1414`).
- **Save** (`save_check`, `:1171`). An unsigned draft is updated in place. After a signature, a new version row is created. The recalculation history is appended. URGENT has no check (409).
- **Sign** (`sign_check`, `:1398`):
  - Checks the move with `_move_allowed` first (`:1519`).
  - Calls `_verify` "FIRST_CONTACT_SIGN"; the meaning text is at `:1466`.
  - The previous signed version gets `superseded_by`.
  - With a likely duplicate, an ESCALATED outcome needs `attach_to` or `create_new` (`_duplicate_choice`, `:1544`), else 422 `DUPLICATE_CHOICE_NEEDED`.
  - Escalation to a Complaint happens in the same transaction.
  - `_after_classification` (`:1318`) then opens:
    - the QMS task;
    - the review task, due in 5 working days, or min(1 working day, 24 h) when a fault or harm word is present (`service.py:342`);
    - the follow-up.
- **QA review** (`review_check`, `:1658`). Outcome AGREED or RECLASSIFIED, note of at least 10. **The reviewer is not the signer outside a trial (403 `REVIEWER_IS_SIGNER`, `:1681`).** Needs password and code. RECLASSIFIED writes a new signed version as COMPLAINT and escalates.
- **Follow-up close** (`close_follow_up`, `:1798`):
  - Outcome ANSWERED or UNRESOLVED, with a note of at least 10.
  - Also closes the communications request (`close_for_intake`).
  - PENDING moves to OPEN.
  - An unresolved "confirm no deficiency" becomes COMPLAINT (`rules.outcome`, `unresolved=True`, `:374`).
  - The follow-up is due at `working_day_end(now, 1, calendar)` (`service.py:315`).
- **Signed checks feed the second look** (`_second_look`, `:1351`). A check that started from a complaint (3.6, service-record check) sets the second-look decision: complaint outcomes give CONFIRMED, reviewed outcomes give DISMISSED.
- **Database checks:** `classification IS NULL OR signed_at IS NOT NULL`, and unique `(intake_event_id, version)` (`db/models/intake.py:289-295`).

**Endpoints:** `PUT /api/v1/intake/{event_id}/check`, `POST .../check/sign`, `POST .../check/review`, `POST .../follow-up`, `POST .../escalate`. All are in the intake.router table in 3.1.

**Tables:** `first_contact_checks`, `intake_events`, `signed_approval_records`, `workflow_tasks`, `complaints`, `record_links`, `triage_records`, `complaint_second_looks`, `communication_entries`, `audit_events`.

**Frontend:** `sections/FirstContact.tsx`.

**Tests:**
- `tests/test_case_rules.py`, `test_case_lifecycle_api.py`, `test_case_signed_before.py`, `test_pv1_case_versions.py`, `test_cases_0_10_93_script.py`.
- Frontend `caseCheck.test.tsx`, `casePage.test.tsx`, `caseWorkflow.test.ts`.

**Invariants:**
- Change Case state only through `set_case_status`.
- Unanswered never means NO.
- Signed check versions are immutable; changes create a new version.
- Escalation and the signature commit together.

### 3.3 Triage at two speeds, threat handling and downgrades

**What the user sees and does**
1. The triage list, sorted by tier and then by earliest due (`service.py:983`).
2. On the FAST track:
   - acknowledge within 1 hour;
   - fill the form: harm now, potential harm, owners and a backup, containment, action or reason, awareness confirmation.
3. On the ROUTINE track: one-click confirm when a prefill exists.
4. A QMS-imported triage is confirmed by saving the full form (`confirm-qms`).
5. Anyone may escalate a record by hand with a reason.
6. Lowering severity or awareness opens a second-person approval. Excluding a threat needs a signed exclusion.

**Rules**
- **Tier.** THREAT, FLAGGED or UNFLAGGED (`rules.py:521`), from `intake_flags`, `complaint_flags` and `quarantine_flags` (`rules.py:420-476`).
- **Speed** (`triage_rules.py:99`). FAST when any of these holds:
  - the record was reopened;
  - harm is NOT_KNOWN;
  - potential harm is at least SERIOUS;
  - any `FAST_FACTS` applies.
  Otherwise ROUTINE.
- **Due** (`triage_rules.due`, `:129`):
  - FAST = opened + `triage_fast_hours` (`oversight_settings`, default 4, `service.py:1047`), in clock hours, 24/7.
  - ROUTINE = `calendar.working_day_end(opened, 1)`.
  - Acknowledge due = min(opened + `ACK_HOURS=1`, target) (`:136`).
  - The clock reads OVERDUE when time left < 0, and DUE_SOON when time left <= hours/4 (`:140`).
  - The legacy `TRIAGE_TARGET_HOURS=24` is stored as `target_hours` (`rules.py:51`); the database requires `target_hours BETWEEN 1 AND 168`.
- **Severity floor** (`:90`). A death gives CATASTROPHIC; a serious injury gives SERIOUS. Going below the floor gives 422 `BELOW_FLOOR`. Lowering severity needs a reason of at least 10 (`:155`).
- **Form checks** (`_save_triage`, `router.py:2368`):
  - `POTENTIAL_NEEDED` when a malfunction is alleged.
  - A death needs a threat answer or a `threat_reason` of at least 10.
  - Owners must be colleagues.
  - `BACKUP_NEEDED` on the FAST track when 2 or more people are active (`triage_rules.py:193`).
  - `ACTION_OR_REASON`.
  - `AWARENESS_NEEDED` when awareness is unresolved or in conflict.
  - A later awareness needs a note of at least 10.
  - An update after completion needs `change_reason` of at least 10.
  - A threat cannot be unticked here (409 `THREAT_NOT_EXCLUDED_HERE`).
- **Downgrade or later awareness** (`:2463-2530`):
  - Outside a trial, this creates a `TRIAGE_DOWNGRADE_APPROVAL` task for the escalation role (default `"LEADER"`, `:2606`), due in `fast_hours`, and returns 409 `SECOND_PERSON_NEEDED`.
  - In a trial, the same person signs with password and code; it is recorded as one person.
  - `decide_downgrade` (`:2828`): the requester cannot approve (403 `SAME_PERSON`). The approver signs APPROVE or REJECT with a reason of at least 10.
- **Threat exclusion** (`exclude_threat`, `:2752`). Reason of at least 10. **The person who marked the threat cannot exclude it outside a trial.** Signed with `decision_type` TRIAGE_THREAT_EXCLUSION. A threat persists until it is excluded this way.
- **Versions.** Every save adds a `triage_record_versions` row (`add_triage_version`, `service.py:2145`; tracked fields `:2121`). Saving also acknowledges.
- **Containment tasks.** HOLD_LOT and FIELD_ACTION open tasks. Harm NOT_KNOWN opens `TRIAGE_HARM_FOLLOW_UP`, due in 1 working day (`router.py:2614`).
- **Auto-reopen** (`reopen_raised`, `service.py:2267`). The triggers (`triage_rules.py:179`) are DEATH, SERIOUS_INJURY, THREAT, FLOOR_RAISED and MORE_HARM. The new due is now + `fast_hours`, plus an urgent alert on death or injury. A closed complaint reopens through `complaint_disposition.resolve.reopen_closed`.
- **Inheritance.** Triage belongs to the event: linked records inherit it (`inherit_triage`, `service.py:2219`).
- **Routine confirm** (`confirm_routine`, `:2643`): only ROUTINE records with a `ROUTINE_PREFILL` (`triage_rules.py:47`), or imported-backlog complaints.
- **Awareness.** A complaint's `awareness_timestamp` is filled when it is null (`router.py:2560`).
- **Database checks:** unique `(tenant_id, subject_kind, subject_id)` and `target_hours` 1-168 (`db/models/intake.py:356-359`).

**Endpoints** (in the intake.router table in 3.1):
- `GET /api/v1/triage`, `GET /api/v1/triage/backlog`
- `POST /api/v1/triage/escalate`
- `POST /api/v1/triage/{kind}/{record_id}/record | confirm | confirm-qms | acknowledge | exclude-threat`
- `GET .../versions`
- `POST /api/v1/triage/downgrades/{task_id}`

**Tables:** `triage_records`, `triage_record_versions`, `signed_approval_records`, `workflow_tasks`, `complaints`, `import_quarantine_rows`, `record_links`, `oversight_settings`, `audit_events`.

**Frontend:** `sections/Triage.tsx` (record and confirm-qms `:413`, acknowledge `:449`, exclude-threat `:583`, versions `:586`, escalate, backlog, confirm), `sections/ShortStory.tsx`, `HybridShell.tsx`, `home.ts`. **No frontend caller was found for `POST /triage/downgrades/{task_id}`.** It may be reached from the My Work task UI (not verified).

**Tests:**
- `tests/test_triage_rules.py`, `test_triage_two_speeds_api.py`, `test_triage_signing_refusals.py`, `test_import_triage_b2.py`, `test_b6a_scoped_watch.py`, `test_b6a_clock_unit.py`, `test_b6a_deadline_list.py`, `test_urgent_alert_b7.py`.
- Frontend `triageForm.test.ts`, `triagePaging.test.tsx`, `triageClose.test.tsx`, `inlineTriage.test.ts`, `importTriage.test.tsx`.

**Limits:**
- `ACK_HOURS=1` is fixed ("make it a setting", `triage_rules.py:29`).

### 3.4 Deadline clocks shown on intake and triage

- **Awareness** (`rules.awareness`, `rules.py:488`): the recorded value, else the earliest candidate (unresolved), else AVAILABLE. For linked records it is the earliest across the group (`service.linked_awareness`, `:403`).
- **Provisional deadlines** (`provisional_deadlines`, `rules.py:622`; table `PROVISIONAL` at `:529`):

| Market | Tiers |
|---|---|
| US | 5 work days / 30 / 30 calendar days; `US_REMEDIAL` 5 work days |
| EU_NI and GB | 2 / 10 / 15 days |
| IN | 15 days in every tier |
| CA | 10 / 10 / 30 days |

  - Until triage is answered, the earlier `earliest_report` applies, shown as "N days unless <ruled out>" (`:689-695`).
  - A signed market conclusion (reportability, another section) replaces the provisional clock.
  - EU IVD labels cite IVDR Art. 82 as a starter text.
  - With the rule register on, the floor in force at awareness is used.
- **Imported backlog.** Items get no market clock, only a backlog target (`service.py:1133-1137`).
- **Delay wording:** `delay_text` (`rules.py:734`).
- **Invariant:** the provisional clocks are labelled provisional. They never replace a signed conclusion.

### 3.5 Duplicates, links and the QMS complaint task

**What the user sees and does.** Opens the Duplicates list or a Case's links, then:
- links or dismisses a pair with a reason;
- undoes a link with a reason;
- for Connect organizations, enters the QMS complaint number, or records why none is needed.

**Rules**
- **Duplicate reasons** (`duplicate_reasons`, `rules.py:774`): same product, plus the same lot or serial, plus event dates within `DUPLICATE_DAYS=2`, plus the same site or country.
- **Similar wording** (`wording_reason`, `:757`; switch `MDARIX_B6_SIMILAR_WORDING`): Jaccard similarity of at least 0.5 within 90 days, scanning `SIMILAR_SCAN=500`. This is a suggestion only. The method is word overlap and misses typos and synonyms (`rules.py:53`).
- **Link** (`decide_link`, `router.py:2028`):
  - Decision LINKED or DISMISSED, reason of at least 10. A pair already decided gives 409 `DECIDED_ALREADY`.
  - Linking to a complaint completes the QMS task as `LINKED_TO_IMPORTED`.
  - `add_link` (`service.py:1706`) records `awareness_before` and `awareness_after`, plus `conflict` when the other record's awareness is older. A conflict reopens triage.
  - Then `inherit_triage` and `reopen_raised` run.
- **Undo** (`undo_link`, `:2151`). Reason of at least 10. Refused with 409 `LINK_HAS_SIGNED_WORK` when a market assessment or disposition was signed since the link (`_signed_since`, `:2125`). The row is kept as UNLINKED, never deleted. Undoing a link that carries signed work is not supported yet (`:2178`).
- **QMS complaint task** (`qms_complaint`, `router.py:1856`; requires `case.log` or `quality.review`):
  - **Path A:** a `qms_ref` is entered. The task becomes WAITING ("Awaiting import confirmation"). `confirm_by_import` (`service.py:1653`) links the records and completes the task as IMPORT_CONFIRMED. A later import also calls it (`backend/app/trial/imports.py:2651`).
  - **Path B:** a `not_needed_reason` (at least 10) is given. This requires `quality.review`, and the person must not be the Case signer outside a trial.
  - For Connect organizations, `ensure_qms_id_task` is due in 5 working days, with decide-by at 10 (`service.py:2033`).
- **Field change history:** `GET /api/v1/intake/changes` lists `record_field_changes`.

**Endpoints:** `GET /api/v1/intake/duplicates`, `POST /api/v1/intake/links`, `POST /api/v1/intake/links/{link_id}/undo`, `GET /api/v1/intake/changes`, `POST /api/v1/intake/{event_id}/qms`. All are in the 3.1 table.

**Tables:** `record_links`, `record_field_changes`, `workflow_tasks`, `triage_records`, `intake_events` (`qms_ref`).

**Frontend:** `FirstContact.tsx` (duplicates, links, undo, qms), `home.ts`, `ImportReports.tsx` (changes), `BackEntry.tsx`.

**Tests:** `tests/test_b6a_similar_wording.py`, `test_b6a_similar_wording_unit.py` (name not verified), `test_b8_similar_table.py`, `test_intake_triage_api.py`.

### 3.6 Case maintenance: void, waiting, contact, corrections, addenda, bulk assign, attachments, exports, import backlog, service-record check

**Void** (`void_case`, `router.py:2975`; `quality.review`)
- Reason of at least 10.
- **The person voiding is not the logger outside a trial.**
- Signed with password and code, `decision_type` CASE_VOID.
- Allowed only from states that may become VOIDED, never ESCALATED or CLOSED.

**Waiting internal** (`wait_internal`, `:3035`; `case.log`)
- Reason of at least 10.
- Returns to PENDING or IN_REVIEW according to the signed outcome, else to OPEN.

**Contact** (`set_contact`, `PUT`, `:3106`)
- Connect organizations (record owner EXTERNAL) get 409 `CONTACT_IN_QMS`.
- A trial refuses a personal name (422 `NO_PERSONAL_NAME`).
- Needs a contact, or a `no_contact_reason` of at least 10.
- Audited by field name only, never by value.
- Readers are `CONTACT_READERS` = SERVICE_DESK, TRIAGE_LEAD, REVIEWER, REGULATORY_REVIEWER, LEADER (`:2971`). Everyone else sees "Contact held".

**Erase contact** (`erase_contact`, `:3172`; reviewers)
- Reason of at least 10.
- Nulls the contact fields and keeps the row. Audited as `CASE_CONTACT_ERASED`.
- The retention job `erase_expired_contacts` (`service.py:2065`) erases contacts of CLOSED Cases older than `CONTACT_KEEP_DAYS=365`, as actor `system:contact-retention`. The 365 days have not been checked against the customer's SOP (`service.py:2059`).
- **No frontend caller was found for set or erase contact.**

**Correct a fact** (`correct_fact`, `:3367`)
- Only `CORRECTABLE` fields (`:3205`), never the reporter's words. Reason of at least 10.
- Refusals: 409 `CASE_VOIDED`, 409 `CASE_CHANGED` (when `seen_version` does not match), 422 `NO_CHANGE`.
- Writes `record_field_changes` with source CORRECTION, bumps the version, and audits the old and new values.
- `_new_information` (`:3250`): on a CLOSED Case, a harm or fault word reopens it to OPEN and calls `reopen_raised`.

**Addenda** (`add_addendum`, `:3305`)
- Kinds come from `ADDENDUM_KINDS` except EMAIL. `said_by` is REPORTER, TECHNICIAN, DISTRIBUTOR or OUR_STAFF.
- Text of at least 10, masked.
- An addendum never edits the original words. A withdrawal is only an addendum: the complaint stands.

**Bulk assign** (`bulk_assign`, `:3262`)
- At most `BULK_MAX=200`.
- The owner must be a colleague.
- Assignment only ("there is no bulk signature"). Audited as `CASE_BULK_ASSIGNED`.

**Attachments** (`intake/case_attachments.py`)
- Adding needs `case.log`. Reading needs log, classify or review.
- Allowed types: PDF, text, CSV, PNG, JPEG. Size limit 10 MB, plus the tenant quota.
- Each file is virus-scanned and its content must match its type. The file name is masked.

**Exports** (`intake/case_export.py`; `data.export`; `MAX_ROWS=20_000`)
- Two exports: the Cases CSV, and the QMS-entry CSV.
- Contacts are masked unless an `unmask_reason` of at least 10 is given, and never unmasked in a trial (`:157`).
- Both are CSV-injection safe and audited (`CASES_EXPORTED` and `QMS_ENTRY_EXPORTED`, with sha256).
- **Mount order matters.** The router is mounted before `intake_router` so that `/intake/cases/export.csv` is not swallowed by `/intake/{event_id}` (`backend/app/main.py:555`).

**Cases list** (`GET /api/v1/intake/cases`, `cases_list.cases_page`, `PAGE_MAX=100`; views at `cases_list.py:25`; fixed query count)
- Imported backlog Cases (`case_import.is_backlog`, `:378`) raise no overdue alarm (`cases_list.py:107`).
- The `needs_information` view searches the identifiers JSON as text (`:91`).

**Case import** (`intake/case_import.py`, driven by `backend/app/trial/imports.py:1441, 2134`)
- Rows become OPEN and unsigned.
- An import never classifies, signs or escalates.
- Contact headers are never read (regex `_CONTACT`, `:107`).
- `MAX_ROWS=5000`. A re-upload fills only empty fields (`FILLABLE`).

**Imported complaints backlog** (`service.imported_backlog`, `:705`; `GET /api/v1/triage/backlog`)
- Covers complaints whose awareness was more than `backlog_days` (default 30) before the import and that were never triaged.
- The target is `working_day_end(import, backlog_target_days)` (default 10). There are no market clocks.
- The backlog is read in one 20k read (`BACKLOG_READ`, `service.py:696`).

**Service-record check** (`check_service_record`, `POST /api/v1/intake/from-complaint/{complaint_id}`, `router.py:1931`; `case.classify`)
- Runs once per complaint.
- Creates an IntakeEvent of kind SERVICE_RECORD (channel SERVICE_RECORD_IMPORT, zone "UTC"), an unanswered check and a link.
- The signed check decides the complaint's second look (3.2).

**Endpoints (`backend.app.intake.case_attachments`, all 3):**

| GET | `/api/v1/intake/{event_id}/attachments` | `list_attachments` |
| POST | `/api/v1/intake/{event_id}/attachments` | `add_attachment` |
| GET | `/api/v1/intake/{event_id}/attachments/{attachment_id}` | `open_attachment` |

**Endpoints (`backend.app.intake.case_export`, all 2):**

| GET | `/api/v1/intake/cases/export.csv` | `export_cases` |
| GET | `/api/v1/intake/qms-entry/export.csv` | `export_for_qms_entry` |

**Tables:** `external_parties`, `intake_events`, `signed_approval_records`, `record_field_changes`, `case_addenda`, `case_attachments`.

**Frontend:** `FirstContact.tsx` (void, waiting, correct, addenda, attachments), `CasesList.tsx` (bulk assign, both exports), `sections/Complaints.tsx` and `OneStory.tsx` (service-record check).

**Tests:**
- `tests/test_case_addenda_api.py`, `test_case_lifecycle_api.py`, `test_case_permissions_api.py`, `test_case_export_b7.py`, `test_case_attachments.py`, `test_case_screens_api.py`, `test_case_load_measure.py`, `test_b6a_query_budget.py`, `test_case_import_b7.py`, `test_pv1_complaint_imports.py`, `test_import_real_exports_unit.py`, `test_b8_export_personal_data.py` (relevance not verified).

### 3.7 Mail-in: complaints arriving by e-mail (`backend/app/mail_in/`)

**What the user sees and does**
1. A Customer Admin switches on the organization's intake address and can rotate it (`customer-admin/sections/EmailIntake.tsx`).
2. Messages arrive in *E-mail to check* (`sections/EmailToCheck.tsx`). For each message a person chooses one of:
   - *Make it a case*: `POST /api/v1/intake` with `inbound_message_id`;
   - *Add to case*: becomes an EMAIL addendum;
   - *Not a case*: with a reason.
3. Reading the raw message is restricted and audited.

**Rules**
- **Switch.** Off unless `MDARIX_EMAIL_INTAKE=on` (`mail_in/__init__.py:17`). While off, the routes answer 404 and the receiver exits.
- **Address.** `cases-<12 random chars>@intake.<domain>` (`addresses.py:25`). States ACTIVE, OFF, ROTATED (still accepted for 30 days) and RETIRED.
- **Receiver** (`receiver.py`):
  - Unknown recipients get 550.
  - Limits: 25 MB per message, 30 messages per IP per hour, and a daily limit (default 200, then 451).
  - There is a sender allowlist.
  - The rate limit is per process and in memory (`receiver.py:155`). A lost job leaves a message "being checked" (`:144`).
- **Processing** (`processing.py`) is rule-based, with **no AI**:
  - Auto-replies go to NOT_A_CASE.
  - Messages thread by Case reference (CALL/URG/SVC-xxxxxx).
  - The duplicate window is 7 days.
  - At most 10 attachments, each scanned.
- **Add to case** (`add_to_case`, `router.py:202`; `case.log`) writes an EMAIL addendum, then `_new_information`.
- **Not a case** (`not_a_case`, `:249`):
  - The reason comes from `NOT_A_CASE_REASONS`; OTHER needs a note of at least 10.
  - A message with harm or fault words needs `quality.review` (403 `QUALITY_REVIEWER_NEEDED`).
  - **Whoever opened the message cannot dismiss it outside a trial.**
  - `purge_after` is 30 days. `purge_expired` (`:325`) keeps the row as PURGED.
- **Raw message** (`:294`): `CONTACT_READERS` only, audited.
- **Settings and rotate:** Customer Admin only. A trial gets 409 `TRIAL_NO_ADDRESS`.

**Endpoints (`backend.app.mail_in.router`, all 7):**
| Method | Path | Function |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/v1/email-intake/messages` | `list_messages` |
| POST | `/api/v1/email-intake/messages/{message_id}/add-to-case` | `add_to_case` |
| POST | `/api/v1/email-intake/messages/{message_id}/not-a-case` | `not_a_case` |
| GET | `/api/v1/email-intake/messages/{message_id}/raw` | `raw_message` |
| GET | `/api/v1/email-intake/settings` | `get_settings` |
| PUT | `/api/v1/email-intake/settings` | `put_settings` |
| POST | `/api/v1/email-intake/settings/rotate` | `rotate_address` |

**Tables:** `inbound_messages`, `case_attachments`, `case_addenda`, `oversight_settings`, and `inbound_provider_mappings` (control plane).

**Tests:**
- `tests/test_email_intake_api.py`, `test_mail_in.py`, `test_mail_processing.py`, `test_email_no_send.py`.
- e2e `frontend/e2e/email-to-case.spec.ts`; unit `emailIntake.test.tsx`.

**Invariants:**
- Received time is the server receipt time.
- No AI in processing.
- No silent delete.
- Mail-in stays off unless switched on.

### 3.8 Communications log (`backend/app/communications/`)

**What the user sees and does.** Records every exchange with a reporter, distributor, hospital or service partner. Requests for information carry an owner and a due date. The user logs attempts, records the reply, or closes the request. Parties and settings are maintained here.

**Rules** (`communications/rules.py`)
- **Parties:** REPORTER, DISTRIBUTOR, HOSPITAL, SERVICE_PARTNER.
- **Statuses:** OPEN, ANSWERED, UNRESOLVED, CLOSED.
- **Requests.** A SENT information request needs an owner and a due date. A RECEIVED request is refused (`:99`).
- **Attempts.** Default 3, range 1-10. After the limit, the request moves from the PRIMARY contact to SECOND, then to UNRESOLVED (`after_attempts`, `:69`).
- **Timeliness.** "Done in MDARIX" means logged within 1 working hour on the organization calendar (`:92`).
- **MDARIX never sends** (`NEVER_SENDS`, `:50`).
- **Reply** (`router.py:770`). The reply time must be after the request and before now. The request becomes ANSWERED.
- **Close** (`:817`). CLOSED or UNRESOLVED, with a note of at least 10. UNRESOLVED needs at least one attempt.
- **Who may write:** `signal.triage`, `investigation.work`, `evidence.request`, `quality.review`, `regulatory.review` or `regulatory.approve`. Settings need `quality.review` or `regulatory.approve`.
- **Intake hooks.** The intake follow-up calls `ensure_intake_request`; closing the follow-up calls `close_for_intake` (`service.py:375, 428`). The task type is `EXTERNAL_REPLY_DUE`.

| GET | `/api/v1/communications/parties` | `parties` |
| POST | `/api/v1/communications/parties` | `create_party` |
| PUT | `/api/v1/communications/parties/{party_id}` | `update_party` |
| GET | `/api/v1/communications/settings` | `settings` |
| PUT | `/api/v1/communications/settings` | `save_settings` |
| GET | `/api/v1/communications/{entry_id}` | `entry_detail` |
| PUT | `/api/v1/communications/{entry_id}` | `update_entry` |
| POST | `/api/v1/communications/{entry_id}/attempts` | `attempt` |
| POST | `/api/v1/communications/{entry_id}/close` | `close` |
| POST | `/api/v1/communications/{entry_id}/reply` | `reply` |

**Tables:** `communication_entries`, `external_parties`, `communication_settings`, `workflow_tasks`. Migration: `tr06guidescomms`.

**Frontend:** `workspace/communications.ts`, `sections/Communications.tsx`, `ShortStory.tsx`.

**Tests:** `tests/test_communications_api.py`, `test_stage3_communication.py`; frontend `communications.test.tsx`.

**Not in this flow.** `backend.app.communication_router` (provider health, Resend webhook; 2 endpoints) is the outbound e-mail provider. It is listed in the checklist as owned by the platform/notifications section (not verified).

### 3.9 Complaints list, second look (hidden complaints), backlog, classification

**What the user sees and does**
- The Complaints list has search, facets and paging.
- *Second look* lists records that may be hidden complaints, most serious first, each with why it was flagged. A person decides CONFIRMED (it is a complaint) or DISMISSED, with a reason. An optional AI explanation is available.
- *Backlog* lists open complaints in no signal and no case, against the organization's target days.
- The coding of the use environment and user type is confirmed inside the disposition signature (3.10).

**Rules**
- **Flags** (`complaint_review/rules.py` `flag`, `:271`):
  - CANCELLED or INQUIRY records need a complaint sign.
  - No fault found or user error recurring at least 3 times in 365 days counts by itself.
  - CLOSED_NO_DECISION means closed with death or injury and no market conclusion.
  - Score weights are at `:149-158`.
- **Backlog states** (`:340`): PAST_TARGET, DUE_SOON (fewer than 7 days left), ON_TRACK. The default target is 30 days, range 1-365.
- **Decide** (`decide`, `router.py:146`):
  - Needs `signal.triage`, `investigation.create` or `quality.review`.
  - Decision CONFIRMED or DISMISSED, reason of at least 10.
  - A revision replaces the row, with old and new values audited.
  - **The complaint itself is never changed.**
  - No password re-check.
- **Settings** (`:238/:247`, `signal.triage`) are versioned and need a reason of at least 10.
- **AI explanation** (`complaint_review/explain.py`; `ai.run`):
  - At most 3 sentences, each quoting record fields verbatim. Sentences asserting complaint, reportability or cause are dropped.
  - Saved through `record_ai_execution` (`ai_executions`, VALIDATED or REJECTED) and shown with author "AI".
  - **It never changes the record or the decision** (`explain.py:11`).
- **Classification** (`backend/app/complaint_classification/rules.py`, 84 lines, **no router**):
  - Fixed lists `USE_ENVIRONMENT_LABELS` and `USER_TYPE_LABELS`. OTHER needs text (`TEXT_MAX=200`).
  - `code_source_value` (`:58`) keeps an unknown source value as UNKNOWN plus the raw text: "nothing is inferred from free text".
  - `CODING_CRITERIA_VERSION` is "coding-starter-1".
  - A classification never sets deadlines, triggers or reportability (`rules.py:7`).
  - It is confirmed inside the disposition signature (`complaint_disposition/router.py:390` `_classification_problem`). Changing a CONFIRMED classification needs a reason of at least 10. A new row supersedes the old one (`:656, :761`).

**Endpoints (`backend.app.complaints_router`, all 1):**
| Method | Path | Function |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/v1/complaints` | `list_complaints` |

**Endpoints (`backend.app.complaint_review.router`, all 6):**
| Method | Path | Function |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/v1/complaints/backlog` | `backlog` |
| GET | `/api/v1/complaints/review-settings` | `review_settings` |
| PUT | `/api/v1/complaints/review-settings` | `update_review_settings` |
| GET | `/api/v1/complaints/second-look` | `second_look` |
| POST | `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/second-look` | `decide` |
| POST | `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/second-look/explain` | `explain_second_look` |

**Tables:** `complaints`, `complaint_second_looks`, `complaint_settings`, `ai_executions`, `complaint_classifications`. Migrations: `md40complaintreview`, `tr01marketassessments`.

**Frontend:** `workspace/complaintList.ts`, `ComplaintList.tsx`, `workspace/complaintReview.ts`, `sections/Complaints.tsx`, `HybridShell.tsx`, `home.ts`.

**Tests:** `tests/test_complaint_list.py`, `test_complaint_page.py`, `test_complaint_review_api.py`, `test_complaint_review_rules.py`, `test_second_look_explain_unit.py`; frontend `complaintReview.test.ts`, `complaints.test.tsx`.

### 3.10 Complaint disposition, resolve-and-close and reopen (`backend/app/complaint_disposition/`)

**What the user sees and does**
1. Opens a complaint's disposition screen.
2. Chooses a branch, answering each question with its rationale.
3. Records the service record when relevant, and the attribution.
4. Confirms the coding.
5. Signs with password and code.
   - On the routine path, *Resolve and close* does the disposition and the ready-to-close record under one password check.
   - A closed complaint can be reopened with a reason.

**Rules**
- **Branches**, in this order (`rules.py:26`): ESCALATED_BY_TRIGGER, KNOWN_ISSUE, SERVICE_LEVEL, ESCALATED_BY_CHOICE.
  - A met trigger forces ESCALATED_BY_TRIGGER (`branch_problem`, `:281`).
  - KNOWN_ISSUE must name a matching open case.
  - The rationale needs at least 10.
  - Choosing another branch when candidates exist needs a `not_known_reason` of at least 10.
- **Service record** (`:208`):
  - troubleshooting text of at least 10;
  - a correction type from the list;
  - correction text of at least 10;
  - result of at least 5;
  - a ticket is required for PART_REPLACED or SETTING_CORRECTED.
  - The no-investigation reason comes from the list; OTHER needs at least 20.
  - WITHDREW is refused while the complaint is on the watch.
- **Attribution.** NO_FAULT_FOUND forces attribution UNDETERMINED (`:240`). UNDETERMINED needs a reason of at least 10.
- **Sign** (`sign`, `router.py:433`):
  - 422 `TRIAGE_FIRST` while a flagged triage is open (`_triage_first`, `:378`).
  - 409 `TRIGGER_MET_SINCE_OPENED` when the triggers changed since the screen was read (`seen_triggers`).
  - Escalation needs a product.
  - A re-sign needs `change_reason` of at least 10. A concurrent signature gives 409 `SIGNED_MEANWHILE`.
  - Administrators are refused.
  - **Signer training is required** (409 `TRAINING_REQUIRED`; `backend/app/signer_training`).
  - Then `verify_signing_credentials` (`:571`).
  - Escalation calls `open_case`. KNOWN_ISSUE writes a new scope version on the named case (`:596-629`).
  - The new `ComplaintDisposition` row supersedes the old one (`:814`). `hands_on_seconds` is recorded (routine target 600 s). The commit goes through `signing_commit` (`:819`).
  - "A signed disposition is never edited" (`rules.py:143`).
- **Resolve and close** (`resolve.py:295`; switch `MDARIX_ROUTINE_PATH`):
  - Always the SERVICE_LEVEL branch, through `sign` with `before_sign` and `before_commit` hooks.
  - A problem code is required unless the record owner is EXTERNAL.
  - `customer_told` is required; NOT_POSSIBLE needs a reason of at least 10.
  - Taking over from another owner needs `take_over` (409 `OWNER_TAKE_OVER`).
  - Any blocked checklist item gives 409 `STILL_OPEN`. **It never signs over open items.**
  - One password check writes two signed records: the disposition and ready-to-close (`:434`).
  - In Complete mode the complaint is closed (status "closed"). In Connect mode it is only marked READY.
  - A RESPONSE communication entry is logged for each reporter.
- **Reopen** (`resolve.py:530`):
  - Reason of at least `REOPEN_MIN=20`, no signature.
  - Complete mode only (409 `NOT_CLOSED` otherwise).
  - The closure is superseded. Triage auto-reopen also calls this (3.3).

**Endpoints (`backend.app.complaint_disposition.router`, all 4):**
| Method | Path | Function |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/v1/complaints/dispositions` | `dispositions` |
| GET | `/api/v1/complaints/dispositions/trend` | `disposition_trend` |
| GET | `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/disposition` | `disposition_screen` |
| POST | `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/disposition/sign` | `sign_disposition` |

**Endpoints (`backend.app.complaint_disposition.resolve`, all 3):**
| Method | Path | Function |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/reopen` | `reopen` |
| GET | `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/resolve-and-close` | `resolve_screen` |
| POST | `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/resolve-and-close` | `resolve_and_close` |

**Tables:** `complaint_dispositions`, `complaint_classifications`, `complaint_market_assessments` (routine "not reportable" conclusions), `event_closures`, `complaints`, `communication_entries`, `signed_approval_records`, `investigations` (through `open_case`). Migrations: `tr02complaintdispositions`, `tr07oversight` (event closure), `tr25routinepath`.

**Frontend:** `workspace/disposition.ts`, `sections/Dispositions.tsx`, `workspace/resolveClose.ts`, `sections/ResolveClose.tsx`.

**Tests:**
- `tests/test_complaint_disposition_api.py`, `test_complaint_disposition_rules.py`, `test_routine_rules_b7.py`, `test_resolve_and_close.py`.
- e2e `frontend/e2e/resolve-and-close.spec.ts`; unit `disposition.test.tsx`, `resolveClose.test.tsx`.

### 3.11 Signals: detection, triage and opening a case (`backend/app/signals/`)

**What the user sees and does**
1. **Detection rule.** Views the rule, edits `enabled`, `min_complaints` and `window_days` with a reason, or runs detection now. Detection also runs:
   - after imports (`signals/hooks.py:54, 64`);
   - from the scheduler (`detection.py:282` `run_for_all_tenants`).
2. **Inbox.** Filters, overdue and review-due lists.
3. Opens a signal and chooses *Start triage*, which makes them the owner.
4. Confirms the priority, with a reason if it differs from the suggestion.
5. Optionally adds complaints or merges other signals.
6. Records a disposition: MONITOR, NO_ACTION, DUPLICATE, INVESTIGATION_REQUIRED or ESCALATED.
7. Reads the rules-only insight. Optionally asks for an AI summary.
8. Opens a case: *Create investigation* after INVESTIGATION_REQUIRED or ESCALATED, or one-click *Open case* from NEW, TRIAGE or MONITOR.
9. **Trend check.** A statistical check that never raises a signal.

**States and transitions**
- **Statuses** (`db/models/signals.py:52-65`): NEW, TRIAGE, MONITOR, NO_ACTION, DUPLICATE, MERGED, INVESTIGATION_REQUIRED, ESCALATED, INVESTIGATION_OPEN, DECISION_RECORDED, CLOSED.
  - Open statuses (`:66`): NEW, TRIAGE, MONITOR, INVESTIGATION_REQUIRED, ESCALATED, INVESTIGATION_OPEN.
  - Awaiting triage: NEW and TRIAGE (`labels.py:37`).
- **Start** moves NEW to TRIAGE only (`router.py:549-550`).
- **Disposition** (`check_disposition`, `logic.py:286-296`; this is the single gate):
  - from NEW, TRIAGE or MONITOR to any disposition;
  - from ESCALATED only to INVESTIGATION_REQUIRED;
  - anything else gives 409 `SIGNAL_STATE_CONFLICT`.
  - DUPLICATE needs a canonical signal that is not DUPLICATE or MERGED and not itself (`:297-307`).
  - MONITOR needs a review date strictly in the future (UTC date) and a trigger (`:308-314`).
  - ESCALATED forces `confirmed_priority = "CRITICAL"` (`router.py:663-666`).
- **Merge.** Sources become MERGED and point to the canonical signal. Refused when:
  - the source is not open;
  - the source has a case (`SIGNAL_HAS_INVESTIGATION`);
  - it is another product;
  - it is another version when the canonical signal has a version (`router.py:709-741`).
- **Case-driven moves** (`hooks.py:177-247`):
  - A signed decision on a linked case moves INVESTIGATION_OPEN to DECISION_RECORDED.
  - Closing the last linked case moves the signal to CLOSED.
  - Reopening a case moves the signal back to INVESTIGATION_OPEN.
- **Nothing is deleted.** DUPLICATE and MERGED signals are kept and linked (`router.py:621-623, 703-705`). Titles are never rewritten (`logic.py:187-197`).

**Rules**
- **Grouping key** is (product, version, problem key). `problem_key` is the problem code, else the complaint type, else "UNSPECIFIED", uppercased (`logic.py:47-54`). Complaints without a product are skipped.
- **Plan** (`plan_detection`, `logic.py:244-267`):
  - New complaints of a group that already has an open signal are linked to it.
  - Otherwise, at least `min_complaints` new events create a signal.
  - Complaints a person linked as one event count once.
- **Defaults** `{"min_complaints": 3, "window_days": 30}` (`labels.py:84`). Validation: `min_complaints` 2-1000, `window_days` 1-365, reason of at least 10 (`router.py:454-458`).
- **Rule versions.** Each rule update bumps `version`, and existing signals keep the version that raised them. An unchanged update gives 422 `NOTHING_TO_CHANGE`.
- **Only one rule exists:** `COMPLAINT_THRESHOLD`. Any other code gives 404.
- **Detection** (`detection.py:34-36, 109-157`) is idempotent, does not commit, and is serialized per organization with `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(...))`.
  - Future-dated complaints are not counted until their day.
  - A MONITOR signal returns to TRIAGE on new complaints or on its review date (`_monitor_reviews`, `:256`).
- **Priority suggestion** (`logic.py:133-143`):
  - death gives CRITICAL;
  - serious injury or high severity gives HIGH;
  - a count of at least 2x the threshold gives MEDIUM;
  - otherwise LOW.
- **Due date** = `created_at` + `DUE_DAYS` {CRITICAL 1, HIGH 3, MEDIUM 5, LOW 10} days, in UTC (`labels.py:47`; `logic.py:151-153`). It is recomputed on a priority change while the signal awaits triage. Overdue means awaiting triage and `due_at < now`. **These are not working-calendar days.**
- **Reasons.** Disposition, merge and add-complaints need at least 10 (`router.py:373-377, 415, 428, 438`). A manual signal title needs at least 5.
- **AI summary** (`insight.py:734-846`; `ai.run`):
  - Keeps only sentences that cite known complaints of this signal and contain no causal or decision wording.
  - Recorded with `record_ai_execution` and returned as `NOT_REVIEWED`, labelled "AI interpretation ... a person decides".
  - On failure it falls back to the rules-only `records_summary`.
- **Trend check** (`trend_rules.py`):
  - Poisson tail test with `RECENT_DAYS=30`, `HISTORY_DAYS=180`, `MIN_HISTORY_DAYS=60`, `MIN_COMPLAINTS=3`, `SIGNIFICANT_P=0.01`, `ELEVATED_P=0.05`, and a Benjamini-Hochberg adjustment (`:291`).
  - "It is a check, never a verdict, and never raises a signal" (`:15`).
  - **This is where "observed vs expected" lives:** the Home trend card and `TrendCheck.tsx` draw observed, expected and version-before bars.
- **Open a case** (`router.py:855-1088`):
  - Allowed only when the status is in `CASE_FROM` (INVESTIGATION_REQUIRED, ESCALATED, INVESTIGATION_OPEN) and the signal has a product.
  - Extra signals must be the same product and open.
  - The assignee must be an active colleague with `records.write` and not a platform role.
  - The question needs at least 10 characters.
  - One-click first records the INVESTIGATION_REQUIRED disposition (`one_click: True`, default reason `ONE_CLICK_REASON`).
  - The case inherits the signal's priority.
  - Each signal moves to INVESTIGATION_OPEN with an `investigation_signals` row. The triage tasks finish with outcome INVESTIGATION_OPENED.
  - **Triage and case opening commit together** (`:1056`); hooks run afterwards.

### 3.12 The investigation ("case"): opening, managing, workflow states, scope, readiness

**What the user sees and does**
1. **Open.** A case is opened from a signal (3.11), from a complaint disposition (3.10), or directly with `POST /api/v1/investigations`: product, question, complaints, `assign_to_me`, `opened_from`.
2. **Manage** with `PATCH /api/v1/investigations/{id}` and a reason: edit the question, assign or unassign, set priority or due date, close or reopen.
3. **Transition** with a reason: start, hold, cancel, or take a cancelled case up again.
4. **Scope.** Edit the scope; every save is a new version, and a change reason is required after v1. View the version history.
5. **Readiness.** Shows the steps and the checks a decision depends on.
6. **My cases** lists the person's open cases, most urgent first.

**States and transitions**
- **States** (`db/models/case_scope.py:41-50`): OPEN, IN_PROGRESS, ON_HOLD, BRIEF_IN_REVIEW, READY_FOR_DECISION, DECIDED, CLOSED, CANCELLED.
  - `CLOSED_STATES` = CLOSED, CANCELLED.
  - `RESERVED_STATES` = BRIEF_IN_REVIEW, READY_FOR_DECISION, DECIDED (`case_workflow/states.py:36-38`). **These are set only by the brief and decision flows, never by hand.**
- **Manual transitions** (`TRANSITIONS`, `states.py:40-45`):

| From | To |
|---|---|
| OPEN | IN_PROGRESS, ON_HOLD, CANCELLED |
| IN_PROGRESS | ON_HOLD, CANCELLED |
| ON_HOLD | IN_PROGRESS, CANCELLED |
| CANCELLED | IN_PROGRESS |

  - IN_PROGRESS and ON_HOLD need `investigation.work`. CANCELLED needs `investigation.assign` (`:47-51`).
  - `transition_refusal` (`:107-124`) refuses reserved states, CLOSED ("Close an investigation from its management"), the same state, and any move not listed, with 409 `INVALID_TRANSITION`.
  - Cancel is refused while a legacy decision is REQUIRES_REVIEW (`router.py:248-263`).
- **Automatic transitions:**

| Event | Case state |
|---|---|
| Brief submit | BRIEF_IN_REVIEW |
| Brief accepted | READY_FOR_DECISION |
| Brief returned | IN_PROGRESS |
| Concluding decision signed | DECIDED |
| REJECT decision | IN_PROGRESS |
| Material impact | IN_PROGRESS |
| Close (PATCH) | CLOSED |
| Reopen (PATCH) | IN_PROGRESS |
| Claiming or accepting the INVESTIGATION task, or the first scope save | OPEN -> IN_PROGRESS (`states.py:208-232`, `router.py:475-481`) |

- **Displayed state.** `effective_state` (`states.py:72-80`): a closed legacy `status` always shows CLOSED or CANCELLED.
- **After a state change** (`after_state_change`, `:235-263`): cancel cancels open tasks (CASE_CANCELLED) and closes signals; un-cancel recreates the task and reopens signals.

**Rules**
- **Open** (`investigation_open_router.py:96-221` `open_case`):
  - Complaints must all belong to the product (422 `COMPLAINT_NOT_FOR_PRODUCT`).
  - The identifier is `INV-YYYYMMDD-XXXXXX` and unique (409 `INVESTIGATION_EXISTS`).
  - The state is IN_PROGRESS if assigned to the opener, else OPEN.
  - Priority defaults to MEDIUM. `due_at = due_at_for(priority, now)` with `DUE_DAYS` {CRITICAL 15, HIGH 30, MEDIUM 60, LOW 90} days in UTC (`states.py:57, 97-99`).
  - Writes scope version 1 and the audit event `INVESTIGATION_OPENED`.
- **Manage** (`:278-459`):
  - Reason of at least 10.
  - A closed case can only be reopened (409 `INVESTIGATION_CLOSED`).
  - Close is refused while a decision is REQUIRES_REVIEW (409 `DECISIONS_AWAITING_REVIEW`) and when the closure criteria are unmet (409 `CLOSURE_CRITERIA_NOT_MET`, `refuse_unmet_closure`, 3.24).
  - A question edit is a new scope version.
  - A naive `due_at` is taken as UTC (`:329`).
  - **Changing priority does not recompute `due_at`** (`:325-329`).
  - Assignees must be ACTIVE, not a platform role, and hold `records.write` (`:240-241, 331-345`).
  - Hooks after commit: assignment notification, `on_investigation_managed`, signal status, `on_case_closed` (follow-ups outlive the case).
- **Scope** (`case_workflow/scope.py`, `router.py:402`):
  - "The latest version is the current scope; older versions are never changed".
  - `write_version` uses max+1 under the case row lock.
  - After v1, a `change_reason` is required (422 `CHANGE_REASON_REQUIRED`).
  - Excluded-complaint reason at least 5; question at least 10.
  - A complaint cannot be both included and excluded. `date_from` must be <= `date_to`.
  - `reviews_must_restart=True` supersedes the case reviews.
  - `save_scope` carries `lot_impact` and `excluded_lots` into every later version (`router.py:435-438`).
- **Readiness** (`case_workflow/readiness.py:119, 672`) is read-only and defensive: an unreadable fact shows "Could not be checked right now".
  - `STEPS` (`:25-35`) and `CHECKS` (`:36-48`): `scope_complete`, `evidence_reviewed`, `no_blocking_request`, ..., `risk_review`, `regulatory_review`, `brief_ready`, `decision`.
- **My cases:** `DUE_SOON_DAYS=7`; overdue means `due_at < now` (UTC).

| POST | `/api/v1/investigations` | `open_investigation` |
| PATCH | `/api/v1/investigations/{investigation_id}` | `manage_investigation` |

**Invariants:**
- Change state only through `states.set_state`, so `status` and `workflow_state` never drift.
- Never set reserved states by hand.
- Scope rows are append-only.

### 3.13 Work tasks (My Work) and approvals (`backend/app/workflow/`)

**What the user sees and does.** My Work has these views: assigned, queue, waiting, returned, due_soon, overdue, completed, created. A person can:
- claim a queue task;
- accept, decline, release or complete a task;
- reassign a task, if they are an assigner.

**States and transitions**
- **Statuses** (`db/models/workflow.py:46-58`): NEW, ASSIGNED, ACCEPTED, IN_PROGRESS, WAITING, COMPLETED, DECLINED, ESCALATED, CANCELLED, SUPERSEDED.
  - Open = all except COMPLETED, CANCELLED and SUPERSEDED.
  - Queue = NEW, DECLINED, ESCALATED (`tasks.py:30-31`).
  - Working = ASSIGNED, ACCEPTED, IN_PROGRESS (`router.py:51`).
- **Transitions:**

| Action | Move | Who and conditions |
|---|---|---|
| accept | ASSIGNED -> ACCEPTED | Assignee only |
| decline | Working -> NEW (role queue), else DECLINED | Assignee |
| claim | Queue -> ACCEPTED | Enforces `_own_decision` (403) |
| release | Working -> NEW | Needs a role queue |
| reassign | Open -> ASSIGNED or NEW | Needs `investigation.assign`; the person must pass `may_hold_tasks` |
| complete | Working or WAITING -> COMPLETED | Assignee only; only `MANUAL_COMPLETION_TYPES` (FOLLOW_UP, IMPACT_ASSESSMENT, EVIDENCE_REVIEW, COMPLAINT_STEP, `labels.py:71`); other types give 409 `TASK_COMPLETES_AUTOMATICALLY` |

- An IMPACT_ASSESSMENT completed through My Work is refused (409 `USE_IMPACT_ASSESSMENT`).
- Decline, release and reassign need a reason of at least 10 (`router.py:549-557`).
- **Clocks.** `DUE_SOON_DAYS=3`; overdue means `due_at < now` (UTC). The INVESTIGATION task has no `due_at` (`hooks.py:59-78`). The SIGNAL_TRIAGE task copies the signal's due date.
- **Case owner sync.** Taking, handing over, releasing or declining an INVESTIGATION task sets or clears `investigations.owner_ref` (`router.py:611-654`).
- **Approvals** (`approvals.py`):
  - `ask` gives each eligible approver a REGULATORY_REVIEW task.
  - In a trial with one person, the requester gets the task. In a pilot with nobody eligible, the task goes to the role queue.
  - Approvers are never administrators.
- **Idempotency.** Task creation is idempotent per (type, source, assignee) (`tasks.py:154-179`). Backfill is idempotent.

| GET | `/api/v1/tasks/{task_id}` | `get_task` |
| POST | `/api/v1/tasks/{task_id}/accept` | `accept_task` |
| POST | `/api/v1/tasks/{task_id}/claim` | `claim_task` |
| POST | `/api/v1/tasks/{task_id}/complete` | `complete_task` |
| POST | `/api/v1/tasks/{task_id}/decline` | `decline_task` |
| POST | `/api/v1/tasks/{task_id}/reassign` | `reassign_task` |
| POST | `/api/v1/tasks/{task_id}/release` | `release_task` |

**Tests:**
- `tests/test_workflow_tasks.py`, `test_workflow_tasks_unit.py`, `test_workflow_roles.py`, `test_approval_told_api.py`, `test_business_permissions.py`.
- Frontend `tasks.test.ts`, `sections/MyWork.test.ts`; e2e `one-navigation.spec.ts`.

**Invariants:**
- Every change locks the task row and writes a history row plus an audit event.
- Keep `_own_decision` on every path that hands a person an approval task. **It is not checked on reassign or accept** (3.31).

### 3.14 The six-step investigation cockpit (frontend)

**Where it lives**
- `frontend/src/features/workspace/caseWorkflow.ts:454-574` defines the steps, the questions, the step mapping, `caseProgress()` and `nextCaseStep()`.
- `sections/Cockpit.tsx` draws the summary strip and the rail: `cockpitFigures` `:41-60`, `Cockpit` `:91-179`, `CockpitRail` `:182-220`, `Fold` `:231-254`.
- `sections/CaseSections.tsx:157` `CaseSections` renders the step bodies. A `Fold` loads its content only when opened.

**The six steps** (`CASE_SECTIONS`, `STEP_QUESTION`):

| Step | Question shown | What it contains (component -> endpoint) |
|---|---|---|
| Signal | "What happened?" | `CaseScopePanel` -> `/investigations/{id}/scope` (3.12) |
| Product reality | "What changed in the product?" | `VersionCompareCard` (`sections/VersionCompare`; uses the Product 360 read, 3.15); `LotImpactPanel` -> `/lot-impact`, **trials only** (`CaseSections.tsx:341`) |
| Evidence | "What do we know, and what not?" | Note "Every statement shows its kind and its author. AI suggestions are never facts." (`:368`); `OverviewView` (intelligence lists, RCA event versions); `CaseEvidencePanel` -> evidence inventory, links, review, provenance, `GET /api/v1/evidence?limit=200` picker; Request evidence drawer -> evidence requests; `UnknownsView`; `ContradictionsView`; Ask (not covered here) |
| Possible causes | "What could have caused it?" | `AiProposalsCard` (AI items waiting for a person); `HypothesesView`; folds: Fishbone, 5 Whys, Failure chain, Observations, Challenge, History; last fold `InvestigationWorkspacePanel` (legacy AI tabs, 3.20) |
| Brief | "What should we decide?" | `DecisionBoard`; `CaseReviewsPanel` -> `/reviews`; `CaseDecisionBriefPanel` -> `/decision-brief` |
| Decision & action | "What was decided, and did it work?" | `DecisionSummaryView`, `CaseDecisionPanel` -> `/decision`; `DidItWorkView` -> `/did-it-work`; `ComplaintNextStep`; fold "The record": record download, timeline, integrity, AI runs (case audit) |

**Rules shown in the UI**
- The strip shows Evidence as "N for · N against · N missing" and Possible causes as "N, M leading".
- "Leading" means a human status of STRONGLY_SUPPORTED, "never a concluded root cause" (`Cockpit.tsx:134`).
- Product reality always counts as "READ": it records nothing itself.
- Once `human_review` is DONE, every step counts as done (`caseWorkflow.ts:553-563`).
- The AI "analysis" step is never offered as the next step (`:566-574`).
- The challenge fold hides when `MDARIX_CHALLENGE` is off. Fishbone and 5 Whys hide when the RCA methods are off (`CaseSections.tsx:306-308`).
- `CaseAiRunCard` (`sections/CaseAiRun.tsx`) is mounted in `Investigations.tsx:49`, not inside `CaseSections`.

**Old addresses.** Old section and part addresses keep resolving through `cockpitStep()` and `linkTable.json` "newHomes" (`CaseSections.tsx:154`). For example, Summary goes to Signal and History goes to Decision & action, part history.

**Tests:**
- Frontend `cockpit.test.tsx` (checks the six rail names, `:101`), `caseWorkflow.test.ts` (`:273-274`), `ux3Integration.test.tsx`, `w1bcInvestigation.test.tsx`, `linkTable.test.ts:484-488`, `whyAsked.test.tsx`, `nextStep.test.tsx`.
- e2e `golden-path.spec.ts`, `persona-journeys.spec.ts`, `flagship-journey.spec.ts`, `app-wide-layout.spec.ts`. The helper `frontend/e2e/support/story.ts` has `STEPS` at L21 and `openStep` at L114.

**Invariants:**
- Exactly these six names, in this order.
- Old links keep working.
- Product reality writes nothing except lot impact.

### 3.15 Product reality: Product 360, Product Lifecycle Twin, change impact, field scope, lot impact

**What the user sees and does**
1. From the product list, opens *Product 360* in one of three views: current, as at the event, or as known at a date. Link tiles lead to related records.
2. The *Lifecycle Twin* of a version shows its versions, changes, lots, components and suppliers.
3. *Change impact* works for a recorded change (`?change=`), or as a preview by component or supplier.
4. *Field-action scope* is computed for a factor: COMPONENT_LOT, SUPPLIER or PRODUCT_VERSION. It can be exported as CSV.
5. Inside a case (trials only), *Lot impact*:
   - shows the suggested factor and previews others;
   - saves the factor and the lots left out, each with a reason;
   - signs the list with password, code and the list fingerprint;
   - downloads it as CSV or PDF, either the signed list or `?list=current`.

**Rules**
- **The Twin is trial-only and read-only.** `_trial_only` gives 404 `NOT_A_TRIAL_WORKSPACE` (`product360/twin_router.py:37-43`).
  - The CSV export needs `data.export` and is audited as `FIELD_ACTION_SCOPE_EXPORTED` with the fingerprint (`:104-136`).
  - Field scope reuses `lot_impact.service.build` "with its fingerprint unchanged". Units by market and by site are added beside the fingerprinted material, never inside it (`twin.py:12-14`).
  - "MDARIX lists; it recommends no recall ... decides no notification" (`twin.py:16`).
  - The limits are listed to the user in `CANNOT_TELL` (`twin.py:48-58`):
    - one genealogy level;
    - first consignee only;
    - "in stock" means released minus shipped;
    - at most `LOTS_MAX=2000` lots.
- **Expected vs observed.** No code in `product360/` or the twin screens computes "expected vs observed". That comparison is the trend check (3.11): `Home.tsx:241`, `OneStory.tsx:267`, `TrendCheck.tsx:324`. What the twin shows is versions, changes and lots as recorded (not verified further).
- **Product 360 audit.** Each Product 360 retrieval is audited as `PRODUCT360_TEMPORAL_RETRIEVAL` (`main.py:787-808`).
- **Supplier lots** are trial-only (`supplier_router.py:16-18`).
- **Graph.** The graph endpoints use `graph/service.py` `RealityGraphService`, with `MAX_DEPTH=3` and `MAX_NODES=250`.
- **Lot impact** (`backend/app/lot_impact/`; owner decision of 1 Nov 2026 to keep it trial-only, `router.py:1-2`):
  - A non-trial case gives 404. A closed case gives 409.
  - An exclusion reason needs at least 10 (max 500). Only lots in the list can be left out.
  - Saving writes a new scope version, unless nothing changed. It is audited as `INVESTIGATION_SCOPE_CHANGED`, and an OPEN case moves to IN_PROGRESS.
  - **Signing:**
    - The fingerprint must match before and after re-authentication (409 `LIST_CHANGED`, `:344-345, 367-368`).
    - Then `verify_signing_credentials`.
    - Writes a `SignedApprovalRecord` (object_type "LotImpactList", decision_type `LOT_IMPACT_LIST`), the audit event `LOT_IMPACT_SIGNED`, and a frozen snapshot in a new scope version. The commit goes through `signing_commit` (`:379-435`).
  - **Who may do what.** Signers need `decision.sign`, `quality.review` or `regulatory.approve` (`DECIDERS`, `:52`). Saving needs `investigation.work`. Export needs `data.export` and is audited as `LOT_IMPACT_EXPORTED`.
  - A later mismatch shows "Changed since signed". The list is never re-signed silently (`:146-151`).
  - "MDARIX lists; it recommends no field action and submits nothing" (`:15-16`).

**Endpoints (`backend.app.product360.twin_router`, all 4):**
| Method | Path | Function |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/v1/field-scope` | `field_scope` |
| GET | `/api/v1/field-scope.csv` | `field_scope_csv` |
| GET | `/api/v1/products/{product_ref}/change-impact` | `change_impact` |
