### 3.16 Evidence: library, case inventory and review, evidence requests

**What the user sees and does**
1. In the Evidence step, opens *The evidence records*. Each item shows its review status, its relationship and the counts.
2. Links library evidence to the case.
3. Reviews an item: status, relationship, and a reason or limitation.
4. Opens an item's provenance.
5. *Request evidence* asks a colleague, a role or a supplier.
   - The recipient acknowledges, then responds (with attachments) or says they cannot provide it.
   - The requester, or an evidence reviewer, reviews the answer. Accepting turns it into case evidence; rejecting needs a reason.
   - Cancel is possible while the request is waiting.

**States and rules (`evidence_workflow/rules.py`)**
- **Review statuses** (`:16-24`): UNREVIEWED, ACCEPTED, ACCEPTED_WITH_LIMITATION, REJECTED, QUARANTINED, SUPERSEDED.
- **Relationships** (`:25-32`): SUPPORTING, CONTRADICTING, CONTEXTUAL, UNRESOLVED, UNRELATED, UNREVIEWED.
- **Origins:** LINKED, DIRECT, REQUEST.
- **Review rules** (`review_problem`, `:81-91`):
  - A review must choose a status and a relationship other than UNREVIEWED.
  - REJECTED, QUARANTINED and SUPERSEDED need a reason of at least 10.
  - ACCEPTED_WITH_LIMITATION needs a limitation.
- **Never unlinked.** Linking is idempotent and **nothing is ever unlinked**; instead an item is marked SUPERSEDED or UNRELATED (`:110-111`).
- **Direct evidence.** Reviewing evidence recorded directly on the case creates the link first. Evidence not in the case gives 404 `EVIDENCE_NOT_IN_CASE` (`inventory.py:402-416`).
- **History and locking.** Every link and review appends an `evidence_review_events` row with `actor_role`, and writes `EVIDENCE_LINKED` or `EVIDENCE_REVIEWED` with old and new values. Writes lock the case row.
- **After commit:**
  - `after_late_evidence` asks for an impact assessment on a decided case (3.24); it never changes the signed decision.
  - `on_evidence_reviewed` closes the request review task.
- **Provenance** reads optional tables inside a SAVEPOINT, so a missing table never fails the view (`inventory.py:494-501`).
- **Evidence requests** (`requests.py`; `rules.py:164-257`):
  - Kinds: INTERNAL and SUPPLIER.
  - Internal flow: OPEN -> ACKNOWLEDGED -> RECEIVED -> REVIEWED, or CANNOT_PROVIDE, or CANCELLED.
  - Supplier flow: SAVED (trial: kept, never e-mailed), PENDING_DELIVERY, SENT, REPLIED, REVIEWED, CANCELLED.
  - Who may do what:

| Action | Who | When |
|---|---|---|
| Acknowledge | Internal recipient | While OPEN |
| Respond or cannot-provide | Internal recipient | OPEN or ACKNOWLEDGED |
| Cancel | Requester or `investigation.assign` | While waiting |
| Review | Requester or `evidence.review` | When answered |
| Attach | Requester or internal recipient | Until finished |

  - A recipient is the named user or anyone holding the named role.
  - Supplier requests are answered by e-mail; recipient actions on them give 409 `SUPPLIER_REQUEST`.
  - Validation:
    - title of at least 3 characters;
    - 1-20 items of at most 240 characters each;
    - a due date in the future;
    - priority LOW, MEDIUM, HIGH or CRITICAL;
    - a platform-admin recipient gives 422 `RECIPIENT_NOT_ELIGIBLE`; a role without `evidence.request` gives 422 `ROLE_NOT_ELIGIBLE`;
    - cancel, cannot-provide and reject reasons need at least 10.
  - **Accepting** creates an `evidence` row (source_system "MDARIX evidence request"), an `investigation_evidence` link, a LINKED event stamped 1 microsecond before the REVIEWED event, and sets `resolved_evidence_id` (`requests.py:1089-1193`). A request with no case cannot be accepted (409 `REQUEST_WITHOUT_CASE`).
  - **Unknown link.** `resolve_unknown` (`:309-356`) links a request to a recorded unknown. For an unknown from the latest AI set, it matches by exact statement text, otherwise it stores a snapshot.
  - **Supplier creation** delegates to `stage3_workflow_router.request_supplier_evidence`.
  - **Attachments:**
    - Upload refusals: wrong type 415, too large 413, tenant quota 413, malware 422, content mismatch 415.
    - Download is allowed for the requester, the recipient and `investigation.work`.
  - **Tasks** (`evidence_workflow/hooks.py`): EVIDENCE_RESPONSE, EVIDENCE_REQUEST (waiting) and EVIDENCE_REVIEW.
- **Library** (`backend/app/evidence/router.py`, legacy Evidence Intelligence over root package `evidence/`):
  - Uses `EvidenceIntelligenceService`. The anonymous default tenant is refused with 401.
  - The list has no ordering and caps at 200.

**Endpoints (`backend.app.evidence.router`, all 8):**

### 3.17 Possible causes: hypotheses, observations, unknowns, failure chains, contradictions, history (`backend/app/case_intelligence/`)

**What the user sees and does**
- **AI proposals waiting for a person,** then the hypothesis list. Each hypothesis carries `kind_label`: "Added by X", "Judged by X" or "AI interpretation" (`rules.py:93-105`).
- **Hypotheses.** A person can:
  - add a hypothesis;
  - set a human status with a reason;
  - link case evidence as supporting, contradicting, contextual or unresolved, with a rationale;
  - withdraw a link.
- **Observations.** Record an observation on evidence: a fact, a source assertion or a derived observation.
- **Unknowns.** Record unknowns and move them through their states.
- **Failure chains.** Build a failure chain, adopt an AI chain, and set link states.
- **Contradictions and history.** Read contradictions, and the full history of changes.

**Rules: AI is never a fact**
- **Two status fields.** "AI output stays as the AI produced it. A person's judgement is recorded next to it" (`rules.py:4-6`).
  - Origin codes are "ai", "system" and "human" (`:32-34`).
  - The AI's status is stored lowercase in `status` (`:52-64`).
  - The person's judgement goes in `human_status`: PROPOSED, HYPOTHESIZED, PARTIALLY_SUPPORTED, STRONGLY_SUPPORTED, WEAKLY_SUPPORTED, CONTRADICTED, RETIRED (`:42-50`).
- **Setting a human status** changes only `human_status`, `status_reason` and `status_changed_by`/`at`, and sets `reviewer_disposition = HUMAN_JUDGED` (`router.py:573-578`).
- **AI re-runs.** A re-run supersedes only hypotheses that are AI-origin, unjudged (`reviewer_disposition IS NULL AND human_status IS NULL`) and whose statement disappeared, together with their open AI unknowns (`hypothesis_engine/service.py:226-253`).
- **Superseded hypotheses are frozen** (409 `HYPOTHESIS_SUPERSEDED`, `router.py:449-450`).
- **People cannot record `AI_INTERPRETATION` observations:** "An AI interpretation is only recorded by the AI" (`rules.py:594-600`). Human observations are stored as HUMAN_OBSERVATION / HUMAN / HUMAN_RECORDED ("not shown as VALIDATED"). AI observations stay "AI proposal, not reviewed" until reviewed via `POST /api/v1/ai-outputs/{id}/review` (AI review, another section).
- **No CONFIRMED link state.** Failure-chain links have SUPPORTED, PARTIALLY_SUPPORTED, HYPOTHESIZED, UNKNOWN and CONTRADICTED only (`rules.py:221-244`).
  - SUPPORTED and PARTIALLY_SUPPORTED must name evidence (422 `EVIDENCE_REQUIRED`, `:248-253`; `router.py:1715-1716`).
  - AI statuses map through `AI_LINK_STATES`: OBSERVED_SOURCE_SUPPORTED -> SUPPORTED, DETERMINISTICALLY_DERIVED -> PARTIALLY_SUPPORTED, everything else -> UNKNOWN.

**Other rules**
- **Hypotheses:**
  - statement of at least 10;
  - duplicate statement gives 409 `HYPOTHESIS_EXISTS`;
  - status reason of at least 10;
  - an unchanged status gives 409.
- **Evidence links:**
  - rationale of at least 10;
  - the evidence must be case evidence;
  - an active duplicate gives 409 `EVIDENCE_ALREADY_LINKED`;
  - **retract is never a delete:** it sets `retracted_at`/`retracted_by` with a reason of at least 10 (`router.py:721-726`).
- **Observations:** statement of at least 5. The list is limited to 500.
- **Unknowns** (`rules.py:149-183`):
  - States: open, in_progress, resolved, accepted_limitation, superseded.
  - Nothing leaves superseded. Only open or in_progress may become superseded.
  - A reason of at least 10 is always required. For resolved, accepted_limitation and superseded it is kept as `resolution`.
  - Description of at least 10.
- **Failure chains** (`rules.py:21-27, 516-577`):
  - name 3-200 characters;
  - 2-20 nodes;
  - 1-40 links;
  - no self-links; each pair only once;
  - a state change needs a reason of at least 10.
- **Adopting an AI chain** (`router.py:1594-1652`):
  - The chain must come from an `ai_executions` row of this case produced by the failure-chain engine.
  - Errors: 404 `AI_CHAIN_NOT_FOUND`, 409 `CHAIN_ALREADY_ADOPTED`, 422 `AI_CHAIN_EMPTY`.
  - "The AI output itself is not changed."
- **Contradictions** (read-only, `:820-946`) combine:
  - contradicting hypothesis links that are not withdrawn;
  - evidence reviewed as CONTRADICTING;
  - the latest AI challenges labelled "AI interpretation".
- **History.** Every write goes through `_record` (`:193`), which writes an `intelligence_events` row and an `INTELLIGENCE_<EVENT>` audit event. The history list pages 50 at a time, max 200.

**Endpoints (`backend.app.case_intelligence.router`, all 16):**

| POST | `/api/v1/investigations/{investigation_id}/intelligence/failure-chains` | `create_failure_chain` |

### 3.18 Case challenge: rule-drafted causes and questions, no AI (`backend/app/case_challenge/`)

**What the user sees and does.** Opens the fold *Challenge: questions drawn from the records*.
- **Drafts H-0 to H-5.** Each draft lists facts for, against and missing, citing records. Each is labelled "Drafted by rule H-n v1 from your records. Not a finding." (`rules.py:34-38`).
- **Questions C-1 to C-9.**
- The user can adopt a draft (ticking facts), dismiss a draft with a reason, or answer a question (or mark it not applicable with a reason).

**Rules**
- **Drafts are deterministic**, in rule order, never ranked or scored. `RULE_VERSION=1` (`rules.py:1-34, 566-571`).
- **H-0 cannot be adopted.** H-0 is "Not enough records": fewer than `MIN_COMPLAINTS=3` complaints, or no version or lot on any complaint. Adopting it gives 409 `DRAFT_NOT_ADOPTABLE`.
- **Draft errors:** a stale key gives 409 `DRAFT_NOT_CURRENT`; adopting twice gives 409 `DRAFT_ALREADY_ADOPTED`; dismissing an adopted draft gives 409.
- **Storage.** Drafts are stored only on adopt or dismiss, as `intelligence_events` rows (object_type CHALLENGE_DRAFT, id `uuid5(case.id, key)`).
- **Adopt** creates a Hypothesis with origin "human", human_status PROPOSED and the `draft_rule` tags. Each ticked fact becomes an Evidence row (evidence_type "record_summary", reliability "unreviewed", fact_type "derived_fact") linked as support or contradict (`router.py:232-283`).
- **Reasons and answers.** A dismiss reason needs at least 10. Answers are 10-4000 characters (`questions.py:24-26`).
- **Answers are stored in the brief draft** (narrative `challenge_answers`), and the brief `content_hash` is recomputed so the signature covers them (`router.py:365-379`).
- **Blocking questions.** Material questions (C-1, C-2, C-3, C-4 above 20%, C-5) block *Submit for review* of the brief (`questions.py:1-5, 261`).
- **Structured fields only.** Facts read structured fields only, never free text as instructions (`facts.py`).
- **Audit and permissions.** The first view per scope version is audited as `CHALLENGE_VIEWED`. Writes need `investigation.work` and refuse a closed case. The switch `MDARIX_CHALLENGE=off` makes the routes answer 404.

**Endpoints (`backend.app.case_challenge.router`, all 4):**
| Method | Path | Function |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/v1/investigations/{investigation_id}/challenge` | `read_challenge` |
| POST | `/api/v1/investigations/{investigation_id}/challenge/drafts/adopt` | `adopt_draft` |
| POST | `/api/v1/investigations/{investigation_id}/challenge/drafts/dismiss` | `dismiss_draft` |
| POST | `/api/v1/investigations/{investigation_id}/challenge/questions/answer` | `answer_question` |

**Tables:** `intelligence_events`, `hypotheses`, `evidence`, `hypothesis_evidence`, `decision_brief_versions`, `audit_events`. Migration: `tr26challengeloop`.

**Frontend:** `workspace/challenge.ts:57`, `sections/caseIntelligence/Challenge.tsx`.

**Tests:**
- `tests/test_b5_challenge_api.py`, `test_b5_challenge_performance.py`, `test_b5_brief.py`; frontend `challenge.test.tsx`, `briefSources.test.tsx`.
- e2e `frontend/e2e/challenge.spec.ts`. Its header says it was written without being run, so its status is not verified.

### 3.19 RCA tools: fishbone, 5 Whys, Pareto, event versions, no AI (`backend/app/rca/`)

**Rules**
- **Cause categories.** There are seven (`rules.py:18-27`). Software shows only for SaMD, or when it is already used.
- **Cause statements** need at least 10 characters and must not contain the causal-claim phrases of the hypothesis validator: "A possible cause is not yet a finding" (`:71-106`).
- **Category changes** need a reason of at least 10.
- **5 Whys** is a failure chain with method FIVE_WHYS and 2-7 whys.
  - Each why's state follows the evidence rule for SUPPORTED.
  - "The last why is never a root cause" (`:6-7, 188-191`).
- **Ruled-out causes** (CONTRADICTED, RETIRED) stay visible with their reason.
- **A confirmed cause exists only through a signed decision** (`root_cause_problem`, `:194-217`, called from `human_decision/router.py:613`).
  - Accepting outcomes need a cause category or NOT_DETERMINED.
  - NOT_DETERMINED needs a reason of at least 10.
- **Pareto** shows counts only, never rates. "No confirmed cause yet" is always the last bar (`pareto.py:1-7`). An unknown grouping gives 422 `GROUP_UNKNOWN`.
- **Errors:** `FAILURE_MODE_NOT_FOUND`, `HYPOTHESIS_EXISTS`, `CAUSE_UNCHANGED`, `WHY_ALREADY_LINKED`.
- **Switches.** `MDARIX_RCA_METHODS` and `MDARIX_RCA_INTELLIGENCE` make the routes answer 404 when off.

**Endpoints (`backend.app.rca.router`, all 8):**
| Method | Path | Function |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/api/v1/investigations/{investigation_id}/rca/causes` | `add_cause` |
| POST | `/api/v1/investigations/{investigation_id}/rca/causes/{hypothesis_id}` | `set_cause` |
| GET | `/api/v1/investigations/{investigation_id}/rca/event-versions` | `event_versions` |
| GET | `/api/v1/investigations/{investigation_id}/rca/fishbone` | `fishbone` |
| GET | `/api/v1/investigations/{investigation_id}/rca/five-whys` | `list_five_whys` |

### 3.20 Case AI run and the legacy AI investigation endpoints (root packages)

**Case AI run** (`backend/app/case_ai/`)
- **One pass, on request only.** The steps are ANALYSIS, HYPOTHESES, CHALLENGES, UNKNOWNS, FAILURE_CHAINS and BRIEF (`runner.py:29-36`). FAILURE_CHAINS is parked under mvp (`:40-43`).
- **Run states:** QUEUED, RUNNING, then done or FAILED. A run is stale after 30 minutes.
- **Never automatic.** `trigger=AUTO` returns `ON_REQUEST_ONLY` (owner decision AI-D6, `router.py:95-97`).
- **AI off.** With AI off, the response is `state: AI_OFF` and nothing starts (`:111-120`).
- **One at a time.** An active run gives 409 `AI_RUN_IN_PROGRESS`.
- **Labelling.** Each step records `author` "AI" or "RULES" and a `review_state`. Steps reuse earlier results.
- **Audit and execution.** Audited as `CASE_AI_RUN_REQUESTED`. The run executes as a FastAPI background task.
- **Table:** `case_ai_runs` (migration `md42caseairun`). Results go to `ai_executions` and the engine tables.
- **Dead code.** The `trigger == "AUTO"` branches at `router.py:99-100` and `108-110` are dead code.

**Legacy AI endpoints** (registered in `backend/app/main.py:515-532`; services in root packages)

| Module | Root package / service | Status in 0.11.07 |
|---|---|---|
| `investigations.router` | `investigation_workspace/`, `investigator/`, `hypothesis_engine/` | Called by `features/investigator/InvestigationWorkspacePanel.tsx` (last fold of Possible causes): workspace `:292`; analysis, hypotheses `:345-474`. `savedResults.ts:72-83` reads latest. **No caller:** GET analysis/{id}, GET hypotheses/{id}. |
| `investigations.brief_router` | `briefs/` (`BriefService`, `model_writer.py` drops uncited, causal, certainty and regulatory wording) | POST used by the panel; GET latest by `savedResults.ts`. **No caller:** list and by-id. This AI brief is what a Decision Brief may start from (3.22). |
| `challenger_router` | `challenger/` | POST and GET used by the panel and `savedResults.ts`. With AI off, only a named saved hypothesis set is challenged. |
| `unknowns_failure_chain_router` | `unknowns/` ("mdarix-controlled-unknowns-radar"), `failure_chain/` (fixed template) | Both used. Legacy GET unknowns is also read by `EvidenceRequests.tsx:329` and `NextSteps.tsx:222`. |
| `counterfactual.router` | `counterfactual/` (stored in `scenarios` with `assumptions.kind = COUNTERFACTUAL`, `counterfactual/router.py:61-68`) | POST used by the panel in the full feature set only. **404 under `MDARIX_FEATURE_SET=mvp`.** |
| `scenario_intelligence.router` | `scenario_intelligence/` (table `scenarios`) | **No frontend caller. 404 under mvp.** |
| `retrieval.router` | `retrieval/` (tables `evidence_chunk_embeddings`, `retrieval_queries`; the query is rate-limited "search:user") | **No frontend caller.** |
| `trust_router` | `trust_engine/` (tables `assurance_results`, `assurance_checks`; automatic checks on every saved AI result in `trust_engine/auto.py`) | `GET /api/v1/ai/trust-policy` is used by `admin/areas/AiControlCenter.tsx:309`. **The assurance GET and POST have no frontend caller.** |

- **Storage.** All AI outputs are stored in `ai_executions`.
- **Graph.** The root `graph/` package serves the `/api/v1/graph/*` endpoints in `main.py` (3.15).
- **Decision context.** The root `decision_center/` serves the legacy `decision_router` (3.23).

### 3.21 Risk and regulatory case reviews, and next steps (`backend/app/case_reviews/`)

**What the user sees and does**
1. Sees the review policy: is a risk or regulatory review required now, and why.
2. Requests a review, or uses the next steps REQUEST_RISK_REVIEW or REQUEST_REGULATORY_REVIEW.
3. A reviewer holding `risk.review` or `regulatory.review` starts the review.
4. The reviewer completes it with an outcome, a rationale and the assessed items (risk reviews: findings per risk), or recuses.
5. The requester or an assigner can cancel.
6. Reviewers have a dashboard.

**Rules**
- **Statuses** (`db/models/reviews.py:50`): REQUESTED, IN_PROGRESS, COMPLETED, CANCELLED, SUPERSEDED.
  - start: REQUESTED -> IN_PROGRESS;
  - complete: IN_PROGRESS -> COMPLETED;
  - recuse: IN_PROGRESS -> REQUESTED;
  - cancel: open -> CANCELLED;
  - superseded when the scope is saved with `reviews_must_restart` (`service.py:282-349`).
  - Only one open review per type (409 `REVIEW_ALREADY_OPEN`).
  - COMPLETED reviews "cannot change afterwards".
- **Outcomes.** `RISK_OUTCOMES` and `REGULATORY_OUTCOMES` (`reviews.py:51-65`). The blocking outcomes are ADDITIONAL_EVIDENCE_REQUIRED, ESCALATE_SAFETY_CONCERN and ESCALATE_REGULATORY. They give the case owner a FOLLOW_UP task and block readiness until a new review completes (`rules.py:65-67, 203-204`).
- **Policy** (`review_policy`, `rules.py:146-187`):
  - Risk review is required for HIGH or CRITICAL priority, a serious injury or death in scope, or a linked signal with serious harm.
  - Regulatory review is required for CRITICAL priority, or a death, serious injury or malfunction in scope.
  - "MDARIX says when a review is required, never what its outcome is, and never decides reportability" (`:5-6`).
- **No segregation of duties, by design.** "Reviews have no segregation of duties" (`rules.py:269-271`; `router.py:320-321`). Only the reviewer completes or recuses (403 `REVIEW_NOT_YOURS`). Only the requester or an `investigation.assign` holder cancels. 409 `REVIEW_TASK_TAKEN` when another reviewer holds the task.
- **No password re-check** on completion.
- **Rationale and findings.**
  - Rationale 20-5000 characters. Recuse, cancel and next steps need a reason of at least 10.
  - Each assessed risk needs YES, NO or NOT_ASSESSABLE plus a reason of at least 10.
  - A "NO" or a new hazard needs an owner (an organization member) and a due date that is not in the past (UTC date, `router.py:431`).
  - Risks must belong to the case's product.
- **Due dates** are optional and must be in the future (UTC). Naive datetimes are taken as UTC. `DUE_SOON_DAYS=7`.
- **Next steps** (`next_steps.py`; codes at `rules.py:459-521`; availability at `:636-656`):

| Next step | What it does |
|---|---|
| CONTINUE_INVESTIGATION, NO_DECISION_YET | Record a note |
| REQUEST_*_REVIEW | Create a review |
| REQUEST_ADDITIONAL_EVIDENCE, REQUEST_ADDITIONAL_TESTING | Create an evidence request (also needs `evidence.request`; testing titles get the "Testing: " prefix) |
| REVIEW_SUPPLIER_CHANGE, REVIEW_PRODUCT_CHANGE | Create a FOLLOW_UP task |
| ESCALATE_FOR_QUALITY_REVIEW | 409 `USE_DECISION_BRIEF`; nothing written |

  Each step is audited as `NEXT_STEP_RECORDED`.

| GET | `/api/v1/case-reviews/summary` | `review_summary` |
| GET | `/api/v1/case-reviews/{review_id}` | `get_review` |
| POST | `/api/v1/case-reviews/{review_id}/cancel` | `cancel_review` |
| POST | `/api/v1/case-reviews/{review_id}/complete` | `complete_review` |

### 3.22 The Decision Brief and its quality review (`backend/app/decision_brief/`)

**What the user sees and does**
1. Composes v1 of the brief, optionally starting from the AI draft.
2. Edits the narrative: problem statement, proposed conclusion, recommended actions and notes. Can refresh the content from the records.
3. Submits. The gate shows blocking items and warnings.
4. A quality reviewer starts the review, then accepts, returns (for evidence, analysis, risk review or regulatory review), rejects the proposed conclusion, or escalates.
5. After a return, the author composes a new version.
6. Quality reviewers have a dashboard and a list.

**States and transitions** (`rules.py:25-116`)
- **Versions:** DRAFT, READY_FOR_REVIEW, UNDER_REVIEW, RETURNED, APPROVED_FOR_DECISION, SUPERSEDED.
- **Review actions:** START_REVIEW, ACCEPT_FOR_DECISION, RETURN_FOR_EVIDENCE, RETURN_FOR_ANALYSIS, RETURN_FOR_RISK_REVIEW, RETURN_FOR_REGULATORY_REVIEW, REJECT_PROPOSED_CONCLUSION, ESCALATE.
- **Version moves:**
  - START_REVIEW only from READY_FOR_REVIEW.
  - ACCEPT gives APPROVED_FOR_DECISION.
  - Any return or reject gives RETURNED.
  - ESCALATE stays UNDER_REVIEW and creates a QUALITY_REVIEW task for role LEADER.
- **Case moves:** submit gives BRIEF_IN_REVIEW; accept gives READY_FOR_DECISION; a return gives IN_PROGRESS.

**Rules**
- **Create** (`router.py:454-526`):
  - 409 `BRIEF_ALREADY_OPEN` while a version is in progress; 409 `CASE_CLOSED`.
  - A new version copies the previous narrative and composes fresh content.
  - The problem statement defaults to the case question. **The proposed conclusion is always the author's own.**
- **Edit.** Only DRAFT is editable (409 `BRIEF_NOT_EDITABLE`, `router.py:129-139`). Every editor is added to `contributors`.
- **Submission gate** (`evaluate_gate`, `rules.py:318-336`). Blocking items:
  - any BLOCKED check;
  - a PENDING check in `GATE_REQUIRED_CHECKS` {`scope_complete`, `evidence_reviewed`, `no_blocking_request`, `risk_review`, `regulatory_review`};
  - an empty problem statement or proposed conclusion;
  - `content.unavailable` (a composition section failed);
  - unanswered material challenge questions (3.18).
  Other PENDING checks are warnings only.
- **Submit** (`router.py:599-744`):
  - Refused when the case is DECIDED or closed.
  - Content is composed one last time; a failed gate gives 409 `BRIEF_NOT_READY`.
  - An earlier APPROVED_FOR_DECISION version becomes SUPERSEDED, and its DECISION_APPROVAL tasks are superseded.
  - A QUALITY_REVIEW task is created with `details.brief_author_refs`.
- **Review** (`router.py:924-1049`):
  - A READY_FOR_REVIEW version is auto-started.
  - ACCEPT creates a DECISION_APPROVAL task. A return creates a FOLLOW_UP task for the author.
  - RETURN_FOR_RISK_REVIEW and RETURN_FOR_REGULATORY_REVIEW also request a case review after the commit. A failure there never undoes the return; it answers `requested:false`.
- **Segregation of duties** (`rules.py:132-138`, `router.py:750-756`). `sod_denied`: the creator, the submitter and every contributor are refused with 403 `SOD_DENIED`: "You wrote or submitted this version of the brief; someone else reviews it". This applies to start-review and review.
- **Comments.** Every action except ACCEPT needs a comment of 10-5000 characters. Narrative fields are at most 10000. At most 20 actions of up to 1000 each.
- **AI draft** (`router.py:372-446`; `rules.py:147, 208-228, 396-425`):
  - Usable only if a person accepted or edited the AI brief (409 `AI_DRAFT_NOT_REVIEWED`; 409 `AI_DRAFT_NOT_AVAILABLE`).
  - Labelled with `AI_DRAFT_LABEL`, and flagged `out_of_date` when the scope or evidence changed since.
  - Turning `ai_draft_used` off drops the AI text.
  - **The leading hypothesis comes only from a person's STRONGLY_SUPPORTED or PARTIALLY_SUPPORTED judgement, never from the AI status.**
- **Hashes:**
  - `content_hash` = SHA-256 of canonical {content, narrative} (`rules.py:369-380`).
  - `evidence_set_hash` is computed over sorted `id|status|relationship` lines (`:383-390`).
  - `hash_verified` recomputes the hash on read (`router.py:288`).
- **Immutability.** After submit, only status and review fields change. Versions are never deleted. Writes lock the case row, then the version row. Unique version per case (`uq_decision_brief_versions_version`).

**Endpoints (`backend.app.decision_brief.router`, all 10):**

| GET | `/api/v1/decision-briefs` | `list_decision_briefs` |
| GET | `/api/v1/decision-briefs/summary` | `decision_brief_summary` |
| GET | `/api/v1/investigations/{investigation_id}/decision-brief` | `get_decision_brief` |

### 3.23 The signed human decision (`backend/app/human_decision/`) and the legacy decision centre

**What the user sees and does**
1. The decision maker opens the queue or dashboard, then the decision screen: approved brief, reviewers, earlier decisions, outcome options.
2. Chooses an outcome and writes the rationale (and conditions, when used).
3. Picks follow-ups, ticks the root-cause hypotheses, sets the attribution and cause category, and acknowledges the residual unknowns.
4. Signs with password and authenticator code. Alternatively, *Return* sends the brief back without signing.
5. Can view the signed record with its verification, and the decision-and-impact summary.

**Outcomes and states** (`rules.py:27-129`)
- **Outcomes:** ACCEPT_CONCLUSION, ACCEPT_WITH_CONDITIONS, REJECT_CONCLUSION, CLOSE_NO_FURTHER_ACTION. Signature codes: ACCEPT, ACCEPT_COND, REJECT, CLOSE_NFA.
- **Decision status:** SIGNED or SUPERSEDED.
- **Case after signing:**
  - A concluding outcome moves the case to DECIDED.
  - REJECT moves it to IN_PROGRESS. The brief becomes RETURNED and the author gets a FOLLOW_UP task (`router.py:871-903`).
- **Allowed case states:** READY_FOR_DECISION. When superseding, also DECIDED or IN_PROGRESS.
- **Superseding.** A new decision must name the current one (`SUPERSEDE_REQUIRED`). Naming one when none exists gives `SUPERSEDE_NOT_ALLOWED`. **A signed decision is never edited; a correction is a new superseding decision** (`rules.py:7-9`; `router.py:812-816`).

**Sign order** (`sign_decision`, `router.py:582-1014`). Each refusal is audited and committed.
1. Not an administrator.
2. Entitled.
3. Idempotent retry by `request_hash`. The hash excludes password and code (`rules.py:221-229`). A retry re-verifies the credentials and returns the earlier decision.
4. The brief version belongs to the case.
5. `brief_problem` (`rules.py:317-330`). The version must be APPROVED_FOR_DECISION. The hash the client sent must equal the stored hash (`STALE_BRIEF`). The recomputed hash must equal the stored hash (`BRIEF_TAMPERED`).
6. Case state.
7. Supersede rule.
8. Root-cause hypotheses valid; attribution and cause category through `complaint_disposition.rules.attribution_problem` and `rca.rules.root_cause_problem` (`router.py:603-621`).
9. Residual unknowns acknowledged (422 `RESIDUAL_UNKNOWNS_NOT_ACKNOWLEDGED`).
10. `approval_check`, the approval authority in `governance_router` (internals not verified).
11. **Segregation of duties** (`rules.py:361-366`; `router.py:707-708`): brief authors are refused with `SOD_DENIED`, "You wrote or submitted this brief; another authorized person decides on it". If the tasks cannot be read, the request fails (`service.py:98-115`).
12. `verify_signing_credentials` (`router.py:711-719`).

**Payload checks** (`rules.py:247-290`)
- Rationale 20-5000 characters.
- Conditions 10-5000, only with ACCEPT_WITH_CONDITIONS.
- Follow-ups only with accepting outcomes, at most 10.
- Supersede reason 10-2000.
- Return comment 10-5000, prefixed "Returned by the decision maker: ".

**What signing records**
- **Meaning text:** "I, <name>, <role>, have reviewed Decision Brief <id> version <n> and decide: <outcome>" (`rules.py:464-479`).
- **Signed material** (`:492-518`): decision id, version (the brief `content_hash`), type CASE_DECISION, outcome, remarks, brief version id, `evidence_set_hash`, `snapshot_hash`, conditions, follow-up types.
- **Context snapshot** (`router.py:729-756`). It freezes the brief, readiness, reviews, signer, outcome, root cause and the AI results (`ai_governance.lock_for_signing`, `:753`, not verified). It is stored in `context_snapshots`.
- **Records written:** a Decision row, a `SignedApprovalRecord`, and the audit event `CASE_DECISION_SIGNED` with `mfa_verified`. The prior decision becomes SUPERSEDED (`CASE_DECISION_SUPERSEDED`). The commit goes through `signing_commit.commit(..., "decision")`.
- **Follow-up defaults** (`router.py:483-528`):
  - Assignee: the named colleague (a platform admin gives 422 `FOLLOW_UP_ASSIGNEE_INVALID`); else the named role; else the case owner; else `FOLLOW_UP_ROLES`.
  - `due_at` defaults to now + 30 days (UTC).

**Return** (`router.py:1054-1163`)
- Checks: administrator, APPROVED_FOR_DECISION, not the current decision's brief (409 `BRIEF_ALREADY_DECIDED`), case state, authority, and segregation of duties.
- **No password is required.**
- The case goes from READY_FOR_DECISION to IN_PROGRESS; the author gets a FOLLOW_UP task; audited as `CASE_DECISION_RETURNED`.

**Verification and summary**
- **Verification** (`rules.py:554-668`; `service.verify_signature`, `:506`) checks fingerprint, signature, brief hash, snapshot, authority and separation. The result is VALID, INVALID or NOT_SIGNED.
- **Summary** (`summary.py:158-177` `worked()`). A planned effectiveness check reads TOO_EARLY; no check reads NOT_PLANNED. "MDARIX never says an action worked: only a signed effectiveness check does."
- **Queue and dashboard.** The queue holds approved briefs with the case in READY_FOR_DECISION, DECIDED or IN_PROGRESS, critical first. The dashboard uses `DUE_SOON_DAYS=3` and `RECENT_DAYS=30`.

**Legacy decision centre** (`backend/app/decision_router.py`; root package `decision_center/`)
- **Create.** `selected_action` must be in `OPTIONS` (`decision_center/service.py:20-30`). `authorized_by` is forced to the session user. The status is REQUIRES_REVIEW and approvers are notified.
- **Review.** The reviewer is forced to the session user. Self-review is refused (`SELF_REVIEW_DENIED`). A decision that is APPROVED, REJECTED or of kind BRIEF is locked (409 `DECISION_LOCKED`, `:316-326`).
- **Advisory.** The decision advisory needs `ai.run` and adds an AI notice.
- **Effect on cases.** A REQUIRES_REVIEW legacy decision blocks case close and cancel (3.12).
- **Frontend.** `features/investigator/DecisionCenterView.tsx:83-174` signs through `/api/v1/governance/decisions/{id}/sign`, which is outside this section (not verified).

### 3.24 After the decision: follow-ups, CAPA, impact assessments, closure (`backend/app/decision_followups/`)

**What the user sees and does**
1. Follow-ups chosen at signing appear in *After decision* and in My Work.
2. The responsible person starts a follow-up and completes it with a note. For field-action and product-change types, they also fill the structured fields.
3. For a CAPA_REQUIRED follow-up, they create the CAPA and later record its QMS reference.
4. When late evidence arrives on a decided case, an impact assessment opens. It is concluded as NO_IMPACT or MATERIAL_IMPACT.
5. The closure check shows the criteria that still block closing the case.

**Rules**
- **Follow-up statuses** (`rules.py:36-43`): OPEN, IN_PROGRESS, DONE, CANCELLED. Follow-ups are created only by a decision signature.
- **Responsible** (`:193-202`): the named assignee; else anyone holding the assigned role; else anyone with `investigation.work`.
- **Start.** Responsible only (403 `FOLLOW_UP_NOT_YOURS`), from OPEN only.
- **Complete:**
  - Note of 10-5000 characters.
  - The field-action and product-change fields are validated against the type and the case's product. With the closed loop off they give 409 `CLOSED_LOOP_OFF`.
  - Completing the My Work task also marks the follow-up DONE (`on_follow_up_task_completed`, `followups.py:638`).
- **Cancel.** Needs `investigation.assign` and a reason of 10-1000.
- **Overdue** means open and `due_at < now` (UTC).
- **Follow-ups outlive the case.** On case close, their cancelled tasks are renewed (`on_case_closed`, `:685`).
- **Create CAPA** (`followups.py:503`; `investigation.work`):
  - CAPA_REQUIRED follow-ups only, once only, not CANCELLED.
  - Locks the case row so that `CAPA-<case>-<n>` is unique.
  - Creates a `capas` row: action_type CORRECTIVE, source INVESTIGATION_DECISION, source_system MDARIX, status "open".
  - Title 5-255 characters.
- **QMS reference.** Pattern `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9 ._/#:-]*$`, max 120 characters. A blank value clears it.
- **Impact assessments** (`impact.py`):
  - Late evidence (linked, reviewed, accepted from a request, or ingested) on a DECIDED or CLOSED case with a current signed decision opens one OPEN assessment per (decision, evidence), with an IMPACT_ASSESSMENT task. Audited as `LATE_EVIDENCE_RECEIVED`.
  - An hourly sweep handles up to 500 per tenant per run.
  - Uniqueness is enforced in code. No database unique index was found (not fully verified).
  - **Complete:** NO_IMPACT or MATERIAL_IMPACT, rationale 20-5000 characters. MATERIAL_IMPACT on the current decision audits `DECISION_UNDER_RECONSIDERATION` and moves a DECIDED or CLOSED case back to IN_PROGRESS, reopening its tasks and signals. **The decision row is never changed.**
- **Closure check** (`closure.py:178`; criteria `rules.py:360-435`). Applies when the case has a brief or a brief decision. Criteria:
  - CASE_OPEN;
  - DECISIONS_REVIEWED;
  - SIGNED_DECISION (closing outcome);
  - IMPACT_ASSESSMENTS (none open);
  - CASE_REVIEWS (none requested or in progress);
  - EVIDENCE_REQUESTS (no open HIGH or CRITICAL request);
  - FOLLOW_UPS (each chosen type has a record and a task; they may still be open);
  - CLOSURE_REASON (at least 10).
  The case PATCH close enforces these through `refuse_unmet_closure` (409 `CLOSURE_CRITERIA_NOT_MET`).

| GET | `/api/v1/follow-ups/{follow_up_id}` | `get_follow_up` |

### 3.25 CAPA effectiveness (`backend/app/capa_effectiveness/`)

**What the user sees and does**
1. The board lists every CAPA's check: attention, scheduled, done or all.
2. The detail shows the plan, the rounds and the recurrence measured so far.
3. The user plans the check (criteria, planned date, measure-from date), or re-plans it with a reason.
4. When due, a person signs the result with password and code: EFFECTIVE, NOT_EFFECTIVE or INCONCLUSIVE, with a summary of what they found.

**Rules**
- **Check status** (`rules.py:15-21`): PLANNED, EFFECTIVE, NOT_EFFECTIVE, INCONCLUSIVE. INCONCLUSIVE was added by migration `tr33inconclusive` (owner decision 17 Nov 2026).
- **Board states** (`state_of`, `:55-67`): OVERDUE, DUE_SOON (14 days or less), NOT_PLANNED, SCHEDULED. The most urgent sort first.
- **Rounds.** After NOT_EFFECTIVE or INCONCLUSIVE, the next plan creates round+1. After EFFECTIVE, planning gives 409 `CHECK_DONE`. The database requires a unique `(tenant_id, capa_id, round)`.
- **Plan** (`plan_problem`, `:70-80`):
  - criteria 10-2000 characters;
  - planned date at most 3 years ahead;
  - measure-from date within the last 10 years and on or before the planned date.
  - **Re-plan:** a reason of at least 10 (`REASON_REQUIRED`); an unchanged plan gives `NOTHING_TO_CHANGE`. `plan_version` is incremented and the reminder is replaced.
  - A cancelled or rejected CAPA gives 409 `CAPA_NOT_ACTIVE`. **Closed CAPAs still need a check.**
- **Sign** (`router.py:263-401`):
  - Requires `quality.review` or `decision.sign`. Administrators are refused.
  - `plan_version` must match what the user read. It is checked twice, the second time under lock (409 `PLAN_CHANGED`).
  - Then `verify_signing_credentials`.
  - **Signed material:** CAPA, check, round, criteria, dates, result, summary, and the recurrence measured at signing (`service.measure`, `:250`). For the trial sample, a labelled synthetic follow-up is included.
  - version = round. The audit event `CAPA_EFFECTIVENESS_SIGNED` includes `signed_before_planned_day`.
  - Summary 10-4000 characters. A concurrent signature gives `SIGNED_MEANWHILE`.
- **Recurrence** (`rules.py:87-114`):
  - Observed counts and a rate per 30 days, with direction FEWER, MORE, SAME or NONE.
  - "Not a statistical test". Reads `too_early` under 14 days. Baseline 30-365 days.
  - `default_criteria` is only a suggestion the person edits.
- **Reminder.** An EFFECTIVENESS_CHECK task when the check is within 14 days or past; priority HIGH when past. Due = `calendar.day_end(planned_on)`, i.e. 23:59:59 in the organization's zone, stored in UTC (`router.py:219-239`). The daily sweep is `sweep_tenant` (`:423`).
- **Clock.** `today_utc` is the UTC date.
- **No segregation of duties beyond the administrator ban.** The planner, CAPA owner or case author may sign the result.

**Endpoints (`backend.app.capa_effectiveness.router`, all 4):**
| Method | Path | Function |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/v1/capas/effectiveness` | `effectiveness_board` |
| GET | `/api/v1/capas/{capa_id}/effectiveness` | `effectiveness_detail` |
| PUT | `/api/v1/capas/{capa_id}/effectiveness/plan` | `plan_check` |
| POST | `/api/v1/capas/{capa_id}/effectiveness/result` | `sign_result` |

**Tables:** `capa_effectiveness_checks`, `capas` (`effectiveness_check_at` is a date from the source system), `signed_approval_records`, `workflow_tasks`, `audit_events`. Migrations: `md41reportingcapa`, `tr33inconclusive`.

### 3.26 Closed loop: "Did it work?" and re-open triggers (`backend/app/closed_loop/`)

**What the user sees.** On a decided case (Decision & action step), *Did it work?* compares complaint counts before and after each action for the scope of the signed brief. It is labelled "not a verdict. Only your signed effectiveness check says whether an action worked."

**Rules**
- **Computed on read and never stored** (`service.py:1-11`). Switch: `MDARIX_CLOSED_LOOP=off`. Readable by anyone who can open the case. Per-lot rows are for trials only (`LOTS_TRIAL_ONLY`). Uses `now_utc().date()`.
- **Windows.** Equal before and after windows of 90-365 days. Settling days: CAPA 0, FIELD_ACTION 30, PRODUCT_CHANGE 0.
- **Test.** Exact binomial test, `P_LIMIT 0.05`, Benjamini-Hochberg adjustment.
- **Denominator.** Shipped units, else released units, else UNKNOWN.
- **Result words:** TOO_EARLY, TOO_FEW, FEWER, MORE, NO_CHANGE, COUNTS_ONLY (`rules.py:1-56`).
- **Triggers** (`triggers.py:1-107`):
  - CAPA_NOT_EFFECTIVE;
  - RECURRENCE_AFTER_EFFECTIVE (at least 2 later complaints);
  - LATER_COMPLAINTS_MORE;
  - PROBLEM_ON_NEW_VERSION (at least 2);
  - COMPLAINT_ON_ACTIONED_LOT;
  - LATER_SERIOUS_HARM.
  - **Each trigger only opens an impact assessment** (3.24). Nothing is reopened or decided automatically.
  - Triggers are idempotent per decision, trigger and group (audit event `REOPEN_TRIGGER_FIRED`). They run in the hourly late-evidence sweep.

**Endpoints (`backend.app.closed_loop.router`, all 1):**
| Method | Path | Function |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/v1/investigations/{investigation_id}/did-it-work` | `read_did_it_work` |

**Tables:** read only: `decisions`, `decision_brief_versions`, `investigation_scope_versions`, `complaints`, `capas`, `capa_effectiveness_checks`, `lot_batches`, `lot_distributions`. Exact list not verified. Writes: `impact_assessments` and `audit_events` through the triggers. Migration: `tr26challengeloop`.

**Frontend:** `workspace/didItWork.ts:81`, `sections/DidItWork.tsx`.

**Tests:** `tests/test_b5_loop_api.py`, `test_b5_loop_rules.py`, `test_b5_triggers.py`; frontend `didItWork.test.tsx`; e2e `frontend/e2e/did-it-work.spec.ts`.

**Limit:** "reads Did it work? for every decided case each hour; fine for trials and pilots, batch it past ~1,000" (`triggers.py:177`).

### 3.27 Case audit: timeline, replay, integrity, export (`backend/app/case_audit/`)

**What the user sees.** The Decision & action step has a fold "The record". It shows:
- the case timeline, filterable by category;
- the AI runs;
- the integrity results;
- *replay*: the case as it was known at a time or at a decision;
- a JSON or CSV export.

**Rules**
- **Read-only.** "Nothing is changed except the export's audit event" (`router.py:13-14`).
- **Timeline.** Categories at `rules.py:16-32`; origins HUMAN, AI, SYSTEM. Items within 5 seconds are deduplicated. Page size 50, max 200.
- **Hidden detail.** Detail keys that match secrets are never shown: pass, secret, token, mfa, otp, cookie, session, credential, api key, authorization, fingerprint, signature_hash, source_ip, user_agent, prompt (`rules.py:48-52`).
- **Accounts by name.** Accounts are shown by name, never by id.
- **Replay** uses `partition_known` and `value_as_of`. The limits are listed in `REPLAY_LIMITATIONS` (`:507`). A decision outside the case gives 404 `DECISION_NOT_FOUND`.
- **Integrity** uses `legacy_signature_checks` (`:670`) and `combine_chains` (`:711`).
- **Export** needs `data.export`. It is audited as `CASE_AUDIT_EXPORTED` with its SHA-256; `EXPORT_VERSION` is "case-audit-1".

### 3.28 Readiness home and inspection pack (`backend/app/readiness_home/`)

**What the user sees and does.** The readiness home shows four home numbers, further checks, and whether detection is pending. The user can:
- read the summary for a period;
- download the summary PDF (management review input);
- download the inspection pack ZIP.

**Rules** (`service.py:4-121`)
- **Home numbers:** COMPLAINTS_TO_RECHECK, COMPLAINTS_PAST_TARGET, REPORTING_CLOCKS, TRENDS_WITHOUT_ACTION.
- **Further checks:** HARM_CLOSED_WITHOUT_CASE, CAPA_EFFECTIVENESS, TRIAGE_OVERDUE, DISPOSITIONS_DUE, RECURRING_SERVICE_LEVEL, EXTERNAL_REQUESTS, RISK_FILE_INPUT.
- **Each check** has a WHY text and a regulatory BASIS. States: CLEAR, ATTENTION, CRITICAL, NOT_SET.
- **Rules only.** No AI, and nothing is changed. `LOOKBACK_DAYS=365`.
- **IST.** "Today" is the organization's calendar day (`organization_today`, quoted in 3.0). Lookback windows still start at UTC midnight (`:120-121`). TRIAGE_OVERDUE compares with UTC now (`:397-405`).
- **Summary period.** Default 365 days, range 30-1095 (`summary.py:32`). The PDF carries the watermark "MANAGEMENT REVIEW INPUT - VERIFY AGAINST SOURCE RECORDS" and a UTC generation stamp.
- **Pack** (`pack.py:1-34`, `MDARIX_INSPECTION_PACK_V1`): README, summary PDF, a checks CSV, one CSV per check, and `manifest.json` with a SHA-256 per file. The pack and the screen use the same functions so that they agree.
- **Audit.** Both exports need `data.export`. They are audited as `READINESS_SUMMARY_EXPORTED` and `READINESS_INSPECTION_PACK_EXPORTED`.

### 3.29 Signed periodic reviews: audit trail, access, configuration (`backend/app/signed_reviews/`)

These are not case steps. They are the organization's signed periodic reviews of its own records, run beside the flow.

**Rules**
- **Switch and access.** `MDARIX_B6_REVIEWS` off makes every route answer 404 `REVIEWS_OFF`. Platform admins never read.
  - The audit trail is for `quality.review` holders or customer admins.
  - Access and configuration are for customer admins or Leaders (`router.py:34-67`).
- **Signing** (`signing.sign`, `signing.py:68-161`):
  - `verify_signing_credentials` runs first.
  - The material (kind, review id, content, findings, corrects) is passed through `safe_details`, which removes secret-named keys.
  - The fingerprint goes into a `SignedApprovalRecord` plus **one audit event holding the material**. There is no dedicated table.
  - A concurrent signature gives 409 `SIGNED_MEANWHILE`.
  - Nothing is edited after signing; a correction is a new review with `corrects`.
  - `read()` recomputes the fingerprint to show "intact". The PDF shows UTC times.
- **Findings.** 1-4000 characters and not blank ("or 'none'").
- **Audit trail** (`audit_review.py`):
  - **Period.** Defaults to the day after the last signed review, else today minus 30 days, up to today. It must satisfy start <= end <= UTC today. Day bounds are UTC midnight.
  - **Flags:** EDIT_WITHOUT_REASON, EDIT_AFTER_SIGNATURE, ADMIN_CHANGE, BULK_CHANGE (more than 50 edits in 10 minutes), OUTSIDE_CALENDAR (in the calendar zone), FAILED_SIGN_IN_SPIKE (10 or more per UTC day), plus a chain check. Beyond `READ_CAP=20000` events the review says "incomplete".
  - **Frequency.** 1-12 months, Leader only, with a reason.
  - **Signer.** Must hold `quality.review` and not be an administrator.
  - **Second reviewer.** If the signer's own actions are flagged, a second reviewer must confirm (`SECOND_REVIEWER_REQUIRED`). The confirmation is signed as version "2".
- **Access review** (`access_review.py`):
  - Due every 90 days. Marks are KEEP, CHANGE or REMOVE.
  - Nobody marks their own row. A Leader marks only the sole admin's row.
  - The customer admin signs once every row is marked and their own row was marked by someone else.
- **Configuration review:** a snapshot without keys or secrets. Compare caps at `EVENTS_CAP=5000`.

**Endpoints (`backend.app.signed_reviews.router`, all 14):**

| GET | `/api/v1/reviews/access` | `access` |
| POST | `/api/v1/reviews/access/marks` | `mark_access` |
| POST | `/api/v1/reviews/access/sign` | `sign_access` |
| GET | `/api/v1/reviews/audit-trail` | `audit_trail` |
| GET | `/api/v1/reviews/audit-trail/events` | `audit_trail_events` |
| PUT | `/api/v1/reviews/audit-trail/frequency` | `set_audit_trail_frequency` |
| POST | `/api/v1/reviews/audit-trail/sign` | `sign_audit_trail` |
| POST | `/api/v1/reviews/audit-trail/{review_id}/confirm` | `confirm_audit_trail` |
| GET | `/api/v1/reviews/configuration` | `configuration_now` |
| GET | `/api/v1/reviews/configuration/compare` | `compare_configuration` |

**Tests:** `tests/test_b6a_signed_reviews.py`.

### 3.30 Feeds and reference data that the flow reads

- **`backend/app/master_data/catalog.py`** (no router of its own) is a read model over the `md_master*` mapping tables (`external_application`, `source_object_definition`, `canonical_field_definition`, `master_mapping_*`).
  - It is served by `backend.app.mapping_catalog_router` (`/api/v1/admin/mapping`, 22 endpoints, admin data configuration). That router is owned by the administration/integration section.
  - Frontend: `admin/areas/DataConfiguration.tsx`, `Integrations.tsx`.
  - Test: `tests/test_master_data.py`.
- **`ingestion/`** (root): `ingestion/normalization/service.py:658` upserts complaints by raw SQL `ON CONFLICT (tenant_id, complaint_identifier)`.
  - The trial's main complaint import is `backend/app/trial/imports.py`, which is outside this section. It calls `intake.record_changes` and `confirm_by_import` (`:2648-2651`), then the signal hooks (3.11).
  - Tests: `tests/test_day4_ingestion.py`, `test_simulator_ingestion.py`.
- **`source_simulator/`** (root; `persistent_app.py`) simulates source-system APIs for demos and tests (`tests/test_persistent_source_simulator.py`). It is not part of the production flow.

### 3.31 Invariants Gemini must not break (consolidated)

1. **Tenant and actor come only from the request context.** Never take a tenant, owner, reviewer or signer from the body. Writes always go to the data plane; signatures go to both planes through `signing_commit`.
2. **Signatures:**
   - Re-authenticate (password, plus code when MFA is required) **before** staging anything in the control session.
   - Never accept recovery codes.
   - Keep the shared 5/15 throttle.
   - Keep `request_hash` free of password and code.
   - Never change the signed-material key sets (`human_decision/rules.py:492-518`, `capa_effectiveness/rules.py:136-160`, lot impact, signed reviews). Changing them invalidates the verification of existing signatures.
   - A PENDING signature is never valid.
3. **Administrators never sign.**
4. **Segregation of duties.** Keep every check in the 3.0 table, especially:
   - brief authors never review or decide (`sod_denied`, `is_author`, `brief_author_refs` kept in task details);
   - `_own_decision` on claim;
   - the second person on case QA review, void, threat exclusion, downgrade and mail dismissal.
5. **Hashes.** Keep the brief `content_hash` and `canonical_json` formula unchanged. Signing refuses `STALE_BRIEF` and `BRIEF_TAMPERED`. Challenge answers must recompute the hash.
6. **Nothing is edited after signing or approval.** Brief versions are frozen after submit. Decisions, dispositions, Case checks, triage threat exclusions and signed reviews are superseded, never edited. Scope versions, evidence links, review events and intelligence events are append-only. Evidence is never unlinked. Hypothesis links are retracted, not deleted.
7. **AI.**
   - AI output is never written into human fields. `status` (AI) and `human_status` (person) stay separate.
   - People cannot record `AI_INTERPRETATION`.
   - No CONFIRMED or root-cause state exists before a signed decision.
   - AI never starts by itself and does nothing while AI is off.
   - AI drafts stay labelled and need a person's review before use.
   - Validators drop uncited, causal and decision wording.
8. **"Worked" belongs to people.** No screen or endpoint may say an action worked except a signed effectiveness check. Closed-loop triggers only open impact assessments.
9. **State machines.**
   - Case (intake) state only through `set_case_status`.
   - Investigation state only through `states.set_state`; reserved states only from the brief and decision flows.
   - Signal dispositions only through `check_disposition`.
   - Task moves through the workflow router.
10. **Hooks never block and never undo the main commit.** Keep side effects after the commit inside a SAVEPOINT.
11. **Clocks.**
    - Store UTC.
    - Keep organization-calendar due dates where they are (routine triage, follow-up, QMS id, CAPA reminder, communications).
    - Keep the IST-correct `organization_today` for readiness.
    - Do not change the India day-1 counting in `reportability/deadlines.py`.
12. **Trial-only gates.** Keep the trial-only gates (Twin, field scope, lot impact, supplier lots, per-lot did-it-work) unless the owner decides otherwise.
13. **Routing order.** Keep the router mount order in `main.py:555` (case export before intake). Keep the six cockpit step names and the old-address mapping.
14. **Personal data.** Contact data stays masked in exports unless a reason is given (never in a trial), is audited by field name only, and is erased by retention after 365 days for closed Cases.

### 3.32 Known limits and gaps (to raise with the owner, not to "fix" silently)

**Segregation-of-duties gaps found in the code** (confirm with the owner whether they are intended):
- The quality reviewer who approved a brief may also sign the decision. `reviewed_by` is not in `brief_author_refs`.
- CAPA effectiveness signing has no check against the planner, the CAPA owner or the case author.
- Lot impact signing has no author/signer separation.
- Evidence review and evidence-request review have none: the requester may review the answer.
- `_own_decision` is enforced on claim only, not on reassign or accept.
- Case reviews have none, by design.

**Signatures**
- The decision-maker *Return* asks for no password.
- Case-review completion, second-look decisions, complaint reopen and CAPA creation and QMS reference need no signature.

**Immutability**
- Brief versions and decisions are protected in application code only. Database triggers protect `business_id` only.

**Clocks**
- Intake uses a UTC fallback zone when the zone is blank.
- The Cases "overdue" view uses UTC midnight.
- Signal, case and follow-up due dates are UTC plus N calendar days, not working days.
- PATCH priority does not move `due_at`.
- The INVESTIGATION task has no due date.

**Caps and scale**
- Evidence picker: 200.
- Brief dashboard blocked scan: 50.
- Impact sweep: 500 per tenant per hour.
- Audit-trail read: 20000.
- Configuration compare: 5000.
- Twin: 2000 lots.
- Backlog read: 20000.
- Closed-loop triggers read every decided case each hour.
- The mail-in rate limit is per process.

**Legacy and unused routes**
- These routes have no frontend caller: `/api/v1/evidence/{id}/*` (7), retrieval (2), assurance GET and POST, scenarios, AI analysis/hypothesis/brief by id, the brief list, counterfactual by id, and `/api/v1/graph/*` (6).
- These intake routes also have no caller: contact set and erase, and triage downgrade decision (may be reached from My Work, not verified).
- Dead code in `case_ai/router.py:99-100, 108-110`.
- The hard-coded `TENANT_KEY` sits in `graph/service.py` and `product360/service.py`.

**"Expected vs observed"** exists in the trend check and Home, not in the Lifecycle Twin.

**Shortcuts in scope** (`shortcut:` comments):
- `intake/rules.py:53`, `intake/triage_rules.py:29`, `intake/service.py:93, 123, 696, 2059`, `intake/cases_list.py:91`;
- `mail_in/receiver.py:144, 155`;
- `signals/insight.py:250`, `signals/trend_rules.py:142`;
- `closed_loop/triggers.py:177`.

No TODO or FIXME markers were found in scope.

**Not verified**
- `governance_router.approval_check` internals.
- `ai_governance.lock_for_signing`.
- The frontend PATCH caller.
- Frontend date display zone.
- The depth of e2e coverage in `golden-path`, `flagship-journey` and `persona-journeys`.
- Whether external clients use the uncalled routes.
- The exact per-query table lists for Product 360, the twin and closed loop.
- Test-to-capability mapping that relies on file names only.

## 4. Regulatory reporting, rules and governance

Source read: worktree `trial-132`, branch `feat/app-wide-layout` (0.11.07 candidate), 19 Nov 2026. Every path below is
relative to the repository root. Endpoint lists are copied from Appendix A (generated from the running FastAPI app);
tables from Appendix B; migrations from Appendix C. "Not verified" marks anything not confirmed by reading code.

### 4.0 Ground rules that apply to every subsection

These rules are stated in module docstrings and enforced in code. Breaking any of them is a regulatory defect, not a
style issue.

1. **MDARIX never submits anything to an authority.** No route sends a report anywhere. A person submits through the
   authority's own channel (FDA eSubmitter/ESG, EU national authority, MHRA MORE, Health Canada e-mail, MvPI e-mail or
   ADRMS) and records the submission in MDARIX. Constant text: `authority_reports/rules.py:NEVER_SUBMITS`,
   `frontend/src/features/workspace/sections/ReportsTab.tsx:NEVER_SUBMITS`.
2. **MDARIX never decides reportability.** Rules point to a *suggested* conclusion; a person confirms or overrides it
   (override needs a reason) and signs. "Unknown is never No": an Unknown that could make an event reportable gives
   `PENDING` ("pending information") and blocks a "not reportable" signature (`reportability/markets.py` docstring,
   `rule_register/engine.py` Kleene logic).
3. **A person signs every answer.** Every conclusion, report approval, guide approval, document, review and readiness
   statement is a `signed_approval_records` row made after re-authentication (password, plus authenticator code when
   `MDARIX_REQUIRE_MFA_FOR_SIGNATURES` is true, which is the default; `backend/app/mfa.py:45`).
4. **Administrators never sign or approve.** `ADMINISTRATOR_ROLES = {PLATFORM_ADMIN, ADMIN, ADMINISTRATOR,
   CUSTOMER_ADMIN}` is checked in `reportability/assessment_router.py:53`, `authority_reports/rules.py`,
   `decision_guides/rules.py:56`, `qms_documents/router.py:41`, `regulatory_router.py:357`. Note that
   `PLATFORM_ADMIN` holds every permission (`access/permissions.py`), so the role check, not the permission check, is
   what stops it.
5. **Segregation of duties (second person).** A report snapshot is approved by someone who is not a preparer, not the
   signer of the market conclusion behind it, not an administrator (`authority_reports/rules.py:approver_problem`).
   A guide version is approved by someone who did not edit it (`decision_guides/rules.py:344`). An IMDRF mapping is
   approved by someone other than the proposer (`regulatory_reference.py:512`). A regulatory copilot document is
   approved by someone who authored no version (`regulatory_router.py:350`, `tests/test_regulatory_controls.py`).
   Exception: in a **free trial only**, a person working alone may approve their own *practice* report or guide
   version ("same person", T-B8), recorded in the signature remarks (`authority_reports/router.py:standing`,
   `workflow/approvals.py`).
6. **Signed content is never edited.** A change is a new version (market assessments, guide versions, snapshots,
   QMS documents, signed reviews with `corrects`). Snapshots, submission attempts and evidence are never edited or
   deleted through the API (`authority_reports/router.py` docstring).
7. **Starter text.** Every question set, floor, counting rule, form mapping and template is marked "Starter, for your
   consultant to confirm". In the rule register every one of the 61 rule versions has `review.status = "STARTER"`
   (read from `backend/app/rule_register/data/*.json`). No regulatory consultant confirmation is recorded in code.
   `qms_documents/templates.py:19 CONFIRMED_CLAUSES` is empty, so every clause prints "pending approval".
8. **Tenancy.** Tenant and actor come only from `get_request_context` (`ctx.tenant_id`, `ctx.user_id`). Business
   records (assessments, reports, guides, oversight, documents) are on the **data plane** (`get_data_plane_db`);
   signatures (`signed_approval_records`) and the audit trail (`audit_events`) are on the **control plane**
   (`get_db`). Signing across the two planes goes through `backend/app/signing_commit.py` (4.16).
9. **Patient and reporter data.** Never stored in report entries. Typed only at "Send for approval", only when the
   organization's Customer Admin switched `report_personal_data` on (migration `tr30personaldata`), kept only inside
   that report's frozen snapshot, never echoed in errors, audit or logs (`authority_reports/router.py:_personal`).
   Every export/CSV in this area carries IDs, dates, owners and states, never patient, reporter or description text.
10. **Practice records.** In a free trial every signed record carries `PRACTICE_LABEL` ("Practice record: made in a
    free trial with sample or de-identified data. Not your regulatory record.", `reportability/markets.py:36`).

**Router mount order is load-bearing** (`backend/app/main.py`): `stage3_router` (line 534, owns
`GET /api/v1/reports/catalog`) is mounted before `authority_reports_router` (line 560, owns
`GET /api/v1/reports/{obligation_id}`); `attached_reports_router` (559) before `authority_reports_router`;
`other_countries_router` (574, `.../market-assessments/OTHER-{country}`) before `market_assessment_router` (575,
`.../market-assessments/{market}`); `case_export_router` (555) before the intake `/{id}` routes. Reordering silently
breaks these paths.

**Feature switches in this area** (`backend/app/feature_set.py`, read from the environment on every call):

| Switch | Default | Off means |
|---|---|---|
| `MDARIX_RULE_REGISTER` | on | floors come from frozen `reportability/legacy_v1.py`; `/api/v1/rule-register` 404; no floor recorded on a conclusion |
| `MDARIX_B9_INDIA` | on | India floor v2 and India chain off; `/reports/{id}/india-chain` 404; official MvPI form not offered |
| `MDARIX_B9_CANADA` | on | Canada floor v2 and Canada chain off; `/reports/{id}/canada-chain` 404 |
| `MDARIX_B9_US_IMPORTER` | on | importer floor `US-IMP-floor-1` not picked |
| `MDARIX_B9_US_USER_FACILITY` | **off** | user facility floor `US-UF-floor-1` not picked (on only when set to `on`) |
| `MDARIX_B8_EU_MIR`, `MDARIX_B8_GB_MIR` | on | MIR XML/PDF and MIR report chains off |
| `MDARIX_B9B_OTHER` | on | every Other-countries route 404 (records stay readable) |
| `MDARIX_B6_DEADLINE_LIST`, `_REVIEWS`, `_CHANGE_REASON`, `_TRACE`, `_VALUE_CENTRE`, `_LATE_ENTRY`, `_SIMILAR_WORDING` | on | the B6a part's routes 404 / screens hide |
| `MDARIX_ROUTINE_PATH` | on | Cases export, QMS-entry export, alerts-and-files setting 404 |
| `MDARIX_FEATURE_SET=mvp` | `full` | parks `/api/v1/regulatory/*` (copilot, eSTAR) and `/api/v1/risk/*` (404) |

A rule-register version that names a switch is in force only while the switch is on; signed records keep the
version they name (`rule_register/register.py:offered`).

**Permissions** (`backend/app/access/permissions.py`): `regulatory.review` and `regulatory.approve` (held by
`REGULATORY_REVIEWER`; `APPROVER` holds approve only; `LEADER` and `PLATFORM_ADMIN` hold all), `data.export`
(downloads), `quality.review` (oversight settings, QMS documents), `ai.run` (AI code suggestions, copilot).
`CUSTOMER_ADMIN` holds only `records.write`, `ai.run`, `data.export`: never regulatory approval, never a signature.

---

### 4.1 Reportability watch, reporting windows and legal clocks

**(1) What the user sees and does**
1. Complaints › Reportability watch lists every complaint that reports a death, a serious injury, a malfunction or a
   patient with unknown outcome, most urgent first, with days left in each reporting window. States
   (`reportability/rules.py:59-63`): `OVERDUE`, `DUE_SOON`, `IN_REVIEW`, `RUNNING`, `NO_WINDOW`, `FOLLOW_UP` (answers
   missing, nothing starts a clock), `DECIDED`.
2. Settings › Reporting windows: a regulatory approver enters the organization's windows (from its own procedure),
   optionally starting from FDA/EU templates (`rules.py:TEMPLATES`, each carrying its `basis` from
   `backend/app/standards.py`). Saved as a new version with a reason.
3. Settings: the organization's role per market (Manufacturer, Importer, User facility in the US) and the
   personal-data-at-report-step switch (Customer Admin only, with reason).
4. When a reporting step needs a fact the record lacks (incident country, awareness date), the step asks for it
   (`reportability/facts.py`); until given, clocks are *provisional* and a market conclusion cannot be signed.

**(2) Business rules**
- Windows are the organization's, never MDARIX's: nothing is set until someone enters them.
- The clock starts at awareness = earliest of awareness, received and recorded times
  (`service.py:AWARE_AT`). One computation for every screen: `reportability/deadlines.py`.
- Counting (`deadlines.py`): authority time zones US `America/New_York`, EU/NI `Europe/Brussels`, GB
  `Europe/London`, India `Asia/Kolkata`; Canada and other countries count in the organization's zone. US and EU count
  from the day after awareness; **GB and India count the awareness day as day 1** (`DAY_ONE_MARKETS`). Latest day =
  earlier of the authority-zone and organization-zone count; ends at the earlier end of day. Work days are Mon-Fri;
  public holidays unknown (count as work days, conservative). No weekend extension. All labels are written in the
  organization's zone. Marked `STARTER`.
- The legacy single decision route is closed: `POST /complaints/{id}/reportability` always returns 409
  `SIGNED_ASSESSMENT_REQUIRED` (`router.py:89`). Old `complaint_reportability` rows are still read.
- UK whole ("United Kingdom") must be split: `COUNTRY_UK_PART_NEEDED` (Great Britain vs Northern Ireland).
- Reminders (`reportability/reminders.py`): scheduler job `reporting_reminders` (every 6 h) creates
  `REPORTABILITY_DECISION` tasks for due-soon/overdue complaints without a decision, at most 50 new per run, and
  completes tasks no longer needed. Idempotent; tasks are not tied to a case.
- Changing windows needs `regulatory.approve`; reading is open to every signed-in member.

**(3) Backend**: `backend/app/reportability/router.py` (watch, settings, roles, personal data), `rules.py` (states,
templates, `clocks`, `follow_ups`), `deadlines.py` (`count`, `end_of`, `state`, `view`, `due_label`), `facts.py`
(`needed`), `service.py` (`watch`, `one`, `clocks_for`, `settings_of`, `awareness`, `reporting_roles`,
`personal_data_on`, `triage_shortest`), `reminders.py` (`sweep_reportability`, `run_for_all_tenants`).

**(4) Endpoints** — `backend.app.reportability.router` (6):
- GET `/api/v1/complaints/reportability`
- GET `/api/v1/complaints/reportability-settings`
- PUT `/api/v1/complaints/reportability-settings` (regulatory.approve; new version, audited `REPORTABILITY_SETTINGS_UPDATED`)
- PUT `/api/v1/complaints/reportability-roles` (regulatory.approve; audited `REPORTING_ROLE_SET`)
- PUT `/api/v1/complaints/reportability-personal-data` (Customer Admin, or a trial's owner; audited `REPORT_PERSONAL_DATA_SET`)
- POST `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/reportability` (closed: always 409)

Also PUT `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/reporting-facts` (in `assessment_router`, 4.2).

**(5) Tables**: `reportability_settings` (windows JSONB, lookback_days, reporting_roles, report_personal_data,
version), `complaint_reportability` (legacy decisions, read only), `workflow_tasks` (reminder tasks). Models
`backend/app/db/models/reportability.py`. Migrations `md41reportingcapa`, `tr28marketroles`, `tr30personaldata`.

**(6) Frontend**: `features/workspace/sections/ReportabilityWatch.tsx`, `ReportingWindows.tsx`, `ReportingFacts.tsx`;
`features/workspace/reportability.ts`, `whyAsked.ts`.

**(7) Tests**: `tests/test_reporting_capa_api.py`, `test_reporting_capa_rules.py`, `test_legal_deadlines.py`,
`test_one_deadline_api.py`, `test_b4_window_floor.py`, `test_b9_roles.py`, `test_b8_personal_data.py`,
`test_b8_personal_data_unit.py`; frontend `reportingCapa.test.ts(x)`, `whyAsked.test.tsx`.

**(8) Invariants**: one deadline function (`deadlines.count`) for every screen; GB/IN day-one rule; never shorten
nothing to "No"; legacy decision route stays closed; windows are versioned and audited.

**(9) Known limits**: no public-holiday calendar for authorities; counting rules are starter text.

---

### 4.2 Per-market assessment and the signed conclusion

**(1) What the user sees and does** (complaint page › market assessment, or Decision trees › a market's tree)
1. For each required market (the market of the incident country when enabled, plus every market already assessed;
   `assessments.required_markets`), the person sees the market's questions, each with definition and clause, and the
   fixed harm-severity scale (`scale-starter-1`, five ISO 14971-style levels with IMDRF Annex F terms).
2. Answers Yes / No / Unknown / Not applicable, each with a rationale; the system shows suggested answers from record
   facts (`markets.suggestions`) and marks each answer's origin.
3. Save draft (PUT); the suggestion (Reportable / Pending information / Not reportable) and report type appear.
4. Sign: confirm or override (override reason >= 10 chars), password + authenticator code. If the person lacks the
   signer-training record on the current question set and scale, 409 `TRAINING_REQUIRED` with the trained colleagues'
   names (4.9).
5. A signed Reportable conclusion **creates the market's report** in the same transaction; a later signed Not
   reportable **cancels** a report not yet submitted (`authority_reports/router.py:follow_conclusion`).
6. For a reportable conclusion filed outside MDARIX without a worksheet, "Record the filing" (reference, day).
7. Canada: for an incident outside Canada, enter the corrective-action decision day (the clock runs from it).

**(2) Business rules**
- Markets: `US`, `EU_NI` (EU and Northern Ireland), `GB`, `IN`, `CA` (`markets.MARKETS`). `OTHER` is deliberately
  outside this tuple (4.3).
- Per-market legal periods (from the floors in force, `rule_register/data`):

| Market / floor | Report types and days | Zone, day 1 |
|---|---|---|
| US manufacturer `US-floor-1` | 30 calendar days (21 CFR 803.50); 5 work days (803.53) | New York, day after awareness |
| US importer `US-IMP-floor-1` (switch on) | 30 calendar days (803.40); no 5-day report | New York |
| US user facility `US-UF-floor-1` (switch **off**) | 10 work days (803.30); no malfunction | New York |
| EU/NI `EU-floor-2` | 2 days serious public health threat (Art. 87(4)); 10 days death/unanticipated serious deterioration (87(5)); 15 days serious incident (87(3)) | Brussels, day after |
| GB `GB-floor-2` | 2 / 10 / 15 days (UK MDR 2002 as amended) | London, **awareness day = day 1** |
| India `IN-floor-2` (switch on) | 15 days serious adverse event (MDR 2017 Rule 26, starter) | Kolkata, **day 1** |
| Canada `CA-floor-2` (switch on) | 10 days death/serious deterioration; 30 days could lead to it if recurred (SOR/98-282 s.60(1)(a)); foreign incident: end of day after the corrective-action decision (s.60(1)(b), starter) | organization's zone |
| Other country `OTHER-floor-1` | no legal period: the organization's window naming the country, or a due date the person enters | organization's zone |

  US provisional clocks (before signing): threat 5 work days, flagged/unflagged 30 calendar days
  (`US-floor-1.content.provisional`).
- Unknown never counts as No; Not reportable is refused while any Unknown could make it reportable
  (`markets.answers_problem`).
- Harm level at or above the market's "serious" threshold with a "not serious" answer needs a written reason
  (`markets.disagreement`).
- The signed material (`assessment_router.py:675-715`) includes questions with clauses, answers, harm level, scale
  version, suggested vs confirmed conclusion, override/change reason, report type, due date, training records, starter
  mark, practice label and, with the register on, the floor code + content hash, rules applied and date calculation.
  `content_fingerprint` = SHA-256 of canonical JSON (`human_decision/rules.py:fingerprint`); `signature_hash` =
  SHA-256(fingerprint + signer + version).
- Signature meaning (`markets.py:1032`): "I have assessed {ref} for {market} with its questions, {version}, and the
  harm-severity scale, {version}, and conclude: {reportable|not reportable}."
- `signed_approval_records.decision` is `VARCHAR(12)`: codes are `REPORTABLE` / `NOT_REPORT`
  (`assessment_router.py:52`). Do not introduce longer codes.
- Optimistic concurrency: `saved_at` must match; otherwise 409 `DRAFT_CHANGED` / `SAVED_MEANWHILE` /
  `SIGNED_MEANWHILE`. A changed floor since saving gives 409 `FLOOR_CHANGED`; a changed scale `QUESTIONS_CHANGED`.
- Re-signing a market needs a `change_reason` (`CHANGE_REASON_NEEDED`, migration `tr18changereason`); earlier signed
  versions get `superseded_by`.
- "Facts changed after signing: re-check" is shown when the country/awareness changed after signing.
- Saving/signing needs `regulatory.review` or `regulatory.approve`; reading is open to members.

**(3) Backend**: `reportability/assessment_router.py` (`detail`, `save_draft`, `sign_conclusion`,
`set_reporting_facts`, `record_filing`, `record_decision_day`), `markets.py` (question sets, `evaluate`,
`suggestions`, `scale`, `market_clock`, `signature_meaning`), `assessments.py` (versions, `markets_summary`,
`settled`, `decision_days`), `legacy_v1.py` (frozen v1 oracle; **never edit**).

**(4) Endpoints** — `backend.app.reportability.assessment_router` (6):
- GET `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/market-assessments`
- PUT `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/market-assessments/{market}` (save draft)
- POST `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/market-assessments/{market}/sign`
- POST `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/market-assessments/{market}/filing`
- POST `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/market-assessments/CA/corrective-action-day`
- PUT `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/reporting-facts`

**(5) Tables**: `complaint_market_assessments` (one row per version; `conclusion` NULL = draft; `floor_code`,
`rules_applied`, `date_calc`, `country`, `signed_record_id`, `content_fingerprint`, `superseded_by`;
model `db/models/market_assessment.py`), `signed_approval_records` (control plane), `audit_events`. Migrations
`tr01marketassessments`, `tr15canadamarket`, `tr18changereason`, `tr27ruleregister`, `tr28marketroles`,
`tr31othercountries`. Audit actions: `COMPLAINT_MARKET_ASSESSMENT_STARTED/SAVED`, `COMPLAINT_MARKET_CONCLUSION_SIGNED`,
`COMPLAINT_REPORTING_FACTS_SET`, `COMPLAINT_MARKET_FILING_RECORDED`, refusals `SIGNATURE_*`.

**(6) Frontend**: `features/workspace/sections/MarketAssessment.tsx`, `TreeAnswer.tsx`, `TreeSign.tsx`,
`DecisionTrees.tsx`; `features/workspace/marketAssessment.ts`, `treeAnswer.ts`.

**(7) Tests**: `test_market_assessment_api.py`, `test_market_assessment_rules.py`, `test_release_0_10_88_markets.py`,
`test_release_0_10_88_resign.py`, `test_b9_canada_decision_day.py`, `test_b9_canada_foreign.py`,
`test_b9_india_period.py`, `test_b9_india_period_db.py`, `test_b9_signed_role.py`, `test_b9_us_role_floors.py`,
`test_b9_role_pending_clocks.py`, `test_b8_northern_ireland.py`, `test_regulatory_decision_tables.py` (rows of

**(8) Invariants**: signed versions immutable; administrators cannot sign; training before signing; floor + hash in

`OTHER` stays out of `markets.MARKETS`.

**(9) Known limits**: question wording paraphrases the regulations and is starter text; the Annex F coded release is

### 4.3 Other countries (B9b)

**(1) User**: picks a country outside the five markets (country picker lists covered countries and those redirected
to a built-in market with the reason, e.g. EU outermost regions -> "may fall under the EU MDR"), answers the generic
starter questions of `OTHER-floor-1`, signs. A signed Reportable creates an **"Other Regulatory Report"** (form
`ATTACHED`): no form to fill; the person attaches the report they made, records "Submitted" with date and Report ID
(or "Not issued yet", entered later), and the due date when no window names the country.

**(2) Rules**: generic questions are "not this country's law: confirm with your consultant"; the "Submitted" record
is signed by the recorder (password + MFA, file SHA-256 in the signed material; **no second person**, OC-D3); every
file is malware-scanned and kept, never overwritten; follow-ups are child records; Close needs a reason and is refused
while a follow-up is open; Not reportable cancels an unsubmitted report. Every route 404 while `MDARIX_B9B_OTHER` or
the register is off.

**(3) Backend**: `reportability/other_countries.py`, `authority_reports/attached.py`.

**(4) Endpoints** — `backend.app.reportability.other_countries` (3):
- GET `/api/v1/other-countries`
- PUT `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/market-assessments/OTHER-{country}`
- POST `/api/v1/complaints/{complaint_id}/market-assessments/OTHER-{country}/sign`

`backend.app.authority_reports.attached` (4):
- PUT `/api/v1/reports/{obligation_id}/attached-due-date`
- POST `/api/v1/reports/{obligation_id}/attached-follow-ups`
- PUT `/api/v1/reports/{obligation_id}/attached-report-id`
- POST `/api/v1/reports/{obligation_id}/attached-submission`

**(5) Tables**: `complaint_market_assessments` (market `OTHER`, `country`), `report_obligations` (market `OTHER`,
`country`, form `ATTACHED`), `report_submissions`, `report_evidence`. Migration `tr31othercountries`.

**(6) Frontend**: `features/workspace/sections/OtherCountries.tsx`.

**(7) Tests**: `test_b9b_other_countries.py`, `test_b9b_other_countries_schema.py`, `test_b9b_other_floor.py`;
frontend `otherCountries.test.tsx`.

**(8) Invariants**: route mount order (4.0); the five markets' loops and the B4 equivalence check never see `OTHER`.
